# Decisions

A record of the calls Better-Cal makes where two or more readings were each defensible and one had to be codified, so any of them can be revisited later without digging through commits or reviews. Started 2026-11-04; revised the same day after an outside review of the first draft.

Each entry carries a status, because "we chose this" and "the code does this" are different claims:

- **Standard:** the standard says it; we follow it.
- **Deviation:** the standard says otherwise; we depart on purpose, and say what it costs.
- **Choice:** the standard or the situation leaves it open; we picked.
- **Workaround:** what the code does because of a constraint (a library, a storage shape), not because it is the ideal reading; kept until the constraint goes.
- **Defect:** the code does this and shouldn't; its repair is scheduled, which is a release decision, not a reading.
- **Planned:** decided, not yet built.
- **Open:** undecided.

And each says whether it is verified: by which check, or not yet. What doesn't belong here: visual design choices, which keep their own records ([calendar marks](calendar-marks.md), [day marks](day-marks.md)).

Two principles behind most of the entries. A change previews its consequences and offers the reasonable readings as choices. And a preselected reading avoids silent deletion, unexpected detachment and the overwriting of a deliberate exception, while every addition and every changed bound is shown plainly; preserving a count and preserving intent are different things, and no default guarantees the latter.

## Repeating Events and the Standard

- **A clock time the spring change skips, for any spelling of the rule.** Status: deviation. Chosen: the occurrence is kept that day, at the same clock plus the gap (02:30 becomes 03:30), and later days are at their own clock. The standard's 3.3.10 says a generated instance at a nonexistent time "MUST be ignored and MUST NOT be counted"; its 3.3.5 rule for a single date-time (the offset before the gap) is what we apply to every generated instance. Why: a weekly event that vanishes one week a year is worse than one an hour later. Cost: a strict client reading the app's ICS may leave that day out and end a COUNT series an occurrence later. One rule for every spelling: a plain DAILY, a rule naming its hour in BYHOUR, and a rule listing all seven weekdays express no different intention, since another calendar or serializer picks the spelling. The app's own expander (1.5.0) applies the one rule with no state carried between days, for every interval and from any window; until then a BYHOUR rule omitted the day and a rule listing weekdays kept the shifted clock for the rest of the series (the library's readings, [#201](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/201)), and migration 064 moves the keys those readings wrote. Verified 2026-11-04: the expander's hand-listed tests (`server/tests/expander.php`, "Gap:": five spellings, a monthly and a yearly rule, a half-hour gap, two windows and the model reading the same keys), and the migration's database test; the harness's evaluator still reads the library's two gap readings and is updated by its owner. Verified 2026-11-05 from windows far from the start ([#201](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/201)): `server/tests/expander.php`, "Far windows (#201)", hand-listed, a short and a long window, a year and nine years from the start, across the gap day, for daily, weekly and monthly rules with and without BYHOUR; the issue's own reproduction is "a daily weekday list, April 2028, short and long". Where: [series model](series-model.md#zones-dst-and-all-day-days). Chosen 2026-11-02 for plain clocks, extended to every spelling 2026-11-04 on review; built 2026-11-04.
- **A clock time the fall change repeats.** Status: standard, with a choice on top. Chosen: a wall clock given with no instant is its earlier instant, as 3.3.5 says; an instant given stays that instant; the plan's note says which of the two the series is at and what the zone data says of it. Else: the later instant (what PHP reads a repeated wall clock as, and what the library's monthly and yearly steps gave a later year's day before 1.5.0, whose keys migration 064 moves). Verified: the harness's second-fold case and the fold mutation, with the model's unit tests and the expander's ("Fold:", 2026-11-04). Where: series model, same section; the `fold` and `daylight` fields of a note in the [contract](../api-contract.md). Settled 2026-11-02; the note's data 2026-11-04.
- **A start that would fall in the skipped hour.** Status: workaround. The code stores the series one period earlier, on the rule's latest day before it where the clock exists, as a generated skip with COUNT raised by one, and every sentence names the first day shown. Why a workaround: storage holds one instant and the expander takes every day's clock from the start, so a start stored at the shifted clock would move the whole series an hour. The ideal is a start that is the day the person named; that needs storage of the clock apart from the instant. Every count a person sees is the events shown: the API's rule says the shown COUNT and a COUNT sent is the shown one, the writer adding the stored day back (was, until 1.5.1: the editor's "Ends after" showed one more than the events shown, [#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202)). A start typed into the skipped hour reaches the server as the clock typed (`typed` in the contract), so a new series typed there is stored by this same rule (was: the server received the shifted instant and stored a series an hour later). Verified: the harness's anchored-start cases; 2026-11-05, `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Typed clock:" and "Ends after:", and the smoke test "quick add typed clock". Where: series model, same section. Settled 2026-11-02; count and typed clock 2026-11-05.
- **An end typed after a start in the skipped hour.** Status: choice. Chosen: the end is its clock moved by the push the gap gave the start, then read by the one wall-clock rule, so the range moves as one: 02:30 to 03:30 typed on the spring change day is an hour, shown 03:30 to 04:30 that day and 02:30 to 03:30 on every other day of a series, and 02:30 to 05:00 keeps its two and a half hours (03:30 to 06:00). A start that exists has no push, so every other range is its two clocks' own instants, as before: 01:30 to 04:00 across the gap is 01:30 to 04:00, and the fall change's ranges are untouched. An end at or before the start on the clocks typed is refused (400 "end must be after start"), checked on the clocks since the push could carry it past the start; quick add's strip still reads such an end as the next day. Else: each clock read alone, which put 02:30 and 03:30 on one instant and refused the hour ([#222](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/222), 1.3.0 to 1.5.1) and gave a series typed 02:30 to 04:30 there an hour on every other day; or the length as the clocks' difference for every range, which reads 01:00 to 04:00 on the spring day as 01:00 to 05:00 and 00:30 to 02:30 on the fall day as ending at 01:30, against clocks that exist. Why: a series has one length and the person named it by the clocks of an ordinary day, and a single event typed there shows as that series' change-day occurrence would; where both clocks exist they are what the person named. One function (`Wall::typedEnd`) reads it for the create, the edit and quick add's parse, and the client's `typedInstants` mirrors it for the editor's and quick add's checks. An instant the request gives beside the clocks stays that instant where it is one the clock names, the rule above for a repeated clock applied to a typed one (fixed 2026-11-07: a two-hour event dragged to end in the hour the fall change repeats sends the later 02:30 beside the clocks, and the typed end read alone as the earlier one made it an hour; `server/tests/run.php`, "typed onto the fall change", and the harness's fixed case of the seed 31 drag that found it); the client's reading of clocks alone is unchanged. Verified 2026-11-06: `server/tests/run.php`, "typed across the gap" (a northern zone, a southern one, a half-hour offset and a half-hour gap; create, series, edit and parse), and the smoke tests of the same name. Where: series model, Zones, DST and All-Day Days; `typed` in the [contract](../api-contract.md). Settled 2026-11-06.
- **A time dragged on the grid.** Status: choice. Chosen: the time grid is a wall clock, drawn the same on a day whose clocks change, and a drag is measured on it in absolute wall minutes (a day's number times 1440 plus the minute), never in elapsed time: a moved event keeps its length on the clock, a resized end goes to the clock it was dropped at, a drawn range is the clocks it covers, and the result shows where the preview was. The minutes are then read by the typed range's rule above, one function (`wallRangeInstants`, which `typedInstants` calls in the device's zone), with the one addition typing has no need of, which pass of a repeated hour: a resize holds the end not dragged at its exact instant and reads the dragged one beside it (an end after the start, a start before the end); a move reads the start on the side of the instant it left that the drag went to (at or after it going forward, at or before it going back), so a nudge of an event in the second pass stays in it, and an event brought from another day takes the first pass going forward and the second going back; a drawn range, like a typed one, takes the first. A start pushed by the gap onto or past a held end is backwards, and the resize does nothing. Else: elapsed minutes from the pointer, which made an hour-long event dropped at 1:30 on the fall day end at 1:30 again and read a nudge within the second pass as the first ([#190](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/190)); or every repeated clock as the first, which put a resized end before its start ([#185](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/185)); or the earlier instant for every move, which turns a 15-minute nudge of a second-pass event into a 45-minute jump the other way. Why: the grid shows clocks, so a drop must mean what the preview showed, and a drag asks the same question a typed time does. Verified 2026-11-06: the smoke tests "times when dragging (#190)": a move, a resize and a draw into the repeated hour (both passes), the skipped hour and an ordinary day, in a northern zone, a southern one and one on a half-hour offset, and the typed reading equal to the drawn one. Where: `web/src/lib/dates.js` (`wallRangeInstants`), `web/src/ui/monthmath.js` (`movedDates`, `resizedDates`, `drawnDates`). Settled 2026-11-06.
- **HOURLY across a skipped hour.** Status: choice. Chosen: the walk steps by wall clock, so an every-3-hours series reads 00:00, 03:00, 06:00 on the change day and keeps its pattern after; a skipped wall clock resolves by the offset before the gap. Else: elapsed time (00:00, 04:00, 07:00 that day), under which the series' clocks shift by the change until the next change shifts them back. Why: a rule in hours names clock positions a person reads off a day, like a daily rule does. A rule in minutes or seconds is different on purpose: its periods are stepped in elapsed time from the start's own, as the standard's table reads them, so `FREQ=HOURLY;INTERVAL=3` and `FREQ=MINUTELY;INTERVAL=180` are not the same rule here. That distinction is the weakest-argued entry in this record: it rests on how people write the two, not on the standard, and only imports write minute rules. Verified: the harness's evaluator steps the wall clock since c74c57a, with a spring and a fall example in its hand-listed set, and the expander's tests list the spring and fall days of an hourly and a minute rule side by side ("HOURLY:", "MINUTELY:", 2026-11-04); the earlier revision of this record claimed agreement before the evaluator had it. Where: `tools/edit-harness/README.md`, series model. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **An instant the walk reaches twice.** Status: standard. A wall-clock step that resolves onto the next step's instant (an hourly rule's step to the skipped 02:00 resolves to 03:00, and the next step is 03:00) yields that instant once and counts it once: 3.8.5.3 says "Duplicate instances are ignored." Stated once in the walk, for every frequency. Verified 2026-11-04: `server/tests/expander.php` and the harness's hand-listed examples through the window expander, which is where the duplicate was found. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **A set position on a day whose clocks merge.** Status: standard. A period's set is its instants: its local times are resolved (a skipped clock by the gap rule above) and two that land on one instant are one member, and BYSETPOS counts positions among those instants, positive from the first and negative from the last, for every frequency. So `DAILY;BYHOUR=2,3;BYMINUTE=0,30;BYSETPOS=3` has no occurrence on Los Angeles' spring change day, whose four clocks are two instants (03:00 and 03:30). Else: the position taken among the nominal clocks before they resolve (03:00 that day), which the harness's evaluator read for date frequencies while its hourly path and the expander resolved first. Why: an occurrence is an instant and 3.8.5.3 ignores duplicates, so a clock merged into another holds no position of its own. Verified 2026-11-04: the harness's hand-listed examples (positive and negative positions, with and without a collision), its fixed case, and the mutation that restores the other reading in the expander. Where: `tools/edit-harness/README.md`, The Oracle. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **A rule naming its hour in a zone whose clocks move by half an hour.** Status: defect, fixed in 1.5.0. Read from the start the library's hour-by-hour stepping drifted the minute (03:00 became 03:30 after the change); read from a later window it didn't; keys were stored by the from-start reading. The app's own expander expands clock parts by the standard's table, as wall clocks of each day, so 03:00 stays 03:00 from any window, and migration 064 moves the keys the drift wrote. Verified 2026-11-04: the expander's test "Clock parts: 03:00 by BYHOUR stays 03:00 after a half-hour change", and the half-hour gap in "Gap:". Where: [#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202). Deferred 2026-11-04; fixed 2026-11-04 for 1.5.0.
- **COUNT and the days that are not shown.** Status: standard. Two different things: a day the person skipped (an EXDATE) is one of the N, since the rule generates its set, COUNT bounds that set, and exclusions are taken out afterwards (3.8.5.1's construction); a generated instance the rule can't place (30 February) is not an occurrence and is not counted (3.3.10). Under "keep N events" the count is raised for each skip the change itself generates, never for one the person made earlier, because that was a choice about that day, not a request for one more at the end. Verified: the harness's bound checks and the COUNT mutation, and the expander's "COUNT:" tests (the 31st three times, a skip one of the three). Where: series model, Bounds. Settled 2026-11-02; the wording corrected 2026-11-04.
- **Where a series starts.** Status: choice. Chosen: on a day of its rule; a start the rule doesn't name moves forward to the rule's first day, keeping the time, and the editor says so. The standard's 3.8.5.3 says a start not synchronized with its rule gives an undefined set, so there is no standard reading to follow. Else: keep the typed start as a first occurrence outside the pattern, which some calendars do. Why: one reading everywhere; a stray first day confuses every later edit. Verified: the creation tests in the PHP suite. Where: contract, event creation. Settled in 1.5.0.
- **Minute and second rules.** Status: choice (was: workaround, while the library's iterator, which has no step for them, expanded the other frequencies). They are rows of the app's own expander like any other: periods stepped in elapsed time from the start's own minute or second, BYSECOND expanding a minute, every other part limiting, as the standard's table reads them. Verified 2026-11-04: the model's unit tests, the harness's evaluator, and the expander's table tests (every SECONDLY and MINUTELY cell). Where: series model, "The expander is the app's own". Settled 2026-11-02; a choice from 2026-11-04.
- **A rule part invalid for its frequency.** Status: deviation. Chosen: a rule carrying a part the standard's table marks N/A for its frequency is one the expander can't read: refused where a rule is written, edited only by field. 3.3.10 says such parts "MUST be ignored". Why: a rule read with a part dropped means something the person didn't write, and an imported one keeps its text for the next client. Verified: the PHP suite's refusal tests, and the expander's (every N/A cell of the table refused by the expander and by the writer's check, 2026-11-04). Where: series model, same section. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **A walk that can't complete.** Status: choice. Chosen: a rule has ended when the walk has gone round the calendar's 400-year cycle together with the rule's interval phase without an occurrence, since from there it would only repeat (an interval of 401 years still finds its year; the cycle is the calendar's and the phase's together). Any other limit of the walk (a search budget) is an error the plan reports as a rule it can't read, never a series that ends there. Else: stop silently after a fixed number of tries, which 1.5.0's first candidate did, and which cut a leap-day series to one event while its check agreed. Verified: the harness's sparse cases and the count-cap mutation; the evaluator reports its own horizon as incomplete rather than ended; the expander's "End:" tests (no 30 February, an unreachable hour, 401 years, year 9999, and a walk limit reached as an error). Where: `server/src/Domain/Series/RuleIterator.php`. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **The expander is our own.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.0. Chosen: one expander written from the standard's table (`Series\RuleIterator`), read by the window, the model, the occurrence check and mail admission, with the library kept for reading and writing iCalendar text and for the CalDAV server. Why: the library's iterator ignores BYSETPOS outside MONTHLY and YEARLY, BYMONTHDAY on DAILY and every BY part on HOURLY, places a BYWEEKNO Sunday a week early, yields a phantom occurrence at an UNTIL past a month's end, and loops on a rule with no day; every fix so far was a patch around it, and the patches are where this release's caps came from. The start is always the first occurrence, an UNTIL before it bounding only what the rule generates (3.8.5.3's set is built from DTSTART). The harness's evaluator is a separate writing of the same table and stays the check. The CalDAV server reads it too, from 1.5.1: a calendar-query's time range on VEVENT is answered from the occurrences the window shows (its gap days, repeated hours, skips and edited days) under RFC 4791 9.9's table, an object passing the library's validation for every other part of the filter as well, and an `expand` request (9.6.5) on calendar-query or calendar-multiget is served from the same occurrences ([#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202)). It reads them as the window does, through `Recurrence::expand` with the request's budget, which is charged for every occurrence the walk generates, skipped and edited ones included; the range is read in windows the expander's per-window cap never cuts, each opening one second before the last one ended so an occurrence with no length on a boundary is in one, and a window still cut at two seconds is refused like a range past the budget, never answered short. What still reads the library's iterator: a free-busy-query's busy times (which objects it reads is the expander's), a calendar-query sent with Depth 0 to one object without expand, and a time range on an alarm or a property; clients expand the objects themselves, so what they show is theirs. Verified 2026-11-04: `server/tests/dav-query.php`, hand-listed, each row of 9.9's table and a series' edited, skipped, gap and all-day days through calendar-query and expand. Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/dav-query.php`, 1,100 occurrences running at one second refused and 1,000 answered in full, an occurrence with no length on the first window's end found once, and 900 skipped occurrences refused by a budget of 100. Verified 2026-11-04: `server/tests/expander.php`, one hand-listed case for every cell of the table (RFC 5545's own examples among them) and every reading in this section, with the PHP and database suites and the harness. Verified 2026-11-05 that mail admission's estimate of whether a series is still active reads the rule's BY parts through this expander ([#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202)): `server/tests/mail-security.php`, "mail capacity: a weekday list, BYHOUR, a minute rule limited by its hour and a plain rule are active through their last occurrence and no longer". Decided 2026-11-04; built 2026-11-04.

## Edits to a Series

The [series model](series-model.md) is the full statement; these are its judgment calls.

- **A drag is measured against the occurrence as it shows.** Status: choice. Every part of a drag is a delta from the occurrence as it shows, its own values where it owns them, never an absolute value passed through, because a person drags what they see. What this does not settle: a value typed into the editor. Setting an exception's 19:30 to 20:00 for all events can mean "shift the series half an hour" or "make the series 20:00", and a length typed has the same two readings; 1.5.0 reads both as deltas; from 1.5.1, from a day that owns the field, assignment is the adoption reading beside the delta, by operation and by field: a time, adopted first for a drag and a typed value alike; a length, adopted first when typed (`typed` in the request), the delta first for a resize; a day, by the period threshold either way. Where the day doesn't own the field the two readings are one plan. Verified: the harness's oracle for drags; 2026-11-05, `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Adopt, length" and "Adopt, case 14". Where: series model, Changing the Series from an Edited Day; [#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202). Settled 2026-11-02 for drags; typed values 2026-11-05.
- **A sent rule equal to the stored one is no rule.** Status: choice. Lists compare as sets, INTERVAL=1 is dropped, WKST counts only where the standard makes it count, a bare rule equals its one-member form. Why: an editor round-trip must not read as a change. Verified: the PHP suite. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **Written, not inferred.** Status: choice. "Every 31st" stays `BYMONTHDAY=31`, never rewritten into the last day, and a reading picked in the dialog is written in its own form. Why: what the person wrote is what the editor shows them next time. Verified: the harness's shape check. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **A drag on the 29th to 31st: which reading first.** Status: choice. "On the 30th, or the last day in shorter months" preselected; "skipping months without one" second; a day counted from the end keeps counting from the end first. Else: skipping first, which is what other calendars do. Why: it keeps every month's appointment; it also adds one in months the skipping reading had none, which the row says. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **A list member dragged to another weekday.** Status: choice. "Move every Wednesday to Thursday" preselected over shifting the whole pattern. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **A day moved on its own, when the timetable changes.** Status: choice. It stays where it was moved. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **Changing every event from an edited day.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.1. Each part the day owns and the change moved (its date, time, length) is a group: adopt it as the series' own, or move the series the same way and keep it the day's (1.5.0's reading). Adopting the day is preselected when it moves every event by less than one period of the rule's frequency, the same way first when it is a period or more; adopting the time always. The period is the frequency's as written, never the family's: a DAILY list (Mondays and Wednesdays written DAILY) repeats daily, so its period is a day, where a WEEKLY list's is a week (was: the family's week for both, found by the edit harness, fixed 2026-11-05, `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Adopt, by frequency"). An adoption that would be refused is disabled and never preselected; the same way is then the selection. The threshold is a weak signal (a small change across it flips the default) and stays a judgment; what has to hold is that each choice shows its concrete schedule and the selection is explicit. Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Adopt, case 12/13/14/21" and "Adopt, apart", and the smoke test "adoption:" for the dialog's words. Where: [#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202), series model. Settled 2026-11-02, built 2026-11-05.
- **A day the new rule gains at an edge.** Status: choice. Left out by default, as a generated skip, with "Add it" offered. Why: nothing appears the person didn't ask for. Settled 2026-11-02; preselection moved after the fourth review.
- **A count that would end before the day dragged.** Status: choice. "Keep the end" preselected, the dragged day staying in the series, and the row says the count grows; "keep N events + this one on its own" offered. Settled 2026-11-02; preselection moved after the fourth review.
- **A bound the person sent that cuts off the day acted on.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.1. The Day Acted On Comes First is a rule for drags, where the destination is the person's intent, so a kept count that would end before it is rewritten to keep it. An UNTIL or COUNT typed into the editor is the person's own statement, so a day acted on that it cuts off is an ordinary day outside the bound: it takes the outside group's choice, kept as an event of its own where it was put (preselected, as for every day outside) or deleted, and has exactly one outcome in the plan. Each day's outcome comes from one function in the model, so no day is in two of the plan's lists, and `next` is read from the same outcomes: kept, the day is listed once where it shows, whether or not the request placed it; deleted, not at all. Was: the day acted on was always kept on its own, and with "Delete" chosen for the other days outside it was both detached and deleted (found by the edit harness on a week-number rule); and an UNTIL sent alone kept the day but left it out of `next` (found by the edit harness on a monthly rule). Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Sent bound (weekly)", "Sent bound (week number)" and "Sent bound (UNTIL alone)", and `server/tests/db/series-plans.test.php`, "a bound sent from an edited day that cuts it off" (with "sent UNTIL alone"), each choice committed and undone. Settled 2026-11-05.
- **Scopes a calendar kind cannot take are not offered.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.1. A scope the server refuses for every occurrence of a kind of calendar is left out of every chooser (the delete, answer and new-time rows, the drop chip after a drag, resize, reschedule or a calendar or person drop, and the editor's Apply to), which all read one function, `seriesScopes` in `web/src/app/scopes.js`; where it leaves a scope out the chooser says why in one line, taken from `planwords.js` beside the refusal panel's own sentence for the same code, so the two cannot drift. On a Google calendar that leaves out "This and following" until 1.5.4 (the server refuses the split from any day after the first; from the first day it is all events), and the server's 422 `series_refused` stays as the backstop. A date edited on its own that is moving to another calendar loses following through the same function. Every series dialog has Change scope: delete following and an answer from a day on return to the row that asked, and the choice made there goes through the same preview path. Verified 2026-11-05: the smoke section "the scopes an occurrence has, one rule for every chooser". Settled 2026-11-05.
- **An UNTIL after a drag.** Status: choice. Kept as the end by default, rewritten as the latest image at or before it, with "keep N events" offered when the count changes. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **How far an unbounded series is read.** Status: choice. Chosen: one whole cycle of the rule's periods past the latest day the change names (the new start among them), widened by the change's reach (series model, Bounds: one cycle per family, one period daily and weekly, 4,800 / gcd(n, 4,800) monthly, 400 / gcd(n, 400) yearly), no number chosen; losses and gains are reported by class (the reason and the member, the first days, a count for a bounded series, a rate over the cycle for one with no end), never as a list of every day, and the old occurrences, their images and the new rule's days are the rules' own arithmetic over periods, never an expansion. Why: the first draft listed every day and took the model's tests from 5 seconds to over 4 minutes and out of memory; a whole cycle costs what a walk of its periods costs, and the classes are what a person reads anyway. Verified 2026-11-06: `server/tests/series-model.php` (Book club, skipping: one class, 1 in every 12 months; Salon night: one class of gains, 5 in every 12 months; Cello lesson, keep 6: a bounded series counts its gains; Book club of fourteen: six lost, five listed and one more; a start moved alone on Book club, Stand-up and Book club of six: the days before the new start counted, never given a rate) and the harness's c-placement (every listed class against the harness's own classes over the same span, seed 23, 3,000 cases). Where: [#205](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/205). Settled 2026-11-04; built 2026-11-06.
- **No display cap.** Status: choice. Bounds and splits are worked out over the actual span; the one limit left (400,000 occurrences) is a refusal, never a partial result. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **Rules several times a day.** Status: choice. Every series-wide move of their days, time or zone is refused with its reason; "this event" edits a day by its key. Why: only imports carry them, and the identity machinery for two occurrences a day isn't worth building for them. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **An edited day's all-day flag.** Status: choice. A field like the others; a day deliberately timed in an all-day series stays timed when the series toggles. Reverses the first draft's decision 8. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **What an outcome is.** Status: choice. A day's outcome comes from its start instant and its kind before and after, never its dates; a day whose length alone changes "stays". Verified: the harness's placement check and its mutation. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **A skip belongs to the occurrence it removed.** Status: choice. It moves with that occurrence and is dropped with it, never carried onto a day the person never removed. Verified: the harness. Settled 2026-11-02.
- **The plan's zone.** Status: choice. The series' zone after the change, with every timed value of both sides written in it; an all-day series has no plan zone and its timed days are written in their own. Why: one zone to read a comparison in. Verified: the harness's placement checks. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **The zone "same moment" choice with a member reading.** Status: choice, for 1.5.1. Chosen: "same moment" keeps every row's own instant. The series repeats at the instant the day acted on keeps, read as a clock in the new zone; every edited day keeps the instant it shows at and lands on whatever local day that instant has in the new zone (a day with its own time may not change its date at all), a day on a date of its own at the series' time as much as one at its own time, its inherited time moved only on the clock it showed on (fixed 2026-11-05: its old-zone date was read at the new zone's clock, a day off; `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Zone moment, no drag: a day on its own date", and the database suite's "the same moment in another zone"; and fixed again 2026-11-05: acted on from a day in a zone of its own, whose instant read in the new zone put the series' new keys on another day of the old zone's calendar, a day at its own time moved by that as if dragged; a reading the moment carries no days is its own drag, `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Zone moment from a day in its own zone", and the database suite's same scenario); a skip stays on its occurrence; nothing is measured as a drag. Rejected: reading the change as a drag by the day shift the day acted on showed, so that every edited day moves a day later. Why: the shift was an artifact of subtracting a day read in the new zone from one read in the old, not a thing anyone asked for, and it is wrong for a day whose own clock doesn't cross midnight; the dialog already lists the occurrences whose instants differ where the two places change their clocks on different weeks. How it is built: the request's wall clock is read on the clock it showed on, any drag in it is measured in the old zone alone, and the result is carried to the new zone by the two zones' offsets at that instant: the series' clock there and the days its instants move on the new calendar, every member alike (the drag table's whole-pattern shift by those days). Verified 2026-11-04: `server/tests/series-model.php`, "Zone moment:" (the example above, both readings, and an edited day at its own time keeping its instant) and F9 (a gap day carries the series as any other). Where: [#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202), [series model](series-model.md#zones-dst-and-all-day-days). Deferred 2026-11-04; decided 2026-11-04; built 2026-11-04.
- **A monthly or yearly carry across a day boundary.** Status: choice. Chosen: when the same moment carries a monthly or yearly series to another day on the new zone's calendar, the carry is the drag table's whole-pattern shift by those days and takes its preselected reading where the family offers several. Why: RFC 5545 defines a rule in one zone and says nothing of a rule carried to another, so the standard leaves it undefined; reading it as the drag the dialog would preselect keeps one rule for every shift. Verified: the harness's oracle takes the same reading (README, Known, Accepted Differences). Where: [series model](series-model.md#zones-dst-and-all-day-days). Settled 2026-11-05.
- **A rule the expander can't read.** Status: choice. Its COUNT or UNTIL is still read from the text, so the plan says how it ends, with no dates. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **"This and following" and deletes.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.1. A split is a bound change on the old series (UNTIL at its last occurrence before the day acted on, the skips and edited days keyed before it kept) plus the part from that day as a series of its own, planned as a whole series under the edit, previewed and committed through the plan API like any change, in one Undo entry; delete following is the old part alone, an answer from a day on the split with no fields and then the answer. Sent without a plan it is 409, since a split always changes a bound. Was: 1.5.0 kept their 1.4.6 code and the drop chip. Verified 2026-11-04: `server/tests/db/series-split.test.php` (preview, commit with each choice, stale and missing plans, delete following and an answer, each undone; a part's BYHOUR in step with its start; a part made all-day losing its clock parts; a part several times a day refused all-day), with the PHP and database suites. The harness's driver still sends these without a plan, so until its owner moves it onto the plan API its following cases fail as `series_plan_required` under its known cause. Settled 2026-11-02; built 2026-11-04.
- **A split's sent rule.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.1. Chosen: the rule sent with "this and following" is compared with the series' rule as shown, which is what the editor showed and sent back, never with the new part's, so the model's "A sent rule that is unchanged is no rule" holds for a split exactly as for the whole series, and the part keeps the rest of the series' count. A changed COUNT is the series' whole count, the part taking what is left of it (preselected, since the number shown was the series'), with that many from this day on as the other reading in the bound group (`whole`, `part`), each option showing its end; a whole count the old part already reaches leaves the part nothing and is disabled (`empty`). A changed UNTIL is the part's end as sent; one before the split day leaves the part nothing and is refused as an end before the start is, the same code and words (`empty`), nothing written. A COUNT sent with a new pattern is the part's own: the two parts no longer share a rule, so the series' whole count means nothing, and "every 2nd Wednesday, 3 events" from this day on is three from this day on, with no second reading. Was: the rule went to the part as sent and was compared with the part's own, so a COUNT=6 sent back unchanged gave the part six more events than the series showed, and an UNTIL before the series' last day, split there, read as the end it already had (found by the edit harness after "this and following" moved onto the plan API). Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/db/series-split.test.php`, "the series' rule sent back unchanged", "a changed count" and "a sent end before the split day", each committed or refused and undone. Where: [series model](series-model.md#changing-the-series-from-an-edited-day), api-contract "This and following". Settled 2026-11-05.
- **Google calendars.** Status: probed, build in 1.5.4. Until 1.5.4, only edits that keep the rule's text are offered there, their effect on edited days unchecked, and a plan note says so. The probe of 2026-11-07 read back what Google stores for every kind of edit (series-model.md, Google as a Contract): Google resets every exception on a change of clock, zone, start day or rule, overwrites exceptions' own fields on a field or status change, keeps them on a length or bound change, and answers 412 to a stale If-Match; so the app re-sends each edited day after the master, under its new instance id, and every write is conditional. Settled 2026-11-02; probed 2026-11-07.
- **Floating times.** Status: planned. For the model, once [#167](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/167) lands: a floating key is a wall-clock identity (stored as that wall clock written as if UTC, so arithmetic on it has no DST), never read as a UTC instant, and a zone change between floating and pinned keeps every wall clock. Settled 2026-11-02 for the model; storage open on #167.

- **An inherited time on a day of its own is the series' clock on that date.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. A day moved by its date alone off a spring-gap day (where it showed at 03:30 for a 02:30 series) shows at 02:30 on its new date and follows every later clock change exactly. Else: keep the shifted hour it showed and carry it through every clock change, which 1.5.0 to 1.5.4 did; the gap was a fact about the old date, not about the day. The "this event" write stores the start the day's own list implies (its own date or its occurrence's, its own clock or the series'), never the clock the request showed, and migration 072 places rows stored the old way. Only a rule several times a day, which has no one clock to inherit, carries the time a day shows. Verified: fixed cases 9808 and 9809, b-placed and a-time agreeing. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **A choice the Google side refuses moves the selection, never the whole change.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. When the preselected option of any group (a reading, a bound, an edge, days outside, an own field) would keep a timed day under an all-day series at Google, that option is disabled with its reason and the group's first option Google can hold is selected; the change is refused only when no option of any group holds. Readings followed this from 1.5.4; the other groups refused the whole change until 2026-11-08, and a refused reading was once merged away as a duplicate of another, so a change was refused when another reading could be held. Verified: fixed cases 9810, 9816 and 9818; invariant i reads what Google can hold from each option's local plan. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **The day acted on's outcome is any row's.** Status: choice (the 2026-11-04 rule applied to one more row). A created row for the day acted on says so in its own field (`row: "created"`); its outcome compares its instant and kind before and after like every other row's, so a same-moment zone change from a gap day reads "stays", not "moves". Else: "moves" meaning "a row was made", which the client once read the field for. Verified: fixed case 9815, mutation acted-created-always-moves. Settled 2026-11-08.

## What the Panel Says

The plan is data, and the app composes its sentences in the person's locale and time format.

- **A date is said once.** Status: choice. A date is left out against the same date earlier in the sentence only when both are in the same zone; a date said again because the one before it is in another zone carries its zone on its clock. Verified: the smoke tests. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **An all-day setting of its own.** Status: choice. Read by the day's kind, and a day that becomes timed "now starts at" its clock. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **A zone the device can't read.** Status: choice, with a planned safeguard. The clock is never shown in the device's zone, because a clock in the wrong zone reads as a fact. 1.5.0's first candidate dropped the clause silently, which makes an incomplete preview look complete; the safeguard is a short "time unavailable" mark in its place, so the person can see what the preview couldn't say. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **A repeated clock's note.** Status: choice. "This is the second, in standard time", from the note's data; with neither instant in daylight time the clause after the comma is left out. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **Rows say what was done earlier, never what is kept,** and days are grouped by outcome. A group's line says what all its days do: where they go, and what every one of them takes (a length change: "stay where they are and take the new length"). Verified 2026-11-05: the smoke test "a length-only change". Settled 2026-11-03; takes 2026-11-05.
- **One field table for the editor's save and the panel's lines.** Status: choice. `EDIT_FIELDS` (web/src/lib/editfields.js) lists every field the editor can send, with the fields that go together, its label and how its change reads; the save sends only fields of the table and the panel's lines come from it, so nothing is sent unsaid. What a day keeps reads the server's one table of own fields (`Model::OWN_WORDS`), references included. Verified 2026-11-05: the smoke tests "field table:" and the PHP test "Keeps:". Settled 2026-11-05.
- **"Google Calendar" names the calendar service, "Google" the account.** Status: choice (#285, 1.5.8; the name 1.5.7 settled in the conflict choice). Wherever a person reads where a change went, what the service holds, did or refused, the words are "in Google Calendar", "Google Calendar holds", "Google Calendar refused" (server messages, Activity and Review lines, toasts, dialogs, settings, the user docs); "Google" alone stays for the account and its sign-in ("Connect a Google account", "Reconnect on Google", "Google didn't grant the permission"), and "a Google calendar" stays as the kind of calendar here ("on Google calendar 'Book club'"). A sentence names the service once: a second mention is "there" or "it" ("This event was deleted in Google Calendar; ... Refresh to see the calendar as it is there"). Every toast of a change sent there says ", here and in Google Calendar" through one function, `whereWords` (web/src/lib/planwords.js), which the series toast now uses too. Turned down: search and replace of "at Google", which doubled the name in sentences that already said it ("Google Calendar has a fault here: ..., Google would stop showing"). Stored text keeps its words: lines written by an earlier version are not rewritten, and `GoogleUndo::actionOf` still cuts an old line at its calendar, which comes before any of the reworded parts. Log lines, exception texts no person reads (a lease lost, a recovery marker), comments and API field names are unchanged. Verified by the unit, frontend and database suites, whose asserted sentences changed with the words.
- **Lines true but reading wrong, said from the before and the after** (#281, 1.5.8). Status: choice. A change to all events whose rule and clock read the same before and after (every event moved a week later) says the first day, old and new ("Moved Sample choir (all events): starts 12 Jan (was 5 Jan)."), and no longer the same rule twice; a start moved alone says the same, without the rule "as before". What a zone change kept, where the choice was offered, is read from the clocks, never from the option's name: the clock time where the clock is the same, the same moment where the new clock in the new zone is the old clock's instant, neither where a time moved as well (the "clock" option labels the clock as typed, which can be the old instant). A Google calendar whose access dropped below reader is no failing feed: `GoogleSync` says it with `AccessStateException`, and `Feeds::pollFailed` records that poll as one that reached Google (no streak, no "started failing" or "recovered" line in Activity, no "hasn't updated" toast, no error badge); the access line and the Review notice are its voice. A recovery after a real streak names the time in words and none under a minute ("recovered after 2 failed attempts", "over 3 hours and 20 minutes"; was "after 2 failures over 0m"). A half-done change's Review sentence ends its reason with a full stop and names no step (the step stays in `detail`). A poll's Activity line counts events as a person does, a repeating event and its edited days as one, by the uid every row of a series shares ("1 removed" for a series that went with its edited day; was "2 removed"), for updated as for removed. Not done: the line names a primary calendar by its address, since that is the calendar's name here (Google names a primary calendar after its account); left to the owner. Verified by series-model's Activity entries and google-lines-read-right, which failed before the change.
- **A yearly day some listed months lack.** Status: choice. The months that have the day, each with it, then the others as skipped ("Yearly on 31 March, skipping February"), never a date a month doesn't have; the 29th says February's leap years. One table of rules (`server/tests/fixtures/yearly-words.json`) is read by the server's tests and the editor's, each in its own date order. Was: the server said "31 February and March". Settled 2026-11-05.

## Trips and Links

- **A trip never contains a trip.** Status: choice. One end-state rule checked after every write, by the writers and by Undo alike; Undo refuses a state that would break it and says what it would break. Else: one-direction guards at each writer, which 1.5.0's first candidate had and which let a nesting through a dormant link. Verified: the database suite's trip tests and the two Undo-gate mutations. Verified 2026-11-05 that no undo goes around the gate ([#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202), "Undo passes the trip gate"): the last change, an entry by id with and without force, a run strict and not, and a proposal's undo all restore through one path that ends in `Trips::assertRules`, and a plugin's write-back undo restores only an event's time; `server/tests/db/undo-trips.test.php` runs each case through every one of those paths, the issue's example among them ("detach, then flag the stop"). Settled 2026-11-04.
- **A link whose flags are both clear stays.** Status: choice. It is a relationship the person made and stands until they remove it; the end-state rule alone judges it. It stays visible and removable in the editor. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **A row exists for its own fields and for whatever refers to it.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.1. A day something names keeps its row when it would otherwise rejoin its series, by one table of what refers to a row (`Series\OwnFields::REFERENCES`): its trip links, its duplicate decisions (a pair linked or dismissed; a possible pair is the scan's proposal, made again, not a decision) and its plugin data. The plan lists each kind in the day's `kept` (`trip`, `duplicates`, `pluginData`), and a rejoin never cascade-deletes what names the row. The same table is what Undo keeps of a row a change deletes (`Undo::linksOf`, with its tags and people), so an undone delete brings back its trip links, duplicate decisions and plugin data with it. Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/series-model.php` ("F1: a day a duplicate decision or plugin data names...") and `server/tests/db/series-plans.test.php` ("an edited day a duplicate decision or plugin data names keeps its row and what names it", a whole-series rename, its Undo and "Use series value" for each kind, and a possible pair that rejoins), and `server/tests/db/undo.test.php` (an edited day deleted comes back with its duplicate decision and plugin data). Where: [#202](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/202). Settled 2026-11-04; extended 2026-11-05.
- **Trips in ICS and CalDAV.** Status: standard. `RELATED-TO;RELTYPE=CHILD` on the trip and `RELTYPE=PARENT` on each member. Settled before 1.0.
- **Renaming a tag onto a name another tag has merges the two (#134).** Status: choice, built for 1.5.8. Chosen: `PATCH /tags/:id` with a name another of the account's tags has (compared as the database compares names, so case aside) moves every link of the renamed tag to that one and removes the renamed tag; the survivor keeps its own spelling. The app asks first, naming both tags and their event counts. People already merge this way, and a rename onto a taken name is nearly always someone tidying "Food" and "food" into one. Else: refuse with a plain reason (leaves the person to retag every event by hand, the job the rename was for). Rename and delete are each one function (`Tags::rename`, `Tags::delete`) that changes the tag's links on every row at once, a series' and each of its days', so a day following its series still holds exactly the series' set and a day owning its tags keeps owning them; neither calls `OwnFields::follow`, as the same change is made to every set. Each is one journal entry through `Undo::write` holding the tag rows and every link to them, so undo puts the tags and links back by the same restore as any other entry (a day made since follows its series by `Undo::followLinks`). Filters, saved views and outgoing search feeds hold text, not tags: neither a rename nor a delete changes them. The list says how many keyword and regex filters on tags match each name, so a rename that leaves a filter behind is visible before it happens. Else: rewrite a filter's pattern (a regex or substring can't be rewritten safely). Verified: tags.test.php (rename, a change of case, merge, delete and each undone; a repeating event with a day owning its tags and one following, through rename, merge, delete and each undo; refusals; the filter count) and link-ownership.test.php (merge and delete undone with a day made since, both in its inventory). Settled 2026-11-15.
- **A tag is offered while an event carries it, read from the links each time (#134).** Status: choice, built for 1.5.8. Chosen: the tags `GET /calendars` and `GET /tags` return are those on at least one event that isn't deleted (`Tags::inUse`, one query), so a tag nothing carries drops out of autocomplete, search's chips and the list as soon as its last event loses it, and comes back if an undo puts the event back. The tag's row stays: an older journal entry that names it can still put it back on its event. Else: delete the row when its last link goes, in every writer that removes links (a branch in each, and an undo of an older change would then find its tag gone); or a sweep on a timer (a tag lingers until it runs). Cost: a join over the account's tag links on each list, which is short next to its events. Verified: tags.test.php, which fails on the tree before this change (every tag the account ever made was offered). Settled 2026-11-15.
- **A tag's name that a person types in the app is one the `#` marker can write; the API takes any name (#310).** Status: the owner's choice, built for 1.5.8. Chosen: one spelling rule for a tag's name, `Tags::NAME_PATTERN` (letters, digits, hyphens and underscores, starting with a letter or digit, at least one letter, at most 50 characters), from which quick add's `#` marker builds its pattern; the app's copy (`TAG_NAME` in web/src/lib/markersuggest.js, used by the marker suggestions, the rename field and the editor's Tags field) is the same text, compared by the unit suite, which also checks that the rule allows a name exactly when the marker reads it back whole. The rule covers what a person types in the app: the `#` marker (it is the rule), a rename (`PATCH /tags/:id` refuses any other name with 400 `tag_name_unwritable` and the rule in words; the rename field says so as it is typed and keeps Save off), and the editor's Tags field (a typed name that is not already one of the account's tags, nor on the event, and breaks the rule is named under the field with the same words, and Save stays off until it is changed; a name already a tag is kept however it is spelled, so a list mixing an older tag with a space and a new bad name names only the new one). The API route, `tagNames` on `POST`/`PATCH /events` (a script with an API key, a series plan; the agent API's MCP tools send no `tagNames` and make tags only through quick add's marker), still takes any text up to 80 characters: the test suite and scripts make such names, and the owner may allow spaces later. Quick add makes tags only by the marker, and import, CalDAV, feeds, Google, mail and plugins make none. Existing tags are not rewritten: one with a space is listed, carried and searchable by words, can be deleted, and can be renamed to a name that follows the rule. To allow spaces later, the rule changes in its one place (`NAME_PATTERN` and the app's pinned copy), the `#` marker needs a form that holds a space (a quoted `#"two words"`, say) which quick add and search read, and autocomplete (`tagMarker`) must insert that form. Else: refuse such names on `tagNames` too (about 45 database test files make tags with spaces through it, and a script's save would fail on a name it has always sent); or let rename take any name (a tag renamed with a space could be shown but never typed after `#`). Verified: tags.test.php (six kinds of name refused with nothing written, the reason names the characters, a tag made with a space still offered, renamed to a name the rule allows, and deleted), the unit suite's tag-name block, and the smoke suite's rename-field and Tags-field checks (new names refused, existing odd names kept, the mixed list). Settled 2026-11-15.

## Quick Add

How typed text is read when more than one reading is plausible. Every reading here is a guess the person can correct in the editor before saving; the full list is in [quick-add.md](../quick-add.md).

- **A date already past this year means next year.** Status: choice. Settled before 1.0.
- **"At 7".** Status: choice. 1 to 7 are afternoon, 8 to 11 morning. Settled before 1.0.
- **A day range with a time range.** Status: choice, with its correction built for 1.5.1. "Conference Aug 3-7 9am-5pm" is one stretch from the first morning to the last evening when the end is later in the day than the start; "Shift Aug 3-7 10pm-6am" repeats each night. The daytime shape alone can't tell a conference from a week of daily sessions, so the draft carries the other reading (`draft.other`) and quick add's chips offer it in one tap, "Each day instead" or "One event instead", a second tap putting it back. One rule both ways round: a timed stretch over several days ending later in the day than it starts is also each day; a daily series of 2 to 31 days is also one stretch. Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/run.php`, "quick add other reading", and the smoke tests of the same name. Where: [#198](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/198), quick-add.md. Settled in 1.5.0; correction 2026-11-05.
- **The model never overrides a typed range.** Status: choice. Settled in 1.5.0.
- **Date words are never names, and a weekday beside a date.** Status: choice. A word the date and time readers take (a weekday, a month with its day, a time, "tomorrow") is never offered as a person or a place: the with-clause and the place are read by those same readers and keep only what is left, so there is no separate list of words. A weekday right before or after a calendar date ("November 16, Monday", "Mon, Nov 16") goes with the date when it names that date's day; one that names another day stays in the title and the calendar date wins, as before. Else: the weekday was left in the text and offered as a second person. Verified 2026-11-08: `server/tests/run.php` fp48 to fp54. Where: `FallbackParser::withoutWhen`, `confirmingWeekday`.
- **A cut takes its separator.** Status: choice. Every piece the parser reads out of the text (a date, a time, a weekday, a repeat) goes through one cut, `FallbackParser::cutSpan`, which takes the comma or semicolon right after the piece, else the one right before it, so a pasted "Monday, November 16, 6:30 PM, at Luna" leaves no ", ,". Commas between words the parser did not read stay ("Salt, Fat, Acid, Heat reading"); there is no scrub of the finished title beyond trimming its ends, as before. Else: the cut left a space and the separators stayed. Verified 2026-11-08: `server/tests/run.php` fp55 to fp60; the golden readings unchanged.
- **Where a place ends, and a street address.** Status: choice, the address's title by the owner's answer on #258. One reader, `FallbackParser::place`: the place runs from "at" or "@" to the end of the text or to a with-clause, the comma or semicolon before "with" not part of it, a comma inside it kept ("at Sample Theater, with Alex" is Sample Theater; "at 123 Example St, Springfield CA" keeps its city). A place that starts with a number is a street address when a capitalized name follows the number, the parser's sign of a name as for "Sun Valley"; a number with no name after it ("at 7 sharp") is no place, and a time after "at" was read as the time before the place reader runs. A street address leaves the title once it is the place ("Lunch tomorrow 3pm at 123 Example St" is "Lunch"), which the model's prompt already said; a venue's name stays. Else: the place stopped at the first comma (a comma before "with" lost it, #236) and refused anything starting with a digit (#258). Also weighed: two or more words after the number instead of a capital, turned down because "at 6 or 7" would be a place; the cost is that "at 123 example st" in lower case stays in the title. A trailing note after the place and a comma ("at Luna, bring the book") is now part of the place, where it used to lose the place. Verified 2026-11-15: `server/tests/run.php` fp61 to fp69 (failing before the change but fp67 to fp69), the phrase table's "Dinner at 5 Main St", and 36 golden readings, every one "Lunch at 9 Elm Court ...", reviewed and regenerated.
- **The next such weekday, and a past date's year: one function each.** Status: choice, the reading by the owner's answer on #288. `FallbackParser::weekdayAhead` gives the days to the next such weekday, today counting or not, and every arm that reads a weekday ahead calls it (a weekday alone or with "this" or "next", "N weeks from Friday", "Friday after next", a day range, "next week Friday", "Mon-Fri next week"). A weekday counted from is the coming one, never today: typed on a Friday, "a week from Friday" is 14 days out, as "2 weeks from Friday" and "Friday after next" already were; its own arm, which counted from today and was reached when the article was left off ("starting week from Friday"), is gone, and the counted arm reads a bare "week from". The start phrase leaves "from Friday" to the counted reading by the same pattern (`FallbackParser::counted`), so "Dentist week from Friday" is "Dentist" a week past the coming Friday, where the start phrase used to take "from Friday" and leave "week" in the title (owner's choice, 2026-11-15). Its cost, accepted: "Offsite week from Monday to Friday" reads that one Monday with "to Friday" in the title, where it read the coming Monday with "Offsite week to Friday"; quick-add.md's Limits says to write "Mon-Fri next week". Verified by fp73, failing before. `FallbackParser::upcoming` is the one "a date without a year is next year's once past" (dates, ranges, m/d, nth weekday of a month). The client's jump parser has its own copy of both: its past-date rule agrees; its weekday is always strictly ahead, so "friday" typed on a Friday jumps a week out where quick add reads today, a choice its comment states, left as it is. Else: the rule written out per arm, one copy disagreeing. Verified 2026-11-15: `server/tests/run.php` fp70 to fp72, each phrasing typed on the day it names (fp70 failing before the change); the golden readings unchanged by this part.
- **What a weekday's name is.** Status: choice, by the owner, 2026-11-15. One definition, `WeekdayName`: the full name or a usual short form (sun, mon, tue, tues, wed, thu, thur, thurs, fri, sat), any case, a trailing period or not, with `number()` and `isShort()`. Every reader of a weekday uses it: FallbackParser's counted phrases ("week from fri", "3 weeks from Mon"), "after next", "first Wed of September", "next week Tues", the "all week" check, the start phrase's exception, the bare weekday, the timed stretch and the weekday beside a date, and RepeatParser's repeats and day ranges. Before, the first six read full names only (FallbackParser's `WEEKDAYS`), the last three of FallbackParser read short ones from a second list (`WEEKDAY_ABBR`), and RepeatParser had a third, its own pattern. Where a short form is also a word stays for the reader to weigh: the bare weekday still skips a short name before a capitalized word ("Sun Valley", "Sat Nav"), in a dash range, or first in the line with no time after it ("Sat down with Alex Example Friday", "Wed planning"); the counted phrases need no such guard, as their words around the name say it is a day. Else: three lists, the counted arms missing the short names. Verified 2026-11-15: `server/tests/run.php` fp74 (failing before) and fp75 (the ordinary words, passing before and after); the golden readings unchanged.
- **Two-letter zones.** Status: choice. Read only in capitals and never before an ordinary word. Settled before 1.0.
- **A date already in the editor.** Status: choice. A client with a chosen day keeps it unless the text names one. Settled before 1.0.
- **A pasted event link is read as the page it names.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.8 (#221). Every link goes through one normalisation before any reader sees it (`EventPage::address`): query parameters that only record where a link was shared from (`utm_*` and a fixed list of click ids such as `fbclid` and `gclid`) come off, every other parameter stays as written; the cache key and the event's kept link use the result. Some extras aren't tracking and can't be listed: an invitation or referral code made one site serve a page without its event data. For those, a page that shows no event at the address read but names its own (`<link rel="canonical">`, `EventPage::canonicalIn`) is read there once, and the model, when it reads, reads that page. Considered: a strip list per site (each new site and code a new case), and dropping the whole query when nothing is found (a site whose query names the event would read a listing page and could pick another event). It costs one more request for a page with no event that names another address. Verified: `run.php`'s event page checks (tracking off, other parameters kept, canonical read only when the page has no event; seen failing without the change), and on 2026-11-15 a live Luma link with an invitation code and click ids read through `EventPage::read` to its event.

## Relationships and Review

- **Context is what a calendar is, never what an event is.** Status: choice. Where: [relationships.md](../relationships.md). Settled before 1.0.
- **What your answer to a Google invitation means.** Status: choice. Accepted is planned, anything else is maybe; without an answer the event's status decides. Settled before 1.0.
- **A cancellation older than the invitation.** Status: choice. It closes a waiting invitation only when it is at least as new as the invitation. Settled in 1.5.0.
- **A filter's dim and highlight are one rule for every shape** (#72). Status: choice. Chosen: `lib/filtermarks.js` turns the server's marks on an occurrence (`dimmed`, `highlighted`, `highlightColor`) into the classes and the `--hl` colour, and everything drawn for an occurrence takes them from there: chips, bars, blocks and bands (`EventChip`), context tokens, moments on the timeline and holiday labels (`ContextStrip`), the day sheet's context rows (`DayExpand`). One mark standing for several occurrences (a group of near-duplicates, a day's holidays as one label) is dimmed only when all are and highlighted when any is, in the first one's colour (`combinedFilterMarks`, which grouping now uses too). A moment's marks sit on its label and line, never on the mark around them: a dim's opacity there makes the mark one stacking layer and drops both below the events they cross. Context, already grey italic, is also written in the highlight's colour, since an underline alone barely reads on it. Else: the server already sent the marks for context, and each context renderer could have read them its own way, as none did (a highlight filter on rain changed nothing visible). Not built: the issue's "highlight the day" target. It is a new filter option with open questions in the issue itself (whether a day tint follows events Show has hidden, and how it sits beside today and the weekend shading), and [day marks](day-marks.md) records that a tint across the day's header was replaced for holidays because it looks bad behind coloured chips and grey text; it needs a design pass, not a fix. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/filter-marks.mjs` draws each shape in the stand-in DOM with dimmed, highlighted (with and without a colour) and unmatched items, and reads the stylesheet's rules for each; with the context renderers as they were it fails on the first context token. Not verified by eye: how strong the dim reads on tokens that are already muted.
- **A "+N" wears the marks of what it folds** (#307). Status: choice. Chosen: every mark that stands for items out of sight takes `foldedMarks` (`combinedFilterMarks` over the folded items): highlighted, in the first one's colour, when any is; dimmed only when all are. That is the context strip's "+N", the month cell's "+N more" (its singles and the bars and bands past the lanes), the week's all-day "+N", the timeline's "+N more" (events past the lanes and moments past the few drawn), the day view's all-day "+N more" and the agenda's "+N more on this day". Each now keeps the folded items, not only their count (`overflowItems` from `layoutOverlaps`, `folded` from the agenda's rows and the day view's bars, `foldedByCol` from the month's lanes, the bars themselves in the week's `moreByCol`). Where grayed-out events are left out of a "+N" by design (#126: month and week), they are not among the folded items either, so that "+N" is never dimmed. Else: a highlighted item that did not fit showed nothing until the day was opened. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/filter-marks.mjs` draws the context strip's "+N" with a highlighted token folded (and one drawn), all and not all folded tokens dimmed; checks the folded lists the timeline layout and the agenda name; reads each "+N"'s class in the month, week, timeline, day view and agenda; with the strip as it was it fails on the first. The month, week, timeline and day view "+N" are checked by reading their source, not drawn.
- **A filter's card says what it matches, from the matcher** (#63, first part). Status: choice. Chosen: each card says in one sentence what its filter looks at and what happens to a match ("Events are hidden when this text appears in their title or the name of one of their tags, ignoring case."); an AI prompt filter's card says the model sees each event's title, the first 300 characters of its description, its location and its start time and nothing else, that only events on subscribed and Google calendars are judged (`source = 'feed'`), and which events take the action (the filter's side, see "Every filter says which events its action takes") while events not judged yet stay as they are. The sentences live in `lib/filterwords.js`; the smoke suite reads `Filters.php` and `PromptEval.php` (the default and allowed fields, the substring and case-insensitive regex match, tags by name, the excerpt length, the payload's keys, the feed-only query) and fails when they say something the sentences do not. Else: the server could send the description with each filter; kept on the client because the words are presentation and the server's facts are already pinned by the test. Not in this release: the new conditions (organizer, people, calendar, links, time). Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/filter-marks.mjs`.
- **Which events an AI prompt filter's action takes.** Status: superseded on 2026-11-16 by the next entry, "Every filter says which events its action takes"; kept as history. It was the owner's choice of 2026-11-15, deployed for a day, and the next entry replaces it after he saw his own prompt dim filter flip. Chosen: dim and highlight take the events the model judges to fit the description; hide takes the ones it judges not to fit, so a hide filter keeps what fits, as its form has always said ("Keep events that match"). One function reads a stored verdict, `Filters::promptActionApplies(action, verdict)`, and `promptFilterContext` builds from it the set of events each filter's action applies to; the window, search, the highlight's colour and the hover card's reason all read that set. The stored verdicts are unchanged (`pass` still means the event fits, less anything the negative prompt names), so nothing is rewritten and no format changes: only their reading does. Else (was, to 1.5.7): every action took the events judged not to fit, so "highlight outdoor things" highlighted everything else. Cost: an existing prompt dim or highlight filter flips on upgrade, now marking the events it used to leave and leaving the ones it marked; its description may need rewording to keep the old effect. Hide filters are unchanged. The form's label follows the action ("Keep", "Dim" or "Highlight events that match"), and the card says which events take it. Verified 2026-11-15: `server/tests/db/prompt-filter-actions.test.php` (each action, an event judged to fit, one judged not to and one not judged yet, in the window and in search; with the old reading it fails 11 checks), `server/tests/run.php` "pa" checks, and `web/tests/filter-words.mjs`, which reads the function and checks the cards say the same.
- **Every filter says which events its action takes** (1.5.8). Status: choice, the owner's (2026-11-16). Model: one stored field per filter, `filters.applies_to`, `match` (the events the filter matches) or `nonmatch` (the events in its scope it does not), set beside the action, so a filter reads as a sentence: "Dim events that don't match". One function decides, for every kind: `Filters::takesAction(appliesTo, matches)`, where `matches` is a keyword or regex hit, or for an AI prompt a stored verdict of pass; when the match is not known (a prompt with no verdict yet, a regex that gave up on a field under its PCRE limits or the run's budget) it is null and neither side acts. The keyword and regex readers (`disposition`, `highlightColorFor`, `reasonFor`) go through it with the scope check (`keywordTakes`); the prompt readers through `promptFilterContext`'s set built with it; the window, search, the highlight's colour, the hover card's reason (which now says the side: "(no match)", "(judged not to fit)") and every combined mark (groups, holiday labels, each "+N", which read the occurrence's marks) follow. "Don't match" means the events in the filter's scope (Applies to: everything, a folder's calendars, one calendar) that it does not match: for a global filter that is nearly everything, weather, holidays and trips included (they are events; people's away times are not filtered), and the card says so in a second sentence by scope. Defaults: a new AI prompt filter is `nonmatch` (its description says what to keep, as its form always asked), a keyword or regex filter `match`; changing the type or the action never moves it. Stored data: a new column with the default `match`, and migration 080 fills `nonmatch` on every existing prompt filter from its own type, because that is what every prompt filter did before 1.5.8 (hide, dim and highlight alike took the events judged not to fit). That is filling a new column from the row's own state, a correction, not a rewrite of a stored form, and no format changes: the verdicts stay `pass` and `fail`. An upgrade changes no filter's effect, including a prompt dim or highlight filter on a server that ran the superseded reading for a day: the fill looks only at the type, so it is `nonmatch` again, as it was before that day. Else: the superseded entry above (each action fixed to one side), or a side only for prompt filters; one field for every kind was chosen so "consistent" means every filter says which. The regex null was not in the brief: without it a "hide what doesn't match" regex filter would hide every event the regex gave up on, the opposite of the fail-open the regex limits promise. Cost: a deleted prompt filter recorded in undo before the upgrade and restored after it comes back with the column's default, `match` (the journal's row has no side); a rare sequence, left as is. Verified 2026-11-16: `server/tests/db/filter-sides.test.php` (each of the six for a keyword, a regex and a prompt filter, with an event that matches, one that does not and for prompts one not judged, in the window and in search, with the colour and the reason; the defaults; patching; refusing another value; the migration's fill on rows made without the column; with the side ignored it fails 63 checks), `server/tests/run.php` "ta" checks (the function, a regex that gives up), `web/tests/filter-words.mjs` (reads `takesAction`, `APPLIES_TO` and `defaultAppliesTo` from the PHP and pins the six sentences for each kind), `web/tests/filter-form.mjs` (the switch's defaults by kind, that it stays where chosen, what is saved).
- **What a stored link between two copies is for, and so which are stored** (1.5.8, [#317](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/317)). Status: choice. A linked row in `event_duplicates` is read by the views and search (`Duplicates::linkedFor`, any date a person can go to; the app collapses a group among the calendars shown, `ui/duplicates.js`), by reminders (`silenced`; a hidden calendar still reminds) and by the app badge. So every linked pair is stored, whatever its date and whether either calendar is hidden: a pair years back shows twice in month view and search without its row, and a pair with a hidden side keeps one reminder and collapses the moment the calendar is shown again, with no wait for a scan. Considered: storing only pairs in the scan's 14-days-back to 400-ahead window (old months and search would show doubles), and skipping hidden calendars (two reminders while hidden, doubles for up to a pass after showing). Chosen therefore: no cap on stored links. The 1.5.7 cap (`DUPLICATE_LINKED_EDGES`, 5,000, counting every link the account ever kept) is gone; it stopped linking for good once one calendar of a few thousand events was connected beside its own copies, showed the rest twice, refused every new duplicate on the account, and recorded the same failure every pass. Stored links are bounded by how they are made: a same-UID group is a star (one row per extra copy, and a calendar holds a UID once), a title link needs the owner's own calendars on both sides and is made at most once per event a pass. What stays bounded is work: one slice's rows, comparisons per event and in all, pairs and seconds (the next slice goes on from the cursor, and a full slice is not reported), one read of links (`DUPLICATE_LINK_READ_EDGES`, the read half of the old name), and the Review queue (500 open, 3 per event). Existing rows: nothing is removed, since the rule stores everything the old one did; an install with the old cap full links the rest on its next pass (about 500 events a minute) and its health line clears with one Activity line. No migration. What a pass really leaves out (an event compared with only the first N of a busy day's neighbours; a possible duplicate not added because Review is full) is said once per pass in words (`Duplicates::shortfallWords`: the calendars, about how many events, what shows, what to do), as a condition (`SystemHealth::recordCondition`): the same words while it is recorded change nothing, new words record it again, and it clears with one line; it is journaled at once and never emails by itself. Verified: `tests/db/duplicates-pass.test.php` (two calendars with 40 same-UID events over fourteen years, slices of 8 rows and 6 pairs: every pair linked, a hidden side too, the pass in a bounded number of jobs, no line; from the old cap full and its line failing, one pass recovers; a busy day left out is recorded once over three passes and clears), which fails with the cap put back at 10 (30 of 40 pairs shown twice, the new pair unlinked); the unit suite's words and sums.
- **An event is compared with its nearest neighbours first** (1.5.8, #317, approved by the owner 2026-11-15). Status: choice. On a busy stretch one event is compared with at most `DUPLICATE_CANDIDATES_PER_EVENT` others (100) within 38 hours either way; they were the earliest in that window, so a twin at the same moment could fall outside the cap behind earlier events and show twice. Chosen: the nearest by distance from the event's start, then id (`Duplicates::nearest`), so the cap drops the farthest. Each side of the start is read on the `(user_id, start_utc)` index in its own order, nearest first, at most the cap plus one each, and merged in PHP; ordering by the distance itself could not use the index. EXPLAIN on the test database at 4,000 events: a range on `idx_events_user_start` for each side, reading the window's rows (39), with a filesort over those rows only (the join to calendars), so the cost is bounded by the window, not the account. The 38 hours: two events can be one while a timed event's own-clock date is an all-day day's date, and an all-day day is stored at its UTC midnight, so a timed twin can start from 14 hours before it (UTC+14) to 36 hours after (UTC-12, late evening); two timed ones are within 30 minutes. So the window could be 14 hours before to 36 after for an all-day event, 36 before to 14 after a timed one's start against all-day days, not 38 both ways; proposed, not changed: with nearest-first ordering it changes only which far rows are read, never which twin is found. The crowded notice stays for when even the nearest 100 are not all. Verified: `duplicates-pass.test.php`, four events earlier the same day and the same event on two of your calendars at one moment, compared with three: linked now, missed by the earliest-first query.
- **Review previews a description as `Sanitize::toText` reads it** (#313, 1.5.8). Status: choice.
