The preview of an invitation's description went through a stripping of its own (tags out, entities decoded, every run of whitespace one space), so two paragraphs read as one run of words; now it is `Sanitize::toText`, the one function every other plain-text reading of a description uses (reminders, the ICS export, the Google conflict words), with a run of spaces within a line made one, as the page shows it; the page shows the preview's line breaks (`white-space: pre-line`). The comparison takes the same text, so a change of markup alone is still no change. Verified by a unit test with two paragraphs and a list, which failed on the old stripping.

## Sync and Storage

- **All-day series are stored in UTC** (since 0.9.16). Status: choice. Their local day is the UTC date, and a timed series made all-day takes the dates of its days in its old zone. Settled 0.9.16.
- **Too many wrong CalDAV passwords.** Status: choice. A 429, not a 401, so the client waits rather than deciding its password is wrong. Settled before 1.0.
- **Google access: detect and inform.** Status: choice. Chosen (the owner, 2026-11-13; model [google-access-detect-and-inform.md](google-access-detect-and-inform.md)): a change is sent to a Google calendar only when its own first request was an events list whose `accessRole` is `writer` or `owner`, decided by one function (`GoogleWriter::admit`) at every first read: a write's condition (`GoogleSeries::condition`), a caller's read (`readNow`, which an answer to an invitation and an undo now read through), a change to all events, Finish and Put back. A single event's first read became the same list by iCalUID a series already had (proven equal to a GET, etag included, on real Google: stage listread), so ordinary edits send no extra request; a list answered 404 is the calendar no longer shown, never an event deleted. The poll stores the level of its own list answers (its read of the account's calendar list goes) after the account's check under its lock and only while neither the stored level nor the sync position moved during its fetch; a change's level is stored once its transaction is over, after the same check. On a move between tiers (editable, read-only, not shown), Activity gets one line and Review one dismissible item; a half-done change stays listed with its sentence and no action. One small list is asked only after a refused insert and when a change or an undo is attempted on a calendar stored below writer (the recheck the owner asked for: "that change should trigger a recheck"). Else: the stopped build's confirming request after every refusal and "Leave as Google has it" (dropped by the owner: "We do not need to try to handle every scenario"), or the calendar list as the source (a shared calendar can be missing from it). Cost: a change attempted in the seconds after edit access is given back may still be refused by Google (probe row 6e), and gets "try again in a moment"; access lost between a change's list and its write (milliseconds) is answered as today; an answer to an invitation on a calendar the account can only view is refused like any change; a calendar whose level is busy times only is still polled, and Google's busy-only listing carries no titles, which that poll applies (found, not fixed here; reported for the owner's decision). Verified 2026-11-13: `server/tests/db/google-access.test.php`, every expectation from the test's own record of the level it set the stand-in to (a change minutes after access was lowered, to view only, busy times only and not shown, refused on its first read with nothing sent and nothing lost, a delete not taken for already deleted; access given back, the change through without a poll and the item closed; the poll's level after a 410, the item dismissed and coming back only when the tier moves; an older poll answer under a newer change's read; the compromise reset; a half-done change with no action; the request count of ordinary edits equal to the fix branch's), each of eleven injected damages caught; the stand-in's answers by level checked row by row against the access probe in `tools/google-probe/test.php`, each check failing on the stand-in before. Settled 2026-11-13.
- **A Google listing at busy times only is not applied.** Status: choice (the owner, 2026-11-13). A listing Google gives below reader shows only times (no title in a read, rows 6a, 6c, 6g of the access probe; no description or private properties in a list, row 4), so nothing of it is applied to what Better-Cal holds: one rule, `GoogleWriter::shows`, asked by the poll (`GoogleSync`) and by a change's read-backs (`GoogleSeries::store`). The level and the poll's sync position are stored; the poll fails with "Google now shows this account only busy times for 'X', so its events here stay as they were and don't update, and changes and undo are unavailable. If you have access, restore full details and editing at Google."; the same sentence is the Activity line and the Review item, and the settings line says it too. Busy times only is its own state for what is said (a move from view only gets its line and item). When access returns, the stored token answers 410 (row 4d, stage restored) and the next poll reads the calendar whole and catches up. Else: apply it as before (which replaced every title here with "(No title)"), or stop polling (no catch-up path without a new mechanism). Cost: while a calendar is at busy times only, nothing at Google reaches it, deletions included. `GoogleWriter::apply` needs no check: it stores a write's own answer, which Google gives only at writer or owner. Verified 2026-11-13: `server/tests/db/google-access.test.php`, "busy times only" (two polls, every column of every row as held before, tags kept; given back, the next poll catches up a title changed at Google meanwhile) and "busy times only reached during a change" (read-backs not stored); injected damage caught: the poll applying the listing, the read-backs stored, the level not stored, the calendar not caught up.
- **Every Review notice of a state can be dismissed, by one rule.** Status: choice (the owner, 2026-11-13: "All review items must be dismissible. The dismiss is not, I've dealt with it necessarily. It's, I don't care about it or I acknowledge this."). One row per subject and state (`ReviewQueue::holdState`, `endState`, `reopenState`; one dismissal, `dismissNotice`): dismissed, a notice stays dismissed while its state stays the same; it comes back when the state changes, or when an action the person takes is refused because of that state; dismissing changes nothing but the item. Brought onto it: `google_empty` (a dismissed one used to come back on the next empty poll), `google_read_only` and `google_not_shown` (now also reopened by a refused change), and `subscription` (dismissible for the first time; reopened by a refused refresh). The per-kind dismissals of `mail_limit`, `model_limit`, `mail_error` and `google_differs` are now the one `dismissNotice`; those four report events, not states, and each new event is a new notice as before. Not brought: `invite_new`, `invite_change` (their dismiss is a decision with an Activity entry and refuses later mail of the series), `rsvp`, `proposal`, `duplicate` (decisions with their own stores), and, at first, `google_half` (a half-done change stops the poll updating its series and refuses every change to it, so a dismissed item would hide a series silently not updating). The owner then decided (2026-11-13): `google_half` goes on the same rule, its state the pending row, and the state a dismiss hides is said where the person meets it: every view of the event's details (the popover and the sheet, any day of the series, phone and desktop) carries one small line, shown whether or not the item was dismissed, "A change to this event stopped part way on <date>. It isn't updating from Google and takes no other change until that change is finished or put back.", or, when neither Finish nor Put back can run, the item's own sentence in place of the second part, with a quiet "Go to Review" (or "Show in Review", which opens a dismissed item again). A change, undo or answer refused because of it reopens the item (`GoogleSeries::refusePending`); Finish or Put back completing it ends it; Dismiss is the one action when neither can run. Verified 2026-11-13: `server/tests/db/review-dismiss-half.test.php` (dismissed stays out; the pending row and every column of every row of the series equal before and after the dismiss; the event's line still there; a refused change reopens; Finish and Put back end it; Dismiss alone and the item's sentence on the line when the calendar is read-only), each of three injected damages caught (a dismiss clearing the pending row, no reopen on a refusal, the line missing after a dismiss), and `web/tests/static.mjs` for the line's words, its control and its classes. Verified 2026-11-13: `server/tests/db/review-dismiss.test.php` (a paused feed, a calendar listing nothing, a calendar read-only at Google), each of four injected damages caught (a dismissed notice reopened by the next hold, no reopen on a refusal, a dismissed feed still listed, a changed state not reopening).
- **After the independent read of the access build: four rules.** Status: choice (the coordinator's brief of 2026-11-13, from the read's findings; the model's section 8). A: every refusal for access stored below writer asks Google first, Finish and Put back included, and a preview exactly where its commit would refuse (the second read: an answer or mark is never refused, so its preview asks nothing; the model's section 9), and Check again lets the person ask (the web app shows no change control on a calendar it can't change, so no attempt could ask). B: a read below reader is never kept, the record a stopped change keeps for Put back included. C: a level learned from unknown is a move like any other, said without "now" or "again". D: a sentence follows the state, not the path: a change that had sent part of itself never says "nothing was changed"; "no longer" and "restore" only where the calendar could be changed before; a write refused 403, 404, 410 or 412 asks the small list before it is read as Google refusing, deleted or changed (one list, paid only on such a refusal; the second live round, 2026-11-14, found every DELETE on a calendar no longer shared answered 404, the events still there). Else: the build's first readings, each faulted by the read with a probe. Cost: one small list on each such refusal; Check again's one list. A calendar deleted by its owner answers as one no longer shared (round 2, stage deleted: every list 404, its delta too), so both are "no longer shown"; a 410 to a list sent with no sync token was never seen, so no branch depends on it (it stores nothing and is a failure). Known, not built: a change's older level stored after a newer poll's (seconds, corrected by the next poll or attempt); in the seconds after access is given back Google answers some lists 404 and inserts 403 while others answer writer (round 2), so a change can be refused or the calendar said no longer shown for those seconds, corrected the same way. Verified 2026-11-13 and 14: the read's probes, adopted as `server/tests/db/access-read-*` (each of their fifteen intended failures now passing), `google-access.test.php`, and the damages listed in the hand-back.
- **The branch's last fix round, after the second independent read.** Status: choice (the model's section 9). A preview asks Google exactly where its commit would refuse for access (`Events::googleKeys` the one reading). A refusal built after the small list is a refusal: nothing journaled as sent, nothing listed half done; an unreadable small list refuses as unconfirmed, never "already removed". A dismiss holds health mail only for the failure its notice reports while that state holds. Review's history lists a dismissed notice while its state holds, with Show again, for every notice kind. A level learned from unknown opens no Review item (nothing about before survives the reset). Else: the earlier readings, each faulted with a probe (`server/tests/db/access-read-2-*`). Cost: none in requests. Known, open: N2, the seconds after access returns, stated with the live case that would settle it. Verified 2026-11-14: the probes, and the injected damages listed in the hand-back. Amended 2026-11-14 after the outside review of the candidate: `previewPatch` still refused every change on a calendar below writer, what is held here alone included, where `patchLocked` refuses only on `googleKeys`; the preview now refuses on the same condition (#277), with tags and a reminder for all events and from a day on in `access-read-2-previews`.
- **Re-attaching a Google calendar stores its level through the one store.** Status: choice. `Calendars::reattachGoogle` sets the account and hands the level from the account's calendar list to `GoogleWriter::settleAccess`, so a calendar re-attached below the level it had gets its Activity line and Review item as a poll would give them; before, it wrote the column directly. Verified 2026-11-13: `google-access.test.php`, "re-attach", and the damage of writing the column directly caught.
- **A refused change touches nothing, a Google calendar included.** Status: choice. The trip rule runs before any outward call. Verified: the database suite asserts no call on a refusal. Settled 2026-11-04.
- **Exceptions are carried, not trusted.** Status: choice. Chosen: a series plan on a Google calendar is sent as the master, then the series is read back and every day the read-back doesn't hold as the plan has it is sent on its instance id (an edited day whole with its own fields, a skipped day as a cancelled instance, a record the plan has no exception for put back to the series' values); what Google holds after the last read-back is what the rows here store, and a difference is said in Activity and Review, never papered over. Else: predict from the probe's facts which master changes drop or overwrite exceptions and send only those (fewer requests, and wrong the day Google behaves otherwise, as 3.5 and 3.7 already differ from 3.4 and 3.6); or trust the plan and write the rows from it (what 1.5.0 to 1.5.3 avoided by sending Google nothing it might reset). Why: Google decides what becomes of its exception instances, and the probe found the rule has cases (a start moved onto another day of the same pattern keeps them reset, an added weekday keeps them but drops the cancelled one); a step that follows from a read-back needs no case list, and the read-back is the only acceptance there is. Cost: one list request after the master and one at the end, and one request per day carried, which the stand-in and the database suite count. Verified 2026-11-07: `server/tests/db/google-series.test.php` (one scenario per contract row, a 412 mid-way with Finish and Put back, a lost insert answer) against the probe's stand-in Google, and the edit harness's `--google` mode and `google-split` fixed case (`8-google`, `e-google`: the stand-in holds each series as the rows here hold it, a poll changes nothing). Where: `GoogleSeries`, `SeriesPlans::googlePatchAllPlanned`, `SeriesPlans::googleSplitPlanned`, `GoogleSync::toParsed` (a dropped record is a cancelled item with no `originalStartTime`, never a cancelled master). Settled 2026-11-07 for 1.5.4.
- **Activity records what the account holds, not how it is shown.** Status: choice. Chosen: a calendar's `visible` flag is a display preference stored on the server, like the settings, and is silent with them: showing or hiding calendars, one by its own patch or many in one request (`PATCH /calendars`), writes no Activity entry, undo never puts the flag back, and the flag never makes an older undo stale. A patch that changes other fields too records those, with the flag left out. The reminder address stays the one journaled setting, since it changes where mail goes. The sidebar's Show all is the way back; no toast offers an undo. Else (was, 1.5.1 for one day): a batch of shown and hidden calendars recorded one entry, undoable as one, with a line naming what was done and the folder acted on; and before it, each checkbox click on a calendar was an entry of its own. Why: an undo stack is for changes to what you have; a view preference journaled beside them crowds Activity with entries nobody needs to undo, and an undo of one of them flips what is shown under later work. Verified 2026-11-06: `server/tests/db/calendar-visibility.test.php` (a batch and a single patch write no entry and the flags change; a rename with a hide records the rename alone and its undo leaves the calendar hidden), and the completeness test, which counts the flag as no change to the journal. Where: `Undo::PREFERENCES`, `Calendars::patch`, `Calendars::setVisibility`. Settled 2026-11-06.
- **The events window is validated by its change cursor.** Status: choice. Chosen: `GET /events` carries `ETag: W/"<hash>"` of the account, the cursor's events part and the code version, with `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`; a request whose `If-None-Match` names it is answered 304 with no body before any event row is read. The validator is sound on one rule, applied everywhere: a write that changes what the window returns moves the cursor (a calendar's synctoken for an event row from any path, `users.change_seq` for every API write and for background work outside a calendar's data: filter verdicts, duplicate links, and since 2026-11-07 the ranking's scores and the geocode sweep's coordinates, which moved nothing before). The one clock-dependent field, `isNew`, is folded into the cursor as the count of the account's events inside their first day, so a mark ending moves the cursor and a tab refetches then; no `Vary`, since the account is in the tag. The client sends the tag of a stretch it holds whole under one tag and keeps the stretch on a 304, never an empty one; the browser revalidates a reloaded page's month requests itself. Else: fold the hour into the tag (a mark up to an hour late to end, a full refetch hourly for nothing); or leave the mark out of the validator (a reload keeps a mark that has ended). Why: a tag that names the window exactly makes the 304 safe for any client, the browser's own cache included, and the cursor was already the one name for the account's window state. Verified 2026-11-07: `server/tests/db/window-tag.test.php` (304 to the tag; 200 with a new tag after an API edit, an undo, a filter, a calendar hidden, a feed row landing, the ranking scoring, a new mark ending), `server/tests/run.php` (the cursor's new-mark part, the tag's inputs, a compressing server's suffix), the smoke test "etag:" (the client keeps its window on a 304 and asks plainly for a stretch held in part). Where: `Domain\ChangeCursor::windowTag`, `Controllers\EventsController::window`, `web/src/app/api.js` (`fetchRange`), `store.js` (`heldTag`, `keepWindow`), [#16](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/16). Decided and built 2026-11-07.

- **Floating times are stored as their wall clock in the event's own zone** (1.5.4, [#167](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/167)). Status: choice. Chosen: an `events.floating` flag, with `start_utc` and `end_utc` the wall clock read in the row's `tzid` (the Home zone when the event was made), so every query, index, key and the series model read a floating row as an ordinary row in that zone; one function (`Domain\Floating`) moves a floating row's instants to a viewer's clock and back, and the window, the single record, the reminder scan and every request's instants go through it. The viewer is the device a request names (`X-Device-Tz`), else Home; the reminder scan's viewer is where the device was last seen. Else: keys written as wall clocks as if UTC (the series model's first sketch), which no clock change can touch but which every existing reader would read as an instant, or a separate wall-time column, which no index or query reads. A generated occurrence shows at the clock the rule generated it from (the expander's wall clock, before its zone reads it), read on the viewer's clock by the one wall-clock rule; its key stays the instant the own zone gives that clock. So a wall clock the Home zone skips (2:30 on its spring change day) is 2:30 for a viewer whose zone has it and 3:30 only where the viewer's zone skips it too, and on the Home clock 3:30 as before (fixed after 1.5.4, which showed 3:30 on every clock). A floating series is expanded over the viewer's window moved to its own clock, not a day's widening clipped after, so a minutely series isn't cut short before the window; search reads the same instants before it filters and limits. What it costs: an event made floating from another zone keeps that zone as its frame, and a series under a day (hourly, minutely, secondly) is generated and keyed in the own zone, so that zone's clock changes reach every viewer. On its spring change day the clocks its skipped hour lands on an instant another clock has (an hourly 02:30 and 03:30, a minutely 02:xx and 03:xx) are one occurrence everywhere, shown at the later clock, so a viewer whose zone has that hour sees it empty; minutely and secondly rules step elapsed time there, so one whose interval does not divide the change (every 12 hours as 720 minutes, every 90 minutes) keeps the moved clock after it, and the hour the zone repeats holds its minutes twice. The key cannot carry two clocks on one instant: it is that instant at the second (`recurrence_instance_utc`, skips, instance ids, RECURRENCE-ID out), and a secondly rule already holds every second. The fix is a release of its own: a floating series generated and keyed by its nominal wall clock, as RFC 5545 keys a floating RECURRENCE-ID, read the same way by the expander, the model and the harness's evaluator, with a one-off rekeying of existing edited days and skips. Pinned by `server/tests/floating.php`, "floating limit:". Verified: `server/tests/floating.php`, `server/tests/db/floating.test.php`, and the edit harness with a share of its series floating. Settled 2026-11-07; the skipped clock and the window settled 2026-11-08.
- **A floating event on Google is pinned there, floating here.** Status: workaround. Google has no floating times, so a floating event is sent in its own zone (the Home zone) and keeps its flag here; a time change made at Google is read back as a new wall clock in the zone it came with. Else: keep floating events off Google calendars, which would make a routine on a Google calendar impossible. Settled 2026-11-07.
- **Invitations and events with guests never float.** Status: choice. Everyone invited sees them at the same moment, so the flag is refused with that reason (`floating_refused`). An all-day event is a date and never floats either. Settled 2026-11-07.
- **A calendar's "New timed events keep their clock time in any zone" is a preset, not a property.** Status: choice. It sets the flag on events created in it (the editor, quick add, a file imported into it); the flag on each event is what counts, so turning the setting off changes no event. Settled 2026-11-07.

- **A change made here alone to a Google event undoes like any local change.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. A trip link, Planned, tags, people or reminders on a Google event keep their snapshots and undo here, sending Google nothing, since Google never sees them; an entry is log-only for what it changed (a row or column Google holds that the entry cannot put back), never for the calendar it is on; an edit that changes both Google and local fields is one entry whose undo sends the Google part back and restores the local part. Else: every entry on a Google calendar log-only, as 1.5.0 to 1.5.4 had it. Verified: the database suite's google-local-undo scenarios. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **The undo of a Google edit sends back only the fields that edit changed.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Each changed field goes back at its before value over the event as it stands, a field the edit added is cleared, and a field a later entry changed again is stale as on a local calendar; redo carries the same fields. A day's before value is the inverse of the edit's delta, never a literal old string: a field the day owned before goes back at its value then, and one it did not own (it followed its series) goes back to following it, at the series' value there now, so a day given its own title and undone after a rename for all events shows the new name. A row an undo or redo brings back whole is composed as the sync composes any row, never restored from its snapshot alone: what it held here alone (tags, people, reminders, an icon, coordinates) over the day as it stands now, the row at that day now (a title Google gave it stays its own) or else the series' occurrence there (the series' current notes); Google's answer to the undo, when it sends anything, is stored on top. Where a row holds that day now (a poll made one since), the row brought back is composed onto it and keeps its id, its tags and people with it, for a series undo and a delete's undo too; nothing is ever written over another row's day, which before rewrote the polled row's id (orphaning what names it without a key) or, with a tag on it, was refused as gone since (B2). A place's text and the coordinates picked for it are one value: an undo or redo that puts back coordinates puts back their text in the same write, so Google's answer stored after it never sees the text change and drops them (B1). The composition has one order (R2): the day's Google-held part as it stands (the row at that day now, its columns and the fields of its own Google holds, else the series' occurrence), then the restored row's own part applied last and whole, place text and coordinates together; a series undo landing on a row a poll made keeps the place it brings back. A deleted day of a series brought back is sent as the series' occurrence there now with the removed row's own part over it, never with the values Google kept on the cancelled instance (seed 7 case 35). Removed (2026-11-09): the restore of a row holding only local data from its snapshot with no look at the current series, which left a redone tag showing the series' old notes and a redone icon hiding a title Google gave the day (the review of 9de4e3dc..8e568077, N3; seeds 419 case 578, 11 case 122 and 17 case 283). Else: send the whole historical before body, which overwrote a later edit of another field (the 1.5.5 review's R1). Verified: google-local-undo, the review's probe, google-series-undo's scenarios "a day's own title undone after a rename for all events" and "a tag on a day redone after its undo and a notes change", and the interleaving fixed cases `a-redo-of-a-tagged-day-after-the-series-notes-changed`, `a-redo-of-an-icon-on-a-day-renamed-at-google-during-a-rename` and `an-undo-of-a-day-title-after-a-rename-inherits-the-new-title` (x-undo-fields, x-follow, x-agree); google-series-undo's "a place and its coordinates go back as one" and "a redo bringing a day back where a poll made a row since". Settled 2026-11-08; the composition settled 2026-11-09.
- **The bare Undo steps one change further back on a Google calendar as it does locally.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. An undo's own entry (`details.google.undoes`) is passed over by `POST /undo`; redo is that entry undone by its id, and a redo's entry is a change of its own: the bare Undo after an edit, its undo and a redo takes the redo back, as a person expects of an undo stack, never the series' creation behind it (settled 2026-11-09 when the harness asked). An entry is judged by what it changed, never by who wrote it (2026-11-09): a line that changed no row (log-only, touching nothing, such as the line a Put back of a half-done undo writes) is passed over by the bare Undo and makes nothing stale; a log-only entry that changed rows (a change sent to Google with no way back) is still the answer, refused, with nothing older undone instead. Verified: undo's "undo latest" and google-local-undo's log-only cases (their entries now name the rows they changed, as every real writer's do), google-series-undo's Put back case (the bare Undo then undoes the change). Else: the bare call redoing after a Google undo, one rule for local and another for Google. Verified: google-local-undo. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **A local-only "this event" edit of an ordinary day sends Google nothing.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. The day's row is made here under its instance id, as the sync would make it, and survives polls while the series still has the day; the same rule keeps an edited day whose own fields are all held here (tags, people, reminders, an answer) when a feed, Google or ICS, drops its record of that day. Such a row is a row of its series, under a whole read and a delta read alike: after the poll it exists only while the series, as stored then, has its key (the expander's answer, the question a "this event" edit asks of instanceStart; a key the series' clock or zone moved goes to the one occurrence on its day), and every field it doesn't own takes the series' value (title, place, notes, link, status, the time from its key, all-day, zone, guests). A day the series no longer has is removed with its tags, people and reminders, named in the poll's Activity entry. Else: write an exception equal to its series at Google for no reason, and lose the tags with the record; or keep the row whenever the series is seen, which left it showing after the series ended before it and kept a renamed series' old title (review N3). Verified: google-local-undo (survives a delta poll and a whole read), google-local-follow (COUNT, UNTIL, a new rule, a new clock time, a rename, place and notes, each under both reads; an ICS feed shortened and renamed). A day's identity at Google is its series and its occurrence, so the instance id a day's row carries is derived from the occurrence it stands at and its series' id (`Feeds::googleIdAt`) wherever either changes, a times or zone change here, a poll, an undo placing it, a "this and following" moving it to the new series, never the id of the occurrence or series it left; and an undo or redo acts on that day alone: `GoogleUndo::dayAt` answers the day's id and its occurrence together, so the request sent to Google, the skip stored here and the ground the undo's own entry records all name the occurrence the day stands at now, never the one the entry recorded (external review R2: a redo of a deleted ordinary day after the series' time changed deleted the right instance at Google but stored the skip and its ground at the old time, so the calendar still showed the day until the next poll; verified by undo-day-identity's moved-delete scenario and the review's moved-delete probe; settled 2026-11-09). A tag on an ordinary day, then the series' time changed, then the tag's undo refused with "no longer here" because the row kept its old instance id (seventh adversarial read P2-1; verified by google-series-undo's P2-1 scenario, the interleaving fixed cases `a-tag-on-a-dayless-day-then-a-time-change-for-all-events-then-its-undo` and `...-at-google-then-its-undo`, and the probe a10; for a split, google-series-undo's P2-1 split scenario; settled 2026-11-09). Settled 2026-11-08.
- **A Google request body is built from the allowed list.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Every body sent to Google, by a whole-series plan, a "this event" edit, an edit of an event of its own, "use series value" and an undo, is built from one list: the fields Google holds (`OwnFields::googleFields`, the complement of what is held here alone, plus the series' rule), each only where the change changes it (`GoogleWriter::patchOf`), and an edited day as the fields it owns that Google holds over the series' values (`GoogleSeries::exceptionOf`, a place showing the series' text being owned by its coordinates alone). A change whose body is empty after that filter is local: it sends nothing, journals as a local change and undoes as one, for one day or for all events of a series. Why: a day or series holding only local data is ordinary to every write and undo by construction, not by a check at each writer; before, tags for all events of a series whose rule had changed at Google since the last poll re-sent a skipped day, and the undo of a rename re-sent a day given only coordinates with the series' old title as an edited day. Else: keep sending the whole event or day and rely on each writer to skip local-only changes, which is how those leaks happened. An answer from a day on, and "this and following" of local-only fields, are still splits at Google (the split is what Google holds). Verified: the interleaving harness's x-local-silent (w-all, u-bare, f-lost, f-before, f-fatal) and x-agree on u-entry, the database suite. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Put back is all or nothing.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Every step of a half-done Google series change is checked first (an acknowledged step against what it left, one whose answer was lost against what it should have left, both by the digest since 2026-11-09: the entry "Put back judges an answered step by its content" below); if any series changed at Google since, nothing is sent, nothing changes here, and the answer names each series and points to Finish. Else: reverse the series that pass and leave the rest, which gives two overlapping series after a split whose new series was edited elsewhere. Finish writes the local part of a mixed change from the plan kept with the pending row (migration 074) and reports it when it cannot. "What it should have left" is one digest (the next entry), so a skipped day brought back or an edited day reset elsewhere after a lost rename refuses Put back, which it didn't while the check walked only the records there now and only skips added. Verified: google-series-recovery, the review's probes, the recovery sweep's sequences. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **Finish applies the change's deltas over what holds now.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; narrowed 2026-11-14 by "Finish sets the plan's days where it can't tell its own change from another" (below): it holds for the series' own fields, and for the days while the series' own write has not reached Google; once that write has landed the days are the plan's, and what Google's value "explains" is never a prediction of Google's own effect. A delta is what the change changed, from the series as it found it to the plan: each field of the master, each edited day's own fields, the skipped days, and the fields of its part here. Finish reads Google and the rows as they stand, sets each delta where Google's value is one the change explains (as it was, as the change's own master write leaves it by Google's documented effect on the days, or as the plan has it, a step that landed), and leaves every field and day the change did not change exactly as it finds them; a day or field changed since in what the change changes (a day deleted since, the same field or a tag list changed since) is kept and named in the answer, the way an undo keeps later changes. A record at the series' own values is an ordinary day to every send and comparison, never written or reported, a normal rename included; a day with only local data is an ordinary day to every Google write and every undo; a day a recovery brings back takes its series' columns (the trip and clock-time flags) from the series as it is now. Else: re-run the stored targets and reset every day that differs from them, which overwrote a note added to an ordinary day, a place changed on an edited day, un-cancelled a day deleted meanwhile and replaced a later tag list without a word. Why now: an adversarial sweep of recovery and undo found nine failures, each a neighbour of a fix made for the case found before it; the rule replaces those fixes. Verified: google-series-recovery (the sweep's sequences F1 to F9, its controls), the sweep's probes, the reviews' probes, the Google harness (seeds 7 and 9, the fixed cases, the ten sequences). Settled 2026-11-08.
- **A Google series' state is one digest of every field.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. "Nothing changed since", "landed", "the same event" and "what to send" are all read from one state: every field of the master's resource and of each record's, less what Google rewrites on its own (etag, updated, created, sequence, its page link, the ids and keys, creator and organizer stamps, the call link it derives; listed in `GoogleSeries::VOLATILE`), the fields a column holds compared as normalized and the rest as Google gives them, with a value Google shows for a field never set counted as absent, plus the set of cancelled days. A step whose answer may be lost journals the digest it should leave, computed from the series as read just before it plus its delta (a master write's from a read-back taken before it, one more request per series change). Else: compare the fields the writer sends, which let Put back cancel an inserted series whose guests, colour, busy flag, visibility, reminders or guest permissions were changed elsewhere since. Verified: google-series-recovery (F4 for each field), the sweep's probes. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **A feed's change to a day keeps what is held here.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. One rule for every edited day of a series a feed (Google or ICS) still has, whoever made its row (`created_via` is provenance only): while the feed's record for the day holds something of its own (a field the feed carries: title, place text, notes, link, status, date, time, length, all-day; or its own guest list), the record decides those fields. When the record changes to hold nothing of its own, goes, or never existed, the row keeps exactly what is held here alone (tags, people, reminders, an answer, an icon, coordinates picked for the place text it shows, and what refers to it: trip links, duplicate decisions, plugin data) and takes the series' values for everything else, its key moving to its image. A row left holding nothing is an ordinary occurrence again (no row); a day the series no longer has goes, named in Activity with what it held; a row whose image another row already holds (the feed's record of that day, or another row moving there) gives that row its tags and people (unioned), its reminders, answer and icon where the holder owns none, its coordinates where the holder shows the same place text, and what refers to it, then goes, named as kept on that day's row. Coordinates stay part of the place field (series-model.md, Own Fields): a day given only coordinates owns its place, and since no feed carries coordinates, a place showing its series' text is held here alone; a new place text clears them, as on every feed row. Every series column (`Model::SERIES_COLUMNS`) follows the series. Else: keep or drop by who made the row and whether the feed still lists a record, which deleted a Google-held day with its tag unnamed on a clock, zone or rule change, kept a person-made ICS row's old exception values forever, and deleted a day moving onto a held key with a false reason (the sync sweep's findings 2 to 4); or keep coordinates out of the own list, which removed a day given only a picked place on the next poll (finding 1). Verified: google-local-follow (a Google-held tagged day under a clock move, a zone move, a COUNT, a skip and a split; the moved day onto a day Google edited in the same window; two tagged hours merged by an ICS rule change; an ICS person-made row after its feed exception comes and goes; coordinates through polls, a rename and a new place text; the clock-time flag; unchanged polls), the sweep's probes. Settled 2026-11-08.

- **What an edited day keeps is decided by one function, and a skip is Google's.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. When Google's record of a day stops holding anything of its own (it changes to hold nothing, goes, or never existed), one function (`Feeds::retained`) decides what the row keeps: the fields of `OwnFields::HELD_HERE` it owns, its picked coordinates while it shows the place text they were picked for, and what refers to it; nothing kept, it is an ordinary occurrence again. Every remover calls it: the sync's removal loop under a delta, a whole read and the 410 reset (all through `Feeds::sync`), and recovery for a series with changes not yet at Google (rewind's judgment of the rows a store made, and Finish's and Put back's stores, which go through the same sync). A skipped day is Google-held like a record: a clock, zone, rule or COUNT change at Google drops its cancelled record there, the next delta lists it as a dropped item, and the delta drops the skip here as a whole read would, unless the master's own EXDATE lines in the same list name the day. Else: a retention reading per caller (recovery had its own copy, `GoogleSeries::heldHere`), which could drift; or keep every stored skip a delta doesn't contradict, which left a skip here that Google no longer had until the next whole read, so a delta and a whole read disagreed. Why: local data on a remote event survives every remote change and recovery by one rule, and a half-done change finishes without losing either side's edits; DAVx5 and vdirsyncer would drop the data or stop and ask. Verified by the interleaving harness's x-agree (its fixed case `skip-then-the-rule-changed-at-google-then-a-delta-poll`) and x-dropped and x-alone over one starting row and one clock change per caller (`retention-clock-moved-at-google-then-a-delta-poll`, `retention-clock-moved-at-google-then-a-410`, `retention-clock-moved-at-google-then-a-whole-read`, `retention-clock-moved-answer-lost-then-finish`, `retention-clock-moved-answer-lost-then-put-back`), and `server/tests/run.php` ("merge delta: a skip Google dropped"). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Recovery removes only what it made.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Each store a half-done Google change runs (its failure's, Finish's, Put back's) records the rows it made in the pending row's journal (`made_json`, migration 075), and Finish removes exactly those before it writes the change's part here, but only while each holds nothing held here alone: a recorded row given a tag, a person, a reminder, an answer, an icon or a trip link since is no longer recovery's alone, and what it holds goes to the row Finish or Put back leaves for that day (where the change names one at its key), or the row is kept like any later local change; a day with no row left for it is put back as it was and named, never dropped silently. Any other row of the series that the snapshot lacks is a change made here since (a tag, a person or a reminder on an ordinary day makes that day's row), kept as it stands with what hangs off it; the plan is applied over it, its inherited fields following the series as written and its own fields staying. A day the changed series no longer has can't be placed, so Finish refuses the part here, keeps the row, and Review says why. Else: treat every row missing from the older snapshot as recovery's own, which deleted a day tagged while the change was pending and reported no difference (the follow-up review's N1); or reconcile by guessing ownership from values. A pending row made before migration 075 has no list and its Finish removes nothing the snapshot lacks. Since A6 (below) a write to the series while its change is not yet at Google is refused, so such rows come from a pending row made before that or data reaching a row by a path that is not a write to the series; the tests make them with the pending row out of the gate's sight after checking the refusal. Verified: google-series-recovery (a tag, a person and a reminder added while pending; rows a store made removed at each failure point; a store-made row tagged while pending keeps the tag on its day through Finish and Put back), the review's probe. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **"Nothing changed since" includes the series' zone.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Put back's check of a step whose answer was lost compares everything that decides a series' occurrences: start and end, the all-day flag, the rule, the content, and the zone of a timed event as the string Google holds against the one sent. Two spellings of one zone are different strings and count as a change, which errs toward refusing; an all-day event has no zone at Google. The same comparison decides which days a read-back still needs sent, whether a lost step landed, and the differences Activity names, so a zone Google kept differently is named too. Else: compare instants only, which read a zone change that kept the first instant (Los Angeles to Phoenix) as unchanged and let Put back cancel a series edited since (the follow-up review's N2); or treat equivalent zone names as one, which needs an alias table nothing else uses. Verified: google-series-recovery (a zone-only change after a lost insert, master write and edited-day write), the review's probe. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **A link on a Google event is Google's `source`, both ways.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. A link changed or removed here is sent as `source` (cleared with null), read back on the next poll, and undoable through the changed-fields rule; a Google event's own page is never its link. Until 2026-11-08 link edits on Google events were dropped without a word. Verified: google-local-undo. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **An unchanged feed is not parsed again.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. A poll sends the validators its last read kept (ETag, Last-Modified) and skips parse and sync on a 304 or on a body whose hash matches, with a full read once a day and whenever the address, the Home zone or the parser changes; validators are kept per address and never sent to another. Else: download, parse and diff every feed every interval. Verified: feed-reads scenarios. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **Outside connections do only what changes.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5, from the 2026-11-08 connections audit. A Google poll whose delta is empty stores its token and syncs nothing; one stored access token per account serves the worker and the browser while it is good; requests to one host reuse their connection and resolved address within a process, which is the ceiling for cron and per-request PHP; an idle mail check is login, STATUS and logout; the Fibery plugin asks each database for its count and newest modification date before reading, reads every slot when any moved and at least every 15 minutes; a reminder push gives up after 10 seconds. Left as they are, on purpose: batching a Google move's imports (it would give up the one-unknown-import recovery rule), the LLM transport (batched already, no burst anywhere), the place input re-asking after a backspace (the text changed). Settled 2026-11-08.
- **An undo is stale when a poll has since brought a remote change of the same field.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. An entry whose field a later poll changed (a rename made at Google and read here) is refused with `stale_undo`, as a later local change of that field would make it, and nothing is written; `force` overwrites deliberately, as on a local calendar. Else: put the old value back over the change made at Google. For an undo of a series change the same rule holds (2026-11-09): a later change of a field it changed, made here or polled, is stale by its ground as any other is (the entry "Staleness has one definition: grounds are fields"); a value Google changed while the change ran is what the change left, kept and named, never stale. A day of a series is its series' uid and occurrence, not a row id (2026-11-09, the second read R1): a later change on the row now at that day (a poll's, after the entry's row went) stales the entry as one on its own row would, each side's ground the fields that row owns where it was made or removed whole, so a poll changing the day's notes doesn't stale an entry about its place; an ordinary day deleted or brought back stands on its series' skips alone, so another day edited since stales nothing (seed 307 case 463). An entry about a day that is no longer an occurrence of the series it names (a later "this and following" took it into a series of its own, a rule or bound change dropped it) is stale, refused before anything else is checked, with words that say so (seed 419 case 852; the oracle expects `stale_undo` exactly when the entry's day is no longer an occurrence of the entry's series). Changed 2026-11-10: that refusal is `not_undoable` (`reason: day_gone`), not `stale_undo`. Force never passed it, and the app answers `stale_undo` with "Undo anyway", which then asked again after every yes; its words now name a rule change beside a split and a bound (the entry "An undo acts on a day only where the journal says exactly where it is"). The oracle expects a refusal with nothing written, whatever its code. Staleness is one model with two kinds of entry (2026-11-09, the fourth read, replacing the two readings recorded earlier that day and their planned unification): two entries conflict when their grounds meet, and what differs between kinds is the ground each records, never how two grounds are read. A snapshot undo (a calendar here) puts back every column it changed of every row, so its ground is every field of every row it restores, a series' times put back being every day of that series (its occurrences); that is why it stands on a later skip, day edit or rule change, and they on it (undoing a COUNT change after a time change, or the other way round, is stale there, seed 307 case 178). A Google series undo is planned as a forward change is and carries every day (the entry "An undo of a Google series change is planned as a forward change is"), so its ground is what it changed: a COUNT undone after a time change brings the days back at the new time, and a time change undone after a later edit of one day carries that day back with it. The ground of a day's row made or removed whole is the fields that row owns, on its own row as on any other row at that day, for every undo (the third read, F2): the edit that made a day's row is not staled by a later edit of another field of it, and its undo takes back only what it gave, the row staying, following its series in those fields, for what the later change gave it; a redo bringing a title back to a day a poll or a tag made a row at since goes through (seed 419 case 228, where the oracle expected stale, follows this reading). A skip is its own date's (F1): deleting a day, and a poll or a split skipping one, touch that date alone, two skips on different dates never stand on each other, and an undo puts a series' skips back by their delta over the list as it stands, so a date skipped since stays skipped; a day's own list is put back the same way. On a Google calendar, an undo of a day's change after a later times or zone change for the whole series acts on the day where that change carried it and goes through (F6's refusal, "the series' times changed since", removed 2026-11-09 by rule A), and one whose day left its series says that. Narrowed 2026-11-10: it goes through where the change recorded where the day went (its row still there, a recorded move, a delete's own skip); a day with no row and no skip then is refused, never placed by its date (the entry "An undo acts on a day only where the journal says exactly where it is"). On a calendar here the same undo is refused as stale: a snapshot undo's ground is every field it restores, and a times change put back is every occurrence of the series, so it and a later skip, day edit or times change stand on each other, as above. A day's own list is put back by its delta over the list as it stands, by an undo's snapshot restore and by a series undo's composition alike, so tags a later change for all events gave a day stay as that change left them (seed 7 case 368). `force` overwrites deliberately, as on a local calendar (R3): a forced undo of a series change takes the change's reading for every field changed since, names what it overwrote in its entry, and still reads and checks the series before its first write. Verified: the interleaving harness's x-undo-fields, its fixed case `rename-then-a-rename-at-google-polled-then-undo`, google-series-undo's "a value Google changed while the change ran is kept and named". Settled 2026-11-08.
- **A removed day is named by its row and its date.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (planned 2026-11-08). Chosen: whenever a poll, Finish or Put back removes a row that held something held here alone (tags, people, reminders, an answer, an icon, coordinates, or something referring to it), the Activity entry's `details.removedDays` carry the row's id and its shown date with what it held and why it went, with words in its `summary` ("'Choir' on 2027-04-13 with its tags: its series no longer has that day"), and Finish and Put back also name it in their answer's `differences`; an event or a series deleted at Google names its own tags and people the same way, and its days theirs ("it is no longer in the calendar"), as does a day dropped by a new rule or COUNT and a day merged into the row its day already has. One naming function (`Feeds::droppedDay`, words by `Feeds::droppedWords`) serves the sync and recovery: a recovery's stores sync without an entry of their own and hand what they removed to Finish and Put back (`Feeds::takeDropped`). Else: name the day by its date alone, or count removals ("3 removed"), which leaves the person unable to tell which day's tags went; or name only the days of a series still there, which let a deleted series' tags and people go without a word. Verified: the interleaving harness's x-alone (a removal named by its date alone is reported as such), its fixed case `a-day-tagged-then-the-series-shortened-at-google`; `server/tests/db/google-poll-guards.test.php` (a series, its tagged day and a tagged event deleted at Google, each by id and date; a COUNT that drops the tagged day). Where: `Feeds::sync`, `Feeds::droppedDay`, `GoogleSeries::finish`, `GoogleSeries::putBack`. Settled 2026-11-09. **The date is the day the key names under its series (2026-11-10, the round's hunt).** Was: `droppedDay` read the key under the row's own all-day flag and zone, so a timed day of an all-day feed series was named a day early and a whole day of a 19:00 New York series a day late. Now: a key names a day only under the series it was written in (`Time::dayOf` with the series' form and zone), and every caller gives `droppedDay` that series (the series as it was before the poll; null for an event of its own, named by its own start). Other places that name a day from a key were read against the same rule: 23 sites, 14 follow, `droppedDay` fixed, and four left as they are and reported for their owners (`Undo::dayWords` reads UTC; `GoogleSeries::carryHeld` reads the row's own form; `GoogleSeries::placeKept` and `rebase` read a series' form and zone, but not always the series the key was written under), with four that depend on a reading of the rule (`workfolder/reviews/worker-155-rule1b.md`, round 2). Verified: `server/tests/db/activity-day-named.test.php` (both cases failed before), three pure checks ("day named") and the mutation `dropped-day-by-own-form`.
- **A series with changes not yet at Google takes no other change, and a poll leaves its rows.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. A series has changes not yet at Google while a pending row names it in status half or running (today a change a failure left half done; later, one queued while offline). Chosen: every write, undo and redo touching such a series (an edit of any scope, a tag or other data held here alone on one of its days, "use series value", a delete, an answer, an invitation reply, an undo or redo of any of its entries) is refused with 409 `google_pending`, the change's id in `pending` and words pointing to Review, before anything is read or sent and writing nothing here or at Google; Finish and Put back are the way out. One function answers "does this series have changes not yet at Google" (`GoogleSeries::pendingNames`, by the uids and Google ids the change names), asked by the Events write gate, the undo entry for every entry (`GoogleUndo::refuseWhilePending`, which replaced the undo of a series' own check after the fact), the series change entry (`GoogleSeries::apply`) and the poll. Every undo entry point reaches the one call (the bare Undo, a redo and an undo by entry all go through `Undo::undoById`), and the names it checks are those of the rows an entry's snapshots hold as they hold them as well as those rows now, so an entry whose day row a later step removed still names its series (2026-11-09, seed 11 case 42); the write gate decides by the calendar, never the row's source, since a series a split made here is one of Google's (seed 13). The poll's rule: while a series has changes not yet at Google, a poll neither removes, rewrites nor adds that series' rows and stores everything else; Finish and Put back each read what Google holds when they run, Finish rebases over it and Put back checks against it. Why: a second change on a series whose state at Google is unknown gives Finish rows it can't account for, and a later whole-series write after a trimmed split rewrote Google's bounds and skips from rows that were half of it; a poll storing the series meanwhile took rows from under the change. The tangible benefit over the reference engines: data held here alone on a remote event survives the change and its recovery, and a half-done change finishes without losing either side's edits, where DAVx5 and vdirsyncer would drop the data or stop and ask. Else: let writes through and reconcile at Finish (the follow-up review's N1), or let the poll store the series and have Finish guess which rows were its own. Verified by the interleaving harness's x-pending ("a write to a series whose change is half done", every write kind, undo and redo included) and x-write-asked on f-fatal, its fixed case `rename-answer-lost-then-a-tag-while-pending-then-finish`, and google-series-recovery's A6 scenario (each write kind refused with nothing sent or changed; a poll storing another event and leaving the series' rows; Finish then rebasing over the note made at Google meanwhile). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **An empty listing never deletes.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: a whole read of a Google calendar (the first, or the one after Google's 410) that lists no live event while the calendar holds rows here is not applied: the rows and the sync position stay as they were, the poll fails with that reason and a log line, Review lists the calendar (`google_empty`, one open notice per calendar), and the next poll reads it whole again; the next whole read that lists events applies and closes the notice. A delta that deletes everything still applies, so a calendar emptied at Google on purpose empties here. Else: apply it, which removes every row and everything held here alone on them when a listing comes back empty for a reason other than the calendar being empty (Google names permission changes among a 410's causes, and a calendar no longer shared with the account lists nothing). This is vdirsyncer's guard against an empty storage; the cost is that a calendar emptied at Google while its token had also expired stays as it was until it is removed and added again here. Verified: `server/tests/db/google-poll-guards.test.php` (a whole read and a 410 reset listing nothing: every row kept, the token as it was, one notice, a second empty read adds none, listed again closes it; a delta deleting every event applies); the interleaving harness's p-empty step once it lands. Where: `GoogleSync::poll`, `ReviewQueue::holdGoogleEmpty`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Google's refusals are read by Google's reason.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: `GoogleWriter` classifies an answer that is not a success by Google's reason as well as its status (`GoogleWriter::refusal`): a 429, or a 403 whose reason is a rate or quota limit (`rateLimitExceeded`, `userRateLimitExceeded`, `quotaExceeded`, the `usageLimits` domain, `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`), is "Google is busy": the same request goes again after 1 and then 2 seconds, and still busy it is 503 `google_busy` ("try again in a minute"); a 404 or 410 on the event is 409 `google_deleted` ("deleted at Google"), except where a caller reads it as gone on purpose (a DELETE already done, a day past the expansion cap); any other 403 keeps its words ("Google says this account may not edit that calendar"). Google's status rides along in every case, so each stays a refusal that writes nothing here. The same reading holds on every request the app sends, the read before a write (`GoogleSeries::condition`) included: a busy read is retried as a write is, a read that answers 404 or 410 or lists the event cancelled is `google_deleted` and nothing is sent, and only a read that found the event is compared with the rows here (a conflict is a difference in content, never a missing target). Else: every 403 as a permission refusal, which told a person over a rate limit that they may not edit a calendar they own, and every 404 as "Google rejected the change: HTTP 404". Verified: `server/tests/db/google-poll-guards.test.php` (a rate-limit 403 and a 429 retried once and landing, still busy after the retries, a 404, a permission 403, the reading of each answer; on the read before a write: a 404, a 410, an event deleted at Google, a busy read retried, a read still busy). Where: `GoogleWriter::call`, `GoogleWriter::refusal`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Removing a series already gone at Google succeeds.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. A delete of a series or event whose record Google no longer has (a 404 or 410 on the read before the write, or the record listed as cancelled) completes here: the remaining rows go and the Activity entry says Google had already removed it. Else: refuse with `google_deleted`, which would leave a row here for something nobody can see at Google and no way to clear it but a poll. The refusal stays for an edit of a deleted target, where there is something to lose. The interleaving harness accepts either reading for a removal until this entry; it now expects the removal. Amended 2026-11-09: a single event's delete and a one-day delete (an ordinary day or an edited day's row) read Google's answer by the same function as the series path (`GoogleWriter::gone`: a 404 or 410 on the read before the write or on the write, or the record listed cancelled) and complete here, their Activity line ending "Google had already removed it." (`Events::googleRemove`); before, they refused with `google_deleted`. Verified: google-poll-guards ("deleting an event, one day or an edited day that Google had already removed completes here"). Settled 2026-11-09 (the second external review's case 303).
- **Put back judges an answered step by its content.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; replaces the etag reading settled 2026-11-08. Chosen: "nothing changed at Google since" is read by the digest of the series' state (the entry "A Google series' state is one digest of every field" above) for an answered step as for one whose answer was lost: Put back goes ahead when the series' digest at Google is the one its last answered step left, or, the last step's answer lost, the one that step would leave or the one the answered step before it left. Same content is no conflict (RFC 9110's validators are for detecting a change, and vdirsyncer compares content where an etag moved). A removal is still judged by whether the resource is gone, and a step journaled before the digest existed by its etag. Else (was, 2026-11-08): the master's etag against the one the last answered step left, which refused a Put back after any write at Google that changed nothing, since every instance's etag and the master's move together whenever the master or any instance is written (the probe, group 5). Why: one definition of "changed since" for Finish and Put back. Evidence of etag churn without a content change outside such writes is anecdotal (reports of etags moving on Google's side with nothing edited), and is stated as such; the decision does not rest on it. Verified: `server/tests/db/google-series-recovery.test.php` (a 412 at each write of a clock change after the answered ones, the series then written at Google with its content unchanged: Put back restores it; with its place changed: refused and named), the interleaving harness's x-putback. Where: `GoogleSeries::putBack`, `GoogleSeries::unchangedSince`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Put back restores every row the snapshot holds.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: Put back leaves the rows here exactly as they were before the change, every row of every series the change touched, with what hangs off each: after it stores what Google holds, each row of the pending row's snapshot (the rows here as they were, `local_json`) for a series it put back takes back its place, by its id, its own columns and its links, onto the row the store made at its key (`GoogleSeries::reattach`), or, with nothing at its key, whole from the snapshot (`GoogleSeries::bringBack`, the same restore Finish's rewind uses). A day that holds only local data (tags, people, reminders, an icon, an answer, coordinates: an ordinary day to Google, so no target names it) is one such row. Why: a series with changes not yet at Google has had its rows stored from what Google held when the change stopped, and a "this and following" change or a delete from a later day whose first answer was lost leaves Google with the series cut short, so that store dropped the days past the cut; Put back walked only the rows Google's records correspond to (the edited days and events of their own), so a tagged ordinary day past the cut stayed gone with nothing naming it. The tangible benefit: local data on a remote event survives every remote change and recovery, where DAVx5 and vdirsyncer would drop the data or stop and ask. Else: name the day as removed (true, but Put back promises the rows as before), or keep the store from dropping the days while a change is pending, a second rule for the same rows. Verified: the interleaving harness's x-putback and x-alone on putback (fixed cases `put-back-after-a-delete-from-a-later-day-answer-lost-keeps-a-tagged-ordinary-day`, `put-back-after-a-split-answer-lost-keeps-a-day-with-an-icon`), google-series-recovery (a split and a delete from a later day, first answer lost, a tag on an ordinary day past the cut: Put back keeps its row, id and tag). Where: `GoogleSeries::putBack`, `GoogleSeries::reattach`, `GoogleSeries::bringBack`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Put back removes the rows the change made, events of their own included.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: Put back reads the pending row's list of the rows the change's stores made (`made_json`) as Finish's rewind reads it, by one function (`GoogleSeries::dropMade`): once it has cancelled at Google what the change inserted and put the series back, every row on that list the snapshot does not hold goes in the same run, an event of its own kept outside a shortened or moved series (the outside group's keep) among them, and the day's local data (reminders, tags, people) stays where the snapshot had it, on the series' day. A row on the list given something held here alone since is handled as Finish handles it: what it holds goes to the row left for its day, or the row is kept like any later local change. Why: a series with changes not yet at Google whose first step inserted such an event, a later step refused or its answer lost, left that event's row here after Put back cancelled it at Google, because the restore walked only the series' rows; the calendar showed an event Google no longer had until the next poll removed it, and Put back's answer said nothing of it. The tangible benefit: local data on a remote event survives every remote change and recovery, and the calendar after Put back is the calendar before the change, where DAVx5 and vdirsyncer would drop the data or stop and ask. Else: let the next poll remove the row (a window in which the calendar and Google disagree), or remove it by matching the cancelled id (a second list of what the change made). Verified: the adversary probes s12, s13, s15 and s16 (Put back after a 403, a lost answer or a fatal error at the step after the insert, Google and the rows agree), google-series-recovery (a series cut to four with its edited fifth day kept as an event of its own, a 403 or a fatal error at the second write: Put back leaves no row of that event, the day keeps its row, id, reminder and tag). Where: `GoogleSeries::putBack`, `GoogleSeries::dropMade`, `GoogleSeries::rewind`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A recovery names as removed only the rows that are gone after it.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: Finish and Put back decide once, at their end and against the live rows, which rows they removed (`GoogleSeries::withRemoved`): a row an intermediate store dropped (Google's half state stored before the recovery runs, or a read-back stored on the way) that a later step of the same recovery restored under its own id (`reattach`, `bringBack`) or carried onto another row (`relink`, `carryHeld`) is not named in `differences`, in the Activity summary or in `details.removedDays`, and no words say it was kept on another row while it still stands. Why: every row a store dropped was named, so Put back after a times change said a tagged day was "kept on the row its day already has" when the day stood on its own row with its tag, and Finish named as removed the event of its own it had just carried the day's reminder and tag onto (decision A5 names what a poll, Finish or Put back removes, not what passed through a store on the way). Else: name only what the final store removes (misses rows the first store dropped for good), or stop the intermediate stores from dropping rows, a second rule for the same rows. Verified: the adversary probes s14, s16, s20 and s21, google-series-recovery (a series cut short with a fatal error at the second write, then Finish or Put back: `removedDays` names no live row; a times change refused at the second write, a poll, then Put back: nothing named as removed or merged). Where: `GoogleSeries::finish`, `GoogleSeries::putBack`, `GoogleSeries::withRemoved`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A write to a series whose change is half done is refused.** Status: planned. While a change of a series is listed in Review as half done at Google, every write to that series, undo and redo included, is refused with the pending reason and the change's id, pointing to Review's Finish and Put back, and writes nothing here or at Google; Finish and Put back are the way out. Else: let writes through, which builds a second change on a series whose state at Google is unknown and gives Finish rows it can't account for (the follow-up review's N1). Measured by: the interleaving harness's x-pending ("a write to a series whose change is half done"), its fixed case `rename-answer-lost-then-a-tag-while-pending-then-finish`. Settled 2026-11-08. Extended 2026-11-10 to the calendar (the sibling hunt's G1; 1.5.4 did the same): every pending row has an end, Finish, Put back or closed, and a row that can never run again is closed at the moment that becomes true (`GoogleSeries::closeNeverRunnable`): its calendar deleted here, or no longer a Google calendar here. Closed means the pending row is removed, nothing is sent, the rows here stay as they are, and Activity gets one log-only line: "A change to '{title}' that was half done at Google was closed without finishing: its calendar was deleted here." (or "...: this calendar is kept as a copy here now.", or, for a row an earlier version left, "...: this calendar no longer writes to Google from here."). Deleting a calendar is never refused, and closes its rows in the same write: a Google calendar's delete can't be undone here, so the row could never come back with it. Keeping a calendar as a copy (`Calendars::adopt`) is refused with the pending words only while the calendar still writes to Google for this account (`GoogleWriter::writable`), since Finish or Put back can still run; otherwise (its account disconnected, its access paused by the compromise reset, a stored role that can't write) it goes through and closes the rows. Rows an earlier version left (a calendar kept as a copy or deleted while a change was half done) are closed by the daily retention pass (`Activity::prune`), the same function. Disconnecting the account and the reset are not refused: Review lists the row with no action and says what brings Finish and Put back back (`GoogleSeries::unrecoverable`), and Finish and Put back refuse with the same words (`google_calendar_gone`); reconnecting and adding the calendar again re-attaches it. The one open case keeps its exits: when Finish is refused by the format gate and Put back because Google changed since, deleting the calendar, or keeping it as a copy once it no longer writes to Google, ends the row. Not detectable here: Google taking write access away or the calendar gone at Google (the stored role is not relearned once set), so Finish and Put back are sent and refused and adopt is refused too; deleting the calendar is the exit, and learning the lost access from Google's refusal is filed. Else: refuse delete and adopt while a row is pending (round 2 of this fix: it took away the only exits from a row that can never run); close on adopt or delete in every case (adopt of a calendar that still writes to Google would abandon a change that can still be finished); a Review action to leave a row as Google has it (the owner's pending decision, for a row that could otherwise be finished). Verified: journal-gate (adopt of a writable calendar refused with the words, nothing sent or written, going through after Put back; delete going through and closing the row with its line, nothing sent, the delete's undo refused; write access refused by Google: Finish, Put back and adopt refused, delete closing it; disconnected, paused and reader calendars listed with no action and the reason, then adopt closing each with its line; re-attached, both offered again; rows left on a copy and on a deleted calendar closed by the retention pass, each with its line). Inventory: the worker's report `worker-155-rule3.md`, round 3.
- **A failed part of a write rolls back its whole transaction.** Status: choice. Chosen: a write that fails part way is undone by rolling back the transaction it runs in, whole, never to a savepoint; anything that must still be stored after the failure (what Google holds once a step reached it) is written after that rollback in a transaction of its own, and the request then fails with the deferred error. Finish whose part here can't be written, and a Google step that fails mid-change, both leave the pending row marked half done on the journal's connection and store what Google holds once the account's transaction is over (`GoogleSeries::keepAfterTx`); when nothing reached Google there is nothing to keep. The error and its words are unchanged (`google_finish_local` for Finish's part here). Why: MariaDB 10.11 stops with an InnoDB assertion (`fts0fts.cc` line 2188) when a row of a table with a fulltext index is read, deleted under a savepoint, rolled back to that savepoint and deleted again in one transaction, which is what Finish did when its part here failed on a day Google no longer had: the writer deleted the day's row under its savepoint, and storing what Google holds deleted it again. The interleaving harness reached it with no failure injected. Else: keep the savepoint and avoid the second delete, which leaves the next path that deletes a row twice to find the same assertion. Verified: `grep SAVEPOINT server/src` finds nothing; google-series-recovery (a shortened series half done, a day past its new end tagged here, Finish's part here refused: the error, Review's entry and the rows as Google holds them); the interleaving harness's seed 7 case 260 runs to its end. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Every Google request the app sends is conditional.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: before any PATCH or DELETE, the series (or event of its own) it writes is read from Google, its state is compared with the rows here by one digest (`GoogleSeries::condition`, the series' fields as `GoogleSeries::state` reads them), a difference refuses the write with `google_conflict` before anything is sent, and that read's etag goes as `If-Match`. A day with no record at Google (an ordinary occurrence addressed by its instance id) is read by that id for its etag. A series change reads every series it touches once, before its first step, inserts included, so a change made at Google since the last poll refuses the whole change with nothing sent; its later steps chain the etags of the app's own answers and read-backs. One function for every writer: a "this event" edit, a delete, "use series value", an undo, a Redo, an answer to an invitation and a whole-series change. No caller is exempt: a caller that reads the event itself to build its body (an undo of a day with no row here, an undo of a delete, an answer and its undo) passes that read to the same comparison, and its etag is the If-Match only once the comparison passes (amended 2026-11-09 after the external review's F1: a Redo of a day whose undo had removed its row read the day and sent that read's etag without the comparison, overwriting a place changed at Google since the last poll). Nothing is ever sent without `If-Match`; `GoogleWriter` refuses to. Why: until now a write took its `If-Match` from the row stored here, and a day with no row had none, so it went unconditionally: a "this event" edit after a rename at Google not yet polled sent the day whole with the old title, and the sync then kept it as a day of its own. Comparing content rather than etags is the same rule Put back uses (same content is no conflict, RFC 9110); a field only Google holds (colour, guests, visibility) is not in the rows, so a change of it alone does not refuse a write, and no write sends it. Else: keep the stored etag and read only where none is stored (two rules, and an etag stored here moves with every write to any row of the series); or send unconditionally and reconcile at the next poll, which overwrites the change made elsewhere. Cost: one read before each write. Verified by the interleaving harness's x-conflict ("is refused with google_conflict" on w-this-g, w-this-m, w-del1) and x-own (w-this-m, w-all, putback, u-entry), x-conditional, google-etag ("a series changed at Google since the last poll refuses a write to an ordinary day before anything is sent"), google-undo (a Redo of a day with no row here after the same field changed at Google is refused and changes nothing; one after a change to a field only Google holds goes through) and the interleaving fixed case `a-redo-of-a-day-whose-row-went-is-conditional`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A write is conditional on what is shown here; Put back on everything.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. A write is refused as `google_conflict` when the remote record changed since the last poll in any field the rows here hold, the fields the person could see when they decided; a change at Google in a field the rows do not hold (guests, colour, visibility, transparency today) does not refuse it, since the body never carries those fields and nothing of theirs is overwritten. Put back keeps the whole-resource digest of 2026-11-08: it is a mechanical reversal with nobody looking, so it is conditional on everything. Else: a per-row digest of the fields not held here, refusing a write on an invisible change, which DAVx5 and vdirsyncer do only because an etag is all they have; or one scope for both, which reopens the Put back reading for no gain.
