The rule is general to any source with fields the rows do not model, and grows on its own: a field becomes part of the condition the day it is stored here. The recommendation reversed in discussion: the per-row digest was proposed first as the one-definition answer, and the owner's question about other sources with fields we cannot model showed that refusing on an invisible change protects nothing, while "conditional on what is shown here" is the general rule and needs no storage. Verified: x-conflict (a change in a field not held here alone is no conflict; the etag-only fixed case), the series harness's Google mode. Settled 2026-11-09 with the owner.
- **A series change and Finish apply their deltas over what Google holds now, by one rule.** Status: replaced for a change being sent, 2026-11-14, by "After the series' write, the days are set from what Google holds" (below): the days of a change being sent no longer go over the read back by this rule, they are compared with the plan and sent; Finish keeps it as narrowed by "Finish sets the plan's days where it can't tell its own change from another". Was: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: after a series change's master write, its days go over the read-back through the same rebase Finish uses (`GoogleSeries::rebase`, rule A): a day changed at Google between the read before the change and the read-back keeps that change where the change does not touch the field, and is kept and named in the Activity entry and the Review notice where it does. Untouched means a value the read-back holds that the change explains (as it was, as the change's own master write leaves it, or as the change sets it): such a day takes the change's reading, its own list exactly the change's, and a field joins a day's own list only where Google changed that field since the read before the change; a skipped day the change's own status write brought back at Google is that write's doing, and stays skipped as the change has it; a skip at the day an edited day moves onto is a value of that day, never a field to compare, so the edited day keeps its identity and the skip its place (the review's F3). A day's own list after a read-back is the union of what it holds here (its tags, people, reminders, answer and icon, and its place by coordinates while it shows the text they were picked for, from the rows as they stand before the store) and what the change and Google give it; a record Google made at a day that held only local data never replaces the local part, so a later poll that drops the record keeps the day (the review's F2). An undo or redo of a series change is an ordinary series write (settled 2026-11-09, the owner's restatement after the review of 9de4e3dc..8e568077 found N1 to N3): what it asks is the inverse of what the undone change asked (back to what it found, nothing it learned from Google on the way), planned by the series model over the series as stored now as a forward change is (the entry "An undo of a Google series change is planned as a forward change is"), so a field or day the change did not change stays as it stands, a series title Google changed during a place change included; that target then goes through the same path as any change: the read and digest check before the first step, the master and day writes, the read-back, and the one rebase whose base is the series as stored before this write, where every value the deltas do not explain is a change made elsewhere, kept and named, a day renamed at Google during the undo's own write included. There is no undo input to the rebase: the undone change's `asked` as the rebase's base, the read before the write considered for days' fields and not the master's, and the step that put a day back at that read's value were removed. A day the change left as it found it that Google has since put back to the series (no record now where the change's own write would have left one) follows the series, in a change and in Finish as in an undo: its own list is what it holds now, never the change's, so an undo never sends such a day back as an edited day under an old title (the adversarial read of 89bab68e, A1). A value the undo's deltas change that holds otherwise now is kept and named in the undo's Activity entry and Review notice, as a change's is, when Google changed it while the change ran (the change's after snapshot holds it); one changed since that, polled or made here, is a later change and makes the undo stale (`stale_undo`, `force` to pass) by its ground (the entry "Staleness has one definition: grounds are fields"), the series' own field and a day's alike, a day judged by its series and occurrence, not by which row holds it (C1). An entry saved before changes kept what they asked (1.5.4, within the seven-day undo window after 1.5.5 ships) is refused with a plain reason, `not_undoable`: its after snapshot mixes what it asked with what it learned from Google, and undoing from it would send back a value Google changed meanwhile (D1; refusing chosen over stating the limitation). A Put back reverses a change that stopped part way, so its deltas run from the rows as Google holds them. Before, the commit put every record its plan had no exception for back to the series' values, overwriting a day edited at Google meanwhile without a word. Why: one definition of "apply a change" for a commit and for Finish, and none of the reference engines overwrites a remote change it has not seen; the benefit is that an edit made at Google while a change was being sent survives it. Else: keep the commit's own carrying step and let only Finish rebase, two rules for one question. Verified by the interleaving harness's x-readback, fixed case `a-day-renamed-at-google-during-a-series-write-is-the-changes-again`, and the series harness's Google mode (c-untouched and f-undo at 1,000 cases, fixed cases `google-an-untouched-edited-day-keeps-its-own-list-after-a-zone-and-rule-change`, `google-a-skipped-day-a-status-change-brought-back-stays-skipped-and-undoes`, `google-undo-of-a-move-onto-a-skipped-day-with-a-status-change` and its date-only control), the interleaving fixed cases `coordinates-of-a-day-survive-a-race-and-a-clock-move-at-google` (a delta poll) and `a-local-only-day-renamed-during-a-series-write-then-the-clock-moved-at-google-then-` a whole read and a 410, `an-undo-of-a-rename-keeps-a-day-renamed-at-google-during-it`, `an-undo-of-a-rename-keeps-a-day-renamed-at-google-twice` and `an-undo-of-a-place-change-keeps-a-series-title-changed-at-google-during-it` (x-readback, x-undo-fields, x-agree), google-series-undo ("a day renamed at Google during a rename and again during its undo", "a series renamed at Google during a place change"), and google-series ("a day holding only a tag, renamed at Google during a series rename", its own list asserted right after the read-back). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **An undo that Finish or Put back recovers is journaled as the undo would have been.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: an undo of a series change keeps with its pending row which entry it undoes and its words (its part here); Finish completes it, its part here being that entry's snapshots put back, then marks the entry undone and records the undo's own entry (the redo's befores, the rows as they were and are), saying it was finished from Review and what Google kept, so a redo, the bare Undo and a second undo find exactly what a completed undo leaves; the pending row carries everything Finish needs, the undone entry's snapshots among them, so Finish completes even after Activity's retention removed the entry, and says plainly what it could not put back here rather than leaving the item half done (R4); Put back takes the undo back whole, the entry staying not undone and undoable, and the line saying so is the person's (the one who clicked Put back) and touches no row, so it neither makes that entry stale nor stands before it for the bare Undo: both judge an entry by what it changed, never by who wrote it (2026-11-09). Why: a recovered change's journal is what the change would have written had it completed; before, a finished undo left its change not undone with no entry to redo, refused a second undo as stale, and named the series by its old title, and a put-back undo made its change stale. Else: journal a recovery as a log-only line of its own whatever it recovered. Verified: google-series-undo's "an undo Finish completes from Review is journaled as an undo; one Put back takes back leaves the change undoable" (B3 of the adversarial read of 89bab68e). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **An Activity line on a Google calendar is composed from its parts, never cut from another line.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: a change's line is what was done, then the calendar, then a note (a Finish from Review says so), then what was put back over a change made at Google since (a forced undo, under its own heading) and what Google kept, each part kept in the entry's details (`action`, `kept`, `forced`) and composed by one function (`GoogleUndo::line`), which alone puts the punctuation (no part ends in its own full stop), within the column (300): what is shortened to fit is the end of the words, then of the action, never the calendar. A line written before entries kept their action gives its action by the composer's own reading of it (`GoogleUndo::actionOf`: the line less the calendar and what followed it), never appended to. Put back of a half-done redo says it was a redo (F3 to F5 of the third read). An undo's or redo's line is composed from the undone entry's action, never by cutting its line. Why: the change's line clipped the calendar's name at the column, and an undo's line, cut at the kept words that came before the calendar, lost it (the second read, R5). Else: append and clip as before. Verified: google-series-undo's "an undo's and a redo's lines are composed whole". Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A refusal records nothing here.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: a Google answer that refuses a write or an undo (any 4xx, a 412 among them) changes nothing there, so it changes nothing here: when no step of a change is left in its journal (each one refused, or none sent), the pending row goes, nothing is read back or stored, the message says only why Google refused (the half-done words only once a step reached Google), and the account's transaction rolls back whole, the trip flag and rules written before the first request with it. Rows, etags, the calendar's sync token, `google_pending` and Activity stay as they were; a 403 at the first write leaves no half-done item in Review. Why: a refusal is Google saying it wrote nothing, and a record of a change that never happened would send the owner to Review to finish or put back nothing; reading back and storing on a refusal also dropped what a row held here alone. Else: keep the pending row and store what Google holds on every failure (the behaviour until 2026-11-09), which listed refused changes as half done with nothing done. Verified by x-conflict "the refusal writes nothing" (its local part), x-undo-fields "a refused undo writes nothing", x-pending "a failure before anything was sent" and google-series-recovery. Settled 2026-11-09.

- **Staleness has one definition: grounds are fields.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: an entry's ground is the set of (row or day, field) pairs it changed, written by every change when it is recorded (`Undo::touchesOf`; the Feeds poll through the same one function) and read by every judgement (`Undo::staleFor`, `Undo::conflicts`, with no switch per kind of entry): a column of a row; a day's own field, recorded on its series' row at its occurrence (`day:<occurrence>|<field>`), for a day's row made or removed whole the fields it owns, its tags and people among them, which the tag and people links write too, so a later tag change on that day touches that day's tags (a day's link is that day's field alone, never a row ground of its own, so it is followed through a move as every field of the day is, 2026-11-09, seed 7 case 403); a skip per date; and a day a change carried to another occurrence as a move (`move:<from>><to>`), which a later judgement follows (`Undo::dayNow`), so a day keeps its identity through a times or zone change. Two entries conflict when their grounds meet; the own list and the skips list are never grounds themselves, their contents are. A snapshot entry adds what its restore puts back besides (`Undo::restoredGround`): the values a series change wrote on days following it, and, where it changed a series' times or zone, that series' occurrences, which every day, skip and rule of it stands on. A day's row an undo takes the making of is composed over the row as it stands (the fields the entry gave it follow the series again, `Undo::withoutOwn`), a row brought back whole is its snapshot on a calendar here and composed by the one composition on a Google calendar (`Undo::overCurrent`), and a row whose own part an undo changed stays only while it still owns anything or holds anything here alone (the retention rule). Why: the old judgement switched staleness per kind of entry and treated the own list as one column, so a series time undo after a later change of one day dropped that day silently, two "this event" edits of different fields of one day staled each other, and undoing the edit that made a day's row deleted a tag added later; now each of those undoes as a person expects, and undoing a pair leaves the same end state in either order. Removed: the per-entry switch in `staleFor` and `conflicts`, `ownColumns` with its tags and people pseudo-columns, `keptForLater` and its own-list growth test, the day aliasing at judgement time (`dayRowsOf`), and the Feeds poll's second touch list. Else: keep a reading per kind of entry, the fourth adversarial read's t1, k4 and k6. Verified: x-undo-fields and x-agree on the interleaving fixed set and seeds 7 and 307, undo's "two edits of different fields of one date undo in either order" (k1, k4, k6 and t1 on a calendar here), google-series-undo's k4 and k6 scenarios, completeness (a day's ground said on its series' row). Settled 2026-11-09. Grounds are compared by key along recorded moves; where a later change re-placed a series' days with no record for an entry's day, the undo never reaches this comparison: the gate refuses it first (the entry "An undo acts on a day only where the journal says exactly where it is", 2026-11-10).
- **A start moved earlier onto a day the pattern gives is a bound, like one moved later.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (the series planner, a person's own edits included). Chosen: a request that names no day and moves the series' start onto a day its rule gives is a bound in either direction: the days before the new start leave the series, or the days between the new and the old start join it, and every other day stays on its date; the days a start move alone brings in are what was asked for, never edge gains, so no skip is generated on the new start day. Else: the earlier direction as a drag that shifted every edited day by the move (1.5.0 to 1.5.4), while the later direction was a bound; the model document had said "a bound like the end" for both. Found while making an undo plan carry days as a forward change does; kept on its own merits because it corrects the planner toward its own document. Verified: the sixth adversarial read's 16 rule shapes against series-model.md, both series fixed sets, the local seeds at 3,000 cases. Settled 2026-11-09 with the owner, who asked that a regression from it be answered for.
- **An undo of a Google series change is planned as a forward change is.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; restated 2026-11-09 (the fifth adversarial read). Chosen: the inverse of what the undone change asked (each group of the series' own fields it changed set back to what it found, `GoogleSeries::inverseEdit`) is planned by the series planner as the request a person would send for the whole series from the editor, naming no day (`Series\Model::setPlan` calls `Model::plan`), so its days follow by the forward planner's rules and no other: a day keeps its date through a rule, count, bound or start change (the new-rule reading that keeps days, and a start moved earlier onto a day the pattern gives is a bound like one moved later), a times or zone change moves it with its occurrence, and a day whose date is no occurrence of the restored series is a removed day. The days the change itself carried to other occurrences go back where it found them, read from its recorded moves (`move:` grounds, rows' and skips' alike), so an undo takes back its change's carrying as it takes back its fields; what the change did not change of the series (its rule text, its times) stays as it stands, and the master takes the restored values as given. A day the planner puts outside stays where it is when the restored series has it there, one it places off the restored series' occurrences follows the occurrence on its date as the sync follows one (`Feeds::ofSeries`), else it is removed; a start the drag table refuses is set as values the same way. These passes stay (2026-11-09, the sixth read asked to fold them into the planner or remove them): removed, a times change for all events with its zone kept at the same moment, undone, lost a day's own date and another day's row (Google seed 7 case 609), because the planner plans the inverse to its own master while the undo restores the master's values as given; they belong in the planner as one rule, a plan made to a given master, which is not built yet. A day the change itself dropped (no day of the series it left) is matched to nothing by its date: `Undo::dayNow` says so for every caller, so the planned undo's day deltas and the restore of its row agree, and a row brought back for it comes back at its own key, composed onto a row there (the entry "A row brought back is composed onto the day as it stands"); GoogleUndo's own copy of that test went into it. The days the change changed beyond carrying them (a skip, a field of its own given, taken or changed, a field held here alone among them) are put back at their places in the planned series (`GoogleSeries::withDayDeltas`), each day identified through every state by what carried it (the change's moves, the later ones by `Undo::dayNow`, the planner's placement of its rows and skips), never by its index in a count. A day's row the undo brings back comes back where the undo's series has it (`Undo::restoreSnapshots` takes the planned keys). Skips: the planner records which skip went where (`skipMoves`), and every series change, here or at Google, records those moves as its ground (`Undo::skipMoveGrounds`); a skip no plan moved is its date's alone, never paired by sort order. Why: the nth occurrence of one series is not the same day as the nth of another; undoing a rule change after a later day edit put that edit on another date at Google (1 Feb instead of 18 Jan), and undoing a start move put it a week off. Removed: `Model::placesThrough` and the index mapping in `setPlan` and `withDayDeltas`, GoogleUndo's own dropped-day test (now `dayNow`'s), `Undo::skipsChanged`'s pairing of skips by sort order, `withDayDeltas`' separate pass for fields held here alone (one loop over a day's parts), the own-side filter in `staleFor` (conflicts already reads `+` and `move:` as no ground). Else: carry by index (the 2026-11-09 build, the fifth read's p1 and p8); or hand the planner the inverse drag naming a day, whose readings are not their own inverse (a month-end clamp, a member of a weekly list). Verified: google-series-undo's p1 scenario and the existing t1, t3 and F6 ones, the series harness's Google fixed set and seeds 7 and 211 at 1,000 (f-undo, seed 7 case 915 a yearly rule among them), the probes p1, p4, p8 and adversary4's t1 to s2. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **The days a change brought into being are its ground.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: the days a change added are its planner's gains, the days of the series it leaves that are no old day's image, recorded by the planner when the change is made (`Series\Model`'s `writes['added']`, stored by `Undo::addedGrounds` with the change's ground), never worked out again from dates when a later change is judged (restated 2026-11-09, the sixth adversarial read). A reading that keeps every day on its date (a rule, count, bound or start change, an all-day or field edit) records `added` alone: a day is one it added when the series it found has no day on that date, the date read on the calendar of the series it left (its zone, or its dates when all-day). A reading that carries days (a drag, a times or zone change that moves days) records each added day (`added:<key>`): every one of a bounded series, and of one with no end those through the span the planner read (a whole cycle past every day the change names, after which its days come round as before), with `added><key>` when every day past the last image is added (the series it found ended there). A later change touching such a day meets the ground `day:<key>|*`. A later change of such a day makes the change's undo stale with words that name it ("Mon 8 Mar 2027 exists because of this change and was changed since, so undoing it would remove that day"). Forced, the undo removes it and names each removed day by row and date as the sync names one (`Feeds::droppedDay`, details `removedDays`): at Google in the undo's own entry, here in a line of its own, since a local undo records no entry. Why: undoing a count raise after an edit of a day it added deleted that edit and its tag unnamed at Google, and left a row past the end here; and the judgement by dates read a key in the found series' zone, so an all-day change of a series west of UTC and a start dragged from Monday to Tuesday counted every carried day as added, and any later day edit made the undo refused with a false reason (f6, f4). Else: list every added day when the change is recorded, which a series with no end can't for a reading that keeps dates (every Wednesday a rule gains, forever); or keep reading dates at judgement time, which disagrees with the planner whenever a day moves. Verified: google-series-undo's p5 and f4 scenarios, undo's p6 and f6 scenarios, the probes p5, p6, f4 and f6. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A snapshot undo leaves every row following the series it restores.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: after a snapshot undo puts a series' master back on a calendar here, every day's row of that series takes the series' value in each field the restore puts back on the series and the row doesn't own, tags and people included (`Undo::followRestored`, its own list read by `Feeds::ownOf`, so a row stored before own lists is read as the sync reads it), through the one composition of a row following its series (`Feeds::followSeries`, which Finish's rows kept since use too, `GoogleSeries::follow`): a place's text and the coordinates picked for it as one value (`Feeds::followed`: coordinates the target carries with its text are taken with it, and a new text without them clears them), and its tags and people as the series' links, and a row whose day the series no longer has is removed and named (the rule above). The same on a calendar here and a Google calendar (restated 2026-11-09, the sixth read's f8 and f13: the provider test went), where Google's answer is then stored on top. A place renamed without coordinates leaves none, on a single event, a day and a series alike (`Events::columnPatch`): before, a rename for all events kept the old place's coordinates on the series. Why: a row made after a rename (a day's notes edited) kept the copied new title after the rename was undone. Amended 2026-11-09 after the external review's R3: `followRestored` kept a list of its own of the scalar fields a row follows, without tags and people, so a day made after a series' tags and people changed (owning only its notes) kept the later tags and people once that change was undone, here and at Google; the list is gone, and no second way of following a series is left (`GoogleSeries::follow` kept a copy of the same loop, which went with it). A restore makes the days follow in the fields it puts back on the series (the master's columns and links that differ between the entry's two snapshots; none for a series removed whole, whose rows come back with it as they were), so a field the undone change left alone is left alone on every row: a day the sync made from Google carries no copy of the series' tags, and following every field gave such a day the series' tags on the redo of a title change (interleaving seed 7 case 280, x-undo-fields). The retention rule runs before the rows follow, so a day's row the undone change made, owning nothing now, goes before it could take the series' links. Else: restore only the rows the entry snapshot holds. Verified: undo's p11 and f8 scenarios, undo-day-identity's tags-and-people scenarios (here and at Google: a day made since follows the restored links, a day owning its tags keeps them), the probes p11, f8 and f13, and the review's inherited-links probe. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A pending undo keeps its bare action.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: the pending row of an undo of a series change keeps the undo's action as `GoogleUndo::undoSummary` gives it (no calendar, no kept words), and every line is composed once from it by `GoogleUndo::line`; the plan's `summary` is no longer written, and `SeriesPlans::recoveredUndo` reads it only for a row written before `action` was kept. Why: a Put back of a half-done redo could name the calendar twice. Verified: google-series-undo's p13 scenario. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A row brought back is composed onto the day as it stands.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: every undo or redo that brings a day's row back (one its entry removed, deleted, took out of the series or moved off its day) composes it over the day as it stands, by one function (`Undo::overCurrent`), on a calendar here and a Google calendar alike, in the check before Google is sent anything and in the restore: where a row holds that day now, the row brought back is that row (it takes its id, what it owns is applied last and whole, a place's text with its coordinates, and what the row there owns stays its own); else the day takes its series' values for the fields the row doesn't own. The check runs exactly the restore it guards (`Undo::checkRestore` takes the keys the undo's series places the rows at), and a day the entry itself dropped is matched to nothing by its date (`Undo::dayNow`), so it comes back at its own key. Why: a forced undo of a count lowered and raised again, or of a start moved later and back, after the day was edited again, ended in a raw database error (a duplicate key) here and at Google, inside the check (f9, f10). Else: refuse such an undo, which force exists to pass. Verified: undo's and google-series-undo's f9 and f10 scenarios, the probes f9 to f11. A skip is the day's value as it stands: a row brought back onto a day skipped since, which this restore leaves skipped, has nothing to compose onto, so it is not brought back and is named as removed in the entry, on a calendar here and a Google calendar alike; a skip the entry made itself is the restore's to take back, read from its snapshots or its recorded skip ground, which every delete of a day on a Google calendar now records, an edited day's and its redo's as an ordinary day's already did (seventh adversarial read P2-2, where a forced undo of a count lowered and raised again put a deleted day back beside its skip here while Google ended with the skip; verified by undo's and google-series-undo's P2-2 scenarios and the probes a6 and a7; settled 2026-11-09). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A row brought back is composed onto the day as it stands on a Google calendar; on a calendar here it is its snapshot.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; restated 2026-11-09. Chosen: one decision point by provider (`Undo::apply`): on a calendar here a snapshot undo or redo puts the rows back exactly as they were, byte-identical, the local-only data with them, and its composition is the snapshot (`Undo::snapshotOnto`); where a row it brings back (one its entry removed, deleted, took out of the series or moved off its day) finds another row holding that day now, the snapshot row wins and takes that row's id, so nothing meets on the day's key and what refers to either names the one row. On a Google calendar every undo or redo that brings a day's row back composes it over the day as it stands, by one function (`Undo::overCurrent`), in the check before Google is sent anything and in the restore: where a row holds that day now, the row brought back is that row (it takes its id, what it owns is applied last and whole, a place's text with its coordinates, and what the row there owns stays its own); else the day takes its series' values for the fields the row doesn't own. The check runs exactly the restore it guards (`Undo::checkRestore` takes the keys the undo's series places the rows at), and a day the entry itself dropped is matched to nothing by its date (`Undo::dayNow`), so it comes back at its own key. Why: a forced undo of a count lowered and raised again, or of a start moved later and back, after the day was edited again, ended in a raw database error (a duplicate key) here and at Google, inside the check (f9, f10). Removed (2026-11-09): the local undo routed through `overCurrent` (merge afcb0c37), which gave a calendar here the Google composition and broke byte-identical undo and redo (series harness seed 23 cases 185, 1237, 1753, 1889 and 2532, seed 31 cases 705 and 731: f-undo, f-redo, f-stack). Else: refuse such an undo, which force exists to pass; or one composition for both providers, which a calendar here has no reason for, since nothing else writes its rows. Verified: undo's f9 and f10 scenarios (the snapshot under the id of the row there) and google-series-undo's (composed), the probes f9 to f11, the series harness's local seeds 23 and 31 and Google seeds 7 and 211. Settled 2026-11-09. Since 2026-11-10 the snapshot comes back only where it is still of the series as it stands: a row or a skip it holds is refused, forced or not, once a later change re-placed its series' days (never composed), and a row brought back stands in the calendar its series is in now (the entry "An undo acts on a day only where the journal says exactly where it is").
- **Finish's ground is what the change it completes changed; Put back's is the change it closes.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: Review's Finish records as its ground the series' rows before the half-done change began (its pending row's own record, `local_json`) against the rows Finish leaves (`Undo::touchesOf`), as any change's ground is read; Put back leaves the rows as they were before the change, so its ground is its pending row and no row: no earlier entry stands on it, and the bare Undo still answers with its line, refusing it as Review's own. Before, both recorded the whole series (`*`), so every earlier entry about any day of it was stale after either (seed 7 case 403, seed 13 case 203, seed 419 case 607). Else: read Finish's ground from the rows just before it, which counts the half-done change's own local writes as Finish's. Verified: the interleaving fixed case `a-day-with-its-own-time-then-a-time-change-finished-from-review-then-its-undo` (x-undo-fields). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A row Finish keeps follows its series by the one composition.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: a day's row a recovery keeps because it changed here since the change stopped (a tag on an ordinary day, say) follows the series as Finish's writer leaves it by the composition the sync and an undo use: its day is where the series has it now (`Feeds::ofSeries`, read from the series the row stood under), its own placement read by `OwnFields::placed`, and each field it doesn't own, tags and people among them, the series' (`Feeds::followSeries` over `Feeds::followed`: a place's text and the coordinates picked for it one value). Only a day the series no longer has refuses the part here. Removed: what was left of `GoogleSeries::follow` after `Feeds::followSeries` took its field loop (its own occurrence check and placement, strict column compares), which refused Finish whenever the series' clock had moved since the row was stored (the kept row's key was no occurrence of the series as written). Why: local data on a remote event survives every remote change and recovery, and a half-done change finishes without losing either side's edits; DAVx5 and vdirsyncer would drop the data or stop and ask. Else: keep a recovery composition of its own, which drifted from the sync's (strict compares, coordinates by column, no move to the day's image). Verified: google-series-recovery ("a times change stopped after Google took its master write, a tag on an ordinary day here, then the clock moved again at Google": Finish completes, the same row on that day at the series' time with its tag; before, Finish failed with `google_finish_kept` and the change stayed half done). Where: `GoogleSeries::placeKept`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Recovery finds a day's row where its series has it now.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: every match recovery makes between a day's row and a day of a target (whether a target names the day of a row a store made, which row takes what such a row held here alone, which row a snapshot's day is now, and which row a target's own list goes on) reads both sides by the one day identity the sync follows (`Feeds::ofSeries`: the series' occurrence on the day the key showed under the series it was read under), never by the key the snapshot or an earlier store gave it. Why: a series with changes not yet at Google whose clock then moved at Google is rebased by Finish onto Google's clock while its days stay keyed as the change planned them; matched by key, a row a store made and tagged here since was neither taken nor carried, Finish refused the part here, and the change stayed half done in Review with words that blamed the tag. Matched by day, Finish completes and the tag stays on that day's one row, where DAVx5 and vdirsyncer would drop it or stop and ask. Else: rekey the rebased target's days to Google's clock (a second place days are moved, outside recovery's matching), or refuse Finish after a clock change at Google. The probe (a times change with a lost answer or a refusal at each later write, the store's row for a day tagged here, then another client moving the clock): before, Finish failed every time and no held data was lost; a times change alone, or a clock moved at Google over tagged ordinary and edited days, already completed with every tag kept. Verified: google-series-recovery ("a row a store of a times change made, tagged here, then the clock moved at Google"). Where: `GoogleSeries::whereNow`, `exceptionOn`, used by `dropMade`, `carryHeld`, `reattach` and `store`. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A cancelled event an insert meets is made live by the one conditional write.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: an insert under an id the app chose that Google answers 409 for reads the event back; when it is cancelled (an undo's undo, or another client cancelling it while a change was half done) it is made live by `GoogleWriter::patch`, the write every PATCH goes through: that read compared with the rows here (`GoogleSeries::condition`), its etag the If-Match, and a body of only what differs from that read on the allowed list (`GoogleWriter::patchOf`) with the status confirmed. Removed: the bespoke PATCH of `GoogleSeries::insertChosen`, which sent the whole body with an etag from a bare read and no comparison. Else: insert under a new id (a second event for one change), or keep the bespoke PATCH (the only write not checked by content). Verified: google-series ("an insert whose answer was lost, the event then cancelled at Google": the revive's keys are all allowed, the title and notes the read already holds are not sent, and it carries the read's etag). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **One function decides whether a day's row stays, an undo's rows included.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: after an undo changes a day's own part, the row stays only while it owns a field or `Feeds::retained` finds something held here alone on it (the fields of `OwnFields::HELD_HERE` it owns, picked coordinates, and what refers to it, trip links, duplicate decisions and plugin data included). Tags and people a row has without owning them are its series' copy (a day's row starts with them), never a reason of their own to keep it. Removed: `Undo::retain`'s own tag and people check beside `Feeds::retained`, which kept a row holding only the series' tags, so the sync and an undo disagreed on the same row. Else: count any tag link as held here (a copy of the series' would keep every row a day ever had). Verified: undo ("a date's row made by a title edit, the series then tagged for all events, the date given notes; both edits undone": no row is left, the series keeps its tag). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A ground named against a day's own row is stored as that day's.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: every ground an entry records goes through one writer (`Undo::recordTouches`), which stores a ground a caller names against a day's own row (an answer's `invite_json`, `status` and `attendance`, `GoogleUndo::record`'s and `Events::googleJournal`'s default `*`) in the day form every other day ground has (`Undo::dayGround`: on the series' row at the day's occurrence), so a later change of the same field of that day, whichever path made it, stales the earlier entry's undo. Before, an answer on a day of a recurring invitation stood on its row's own key, a later attendance change of that day on the day form, and the answer's undo was not stale and would have put the old answer back over the newer choice. Else: convert at each caller (three places that could drift), or keep two forms and match both in `staleFor`. Verified: google-undo ("an answer on a day of a recurring invitation, then a later attendance change of that day": the undo is stale; a single event's answer still stands on its own row). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A day's row the sync makes starts with its series' tags and people.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: a day's row a poll makes from a record of its own (Google or ICS) takes its series' tags and people as a copy it does not own, through the one composition (`Feeds::followSeries`, limited to the links), as every row a writer here makes does. The copy is not held here alone, so retention (`Feeds::retained`) still removes the row once the record holds nothing of its own. Before, such a row had no tags or people, so the day showed without its series' tags wherever a reader takes a day's links from its row. Else: read the series' links at display time for a row that owns none (a second reading of the same rule). Verified: google-series ("a tagged series, a poll bringing a record of its own for one day"). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A day kept by an undo shows the series' tags and people it does not own.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: a day's tags and people are its own only when its own list says so; otherwise its links are the series' copy. A day's row an undo keeps (for a field a later change gave it, or because the undo changed its own list) takes the tags and people it doesn't own from its series as it stands, through the one composition (`Feeds::followSeries`, limited to the links, in `Undo::retain` after the retention rule), never left with the empty set the undo's delta leaves when it deletes the copy made with the row. A day's row an undo or redo brings back whole does the same (`Undo::followLinks`), never keeping the copy its snapshot held when the series' tags changed since (seed 857 case 419). An owned set goes back by its delta and is left as that leaves it. The same on a calendar here and a Google calendar, both through `Undo::putBack`. Before, undoing the notes that made a day's row, kept for a place given later, deleted the links made with the row, so the day showed without its series' tag and person and a poll left it so (review of 94693e30 to cb2c6082, R3); a harness fixture had blessed that as an exact link set going back. Else: keep the copy's link rows out of the delta (the copy would then be the series' set as it was when the row was made, not as it stands); or treat a row's links as its own whenever it has them, which contradicts the own list. Verified by the interleaving harness's x-undo-fields (fixed cases `a-day-made-by-notes-kept-by-a-place-keeps-the-series-tags-on-undo-and-redo`, whose probe takes the kept day's tags away, and `a-day-brought-back-by-a-redo-shows-the-series-tags-as-they-stand`), undo-day-identity's "kept for its place" scenarios (here and at Google, judged on the window, with the control of a day owning its tags), and the review's inherited-created probe. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A request that changes nothing sends nothing and is no conflict.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: a write whose result equals what is stored (a time for all events asked to be the clock the series already has, the acted day's own time the same) asks Google nothing, not even the read a write makes first, and writes nothing here, so it is not refused even when the series changed at Google since the last poll: what the refusal protects is the change at Google, and nothing would overwrite it. The interleaving oracle judges x-conflict by what the step did (no request to Google of any kind and no change here), never by the field the request names; a refused write answered ok still counts, since its read reached Google. Before, the oracle demanded a refusal from its request label alone (seed 857 case 249). Else: refuse every request on a series changed at Google, which would refuse a no-op for no gain. Verified by x-conflict (fixed case `a-whole-series-clock-the-day-already-owns-changes-nothing-and-is-no-conflict`, tallied as "nothing to change"). Settled 2026-11-09.
- **Undo of a whole-series change on a Google calendar waits for 1.5.6.** Status: choice for 1.5.5, planned for 1.5.6. Chosen: undo and redo of a change to all events, this and following, delete following, an answer from a day on, a series created or deleted on a Google calendar (an entry of kind series, `GoogleUndo::seriesEntry`) are held back by the `google_series_undo` setting, off by default: the undo, a forced undo and the redo refuse with 409 `not_undoable` and "Undo of a change to all events on a Google calendar comes in a later release; this change stays. Undo of a single day still works." before anything is read or sent, the bare Undo passes over such an entry to the change before it, and Activity lists it as not undoable with that reason (the write's notice offers no Undo for it). Everything else is as built: undo of single-day Google edits, changes held here alone, Finish and Put back, the conditional writes, the pending gate, retention and naming. The setting follows the internal-key pattern of `Settings::DEFAULTS` (like `mailSenderMigrationDone`): stored in the account's settings, never client-writable (a patch naming it is refused as an unknown setting) and not in the Settings page. Why: the mechanism needs one more planner rule before it is sound for every interleaving: the planner plans an inverse to a given master, carrying days by the undone change's own mapping and recording its added days from that plan. 1.5.6 builds that rule and turns the setting on. Else: ship it on and fix the remaining interleavings in 1.5.6, which would let an undo land a series somewhere no reading of it produces. Verified: the database suite and both edit harness modes run with the setting on (`freshUser` in `server/tests/db/lib.php` turns it on), so every scenario and the interleaving oracle keep measuring the mechanism; one scenario in `google-series-undo.test.php` turns it off and asserts the refusal, the bare Undo passing over and nothing sent to Google. Settled 2026-11-09. A known limit of 1.5.5 that follows from this and from "An undo acts on a day only where the journal says exactly where it is", kept by the owner's choice (2026-11-10) and named in the release notes: right after such a change, the bare Undo passes over it to the newest change before it; where that is a change to one day the held-back change cut off, split off or re-placed, the day's undo is refused with its reason, and every later bare Undo stops at the same entry until a newer change is made (other entries undo from the Activity page). Measured at about 8% of drawn Google cases (79 of 1,000, seed 911). It was not so before the day gate, when that undo went through or was offered as "Undo anyway", sometimes on the neighbouring day. 1.5.6 removes most of it by turning whole-series undo on, so the bare Undo takes the series change itself first. Amended 2026-11-14 (1.5.7, #290): 1.5.6 shipped with the setting still off, and it stays off until the rebuild (#274), so the suites now run as an install does. `freshUser` stores nothing for it, the edit harness defaults to off (`--series-undo on` measures the held-back mechanism), and the four database test files that test this undo say so at their top (`seriesUndoDefault(true)`: `google-series-undo`, `google-series-recovery`, `journal-gate`, `link-ownership`). Possible instead: delete the undo path until the rebuild, which removes code every Google change must keep passing but touches Finish and Put back; left for the owner. Costs: the mechanism is measured by four files, by the two validators of the harness's own checks (the oracle probes and the mutation list, which run with the setting on because they plant their damage in this undo and a check is proved only where its mechanism runs) and by harness runs asked for with the flag, no longer by every scenario. Verified: with the default off and before any file opted in, 101 of the 106 database test files passed unchanged and the five that failed were the four above and the harness slice in `edits.test.php`, which follows the harness's default; all five pass after the change.
- **Journal entries carry their format; an older format is log only.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: every journal entry (`mutations.format`) carries the format it was written in; the current one is `Undo::JOURNAL_FORMAT` (2), which `Undo::record` writes, and every entry from before 1.5.5 is format 1. Migration 077 adds the column (default 1, so every row it finds is format 1) and makes every format-1 entry log only, as 058 and 064 made the entries they could not carry over: its snapshots and pieces dropped, `details.notUndoable` "Recorded by an earlier version of Better-Cal; it can't be undone after the upgrade.", its touches and calendar references kept. `Undo::undoById` refuses an older-format entry with 409 `not_undoable` and its reason whatever the force flag (so its redo, an undo's entry, refuses too), the bare Undo passes over it, and Activity lists it as `undoable: false` with the reason in `notUndoable`. Nothing reads an older entry's grounds or snapshots to decide anything, and the readers that served only pre-1.5.5 entries are gone: the 077 rewrite (`Undo::rewriteLegacyGround`, `legacyGround`, `holdsLegacyGround`), the `~` mark a day left on its series and the `col^` one-way ground in `Undo::conflicts`, and in `GoogleUndo` the `local`-list restore of a deleted day (`rowForLegacy`, `restoreLegacy`), the edit's fallback when it kept no `changed` list, and the series undo's refusal of a `before` with no `asked`. The 077 migration is set-based (no row read into PHP), so memory stays flat whatever the journal's size. The rule beyond this case: a change to how anything compared across entries is stored (grounds, keys, a column's form) is a journal format bump, and its migration is that bump: the column's new value for new entries, and the older entries made log only, never rewritten or read two ways. Why: 1.5.5 records a day's field on its series' row (`day:<occurrence>|<field>`) where 1.5.4 recorded the day's own row and column, so an unforced undo of a 1.5.4 entry never saw a later change to the same day (external review R1 of 80a6bd84 and earlier). Two rewrites of the old grounds into today's form failed review: the first read a large deletion's pieces whole and ran out of memory at a 128 MB worker limit; the second opened only entries whose keys looked old, missed an old whole-series move whose keys looked current (its unforced undo then deleted a newer edit), and still grew with the number of edited days (review of 94693e30..cb2c6082, R1 and R2). The owner's principle: no compatibility code for data that ages out in a week; an entry is undoable for 7 days, so an upgrade costs at most a week of undo for changes made before it. Else: rewrite every old entry's grounds (two failed attempts, each needing a reading of every old form to agree with today's); read both forms at undo time (every pair of forms agreeing in every comparison for as long as an old entry can be undone); make older entries force-only (keeps the old snapshots and readers alive for an undo that can't be checked). Verified: journal-format (an entry records the current format; an older entry made log only by 077 with its reason and pieces gone, refusing its undo forced and unforced with the words, listed so in Activity, passed over by the bare Undo to nothing older and to a current change below it), google-local-undo (an older undo's entry: its redo refuses forced and unforced, nothing sent to Google), google-series-undo (an older series entry refuses even forced), the pure suite's 077 check on SQLite (log only with the reason, pieces gone, an earlier 064 reason kept, unreadable details left, a second run changes nothing), and the review's probes. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **An undo entry's snapshots hold exactly the rows its write reaches, each with its links.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: the before and after tables a writer records (`Undo::record`) contain every row its own statements change, read with the same condition the statements use, and the link rows of each (`Undo::linksOf`: tags, people and every reference table), so an undo restores all of it and nothing a write touched is outside the entry. A writer that reaches deleted rows (a CalDAV PUT brings a deleted row of its UID back, or removes it whole) records them too, before and after. Why: two writers broke this in 1.5.4 and earlier. A "this event" edit of an existing day's row recorded the row without its links, so undoing a tag or people change on such a day left the new set; a CalDAV PUT kept only live rows in its before snapshot, so a deleted event a phone put back read as made by the PUT and its undo deleted it for good, links and all. An audit on 2026-11-09 read every site that records an entry (about 50) against this rule; the two were the only breaks, and the remaining copies of "a row and its links" (`Labels::eventLinkRows`, `GoogleUndo::rowTables`) are to be folded into `Undo::linksOf` in 1.5.6 (#241). Else: capture the links per site as each writer sees fit (what produced the two gaps), or derive the links at undo time from the row (an undo would then restore what the links are now, not what they were). Verified: undo-day-identity (a later change to the set a day owns, undone, is the earlier set, tags and people alike, on a calendar here and on a Google calendar), completeness ("an event deleted here, put back from a phone": undone, the row is deleted again with its tags and person, and every row is as it was), and the completeness test's rule that every entry says everything its write changed. Settled 2026-11-09.
- **A day's tags and people follow its series through one function every writer calls.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: the rule is that a live day row holds, as its stored tag and people links, its own set where its own list names `tags` / `people`, and otherwise exactly its series' set as the series has it now. One function keeps it, `OwnFields::follow(db, series, days?, fields?)`: every live day of the series (or the ones named) whose list does not name a field takes the series' set for it, only where the sets differ; a day owning the field and a series gone or deleted are left alone. Every writer calls it in its own transaction once it has made, brought back, re-listed or re-parented a day's row or written a series' links: the link setters themselves (`Labels::setEventTags`, `replaceEventPeople`), so any write to a series' links brings its days along; a "this event" edit here and on Google, an answer for one day, "use the series' value", a whole-series plan and a split (each ending with the series followed whole, which is what Finish now relies on), a CalDAV PUT and an import (each ending with the series followed, the import's entry carrying the links its days took), a poll (every series it changed), an undo or redo (once it has put back what it puts back, every series that any row it touched stands under now: each event a row of its snapshots names, in any table, its tags and people and references included, read from the database after the restore, never from the parent a snapshot names; an undo of a person's delete or merge is no different from one of a tag's, and a link row it puts back onto a day that no longer owns that field is the series' copy, so the day holds exactly the series' set; rows an entry kept in pieces, a calendar deleted whole, come back whole with their series and are not read again), and recovery's store, bringBack, reattach and carryHeld. `Feeds::followSeries` keeps the content fields and calls it for the links; the six inline copies of a series' links onto a day are gone, and so is the plan's per-day tag and people write. Recovery's relink carries a day's link ownership with its links: a set the snapshot's day owned is named in its list again and is that set; a set it did not own is not carried and the day follows. Why: the audit of every write site (workfolder/reviews/audit-155-links.md, about 70 sites) found the rule kept by six copies and two composers, with three writers outside them: a CalDAV PUT made a day with no tags under a tagged series (review of cb2c6082..63a96467, R2), an import into a calendar holding the series did the same, and Finish of a half-done Google change whose series' tags or people changed here since gave the days the planned set while the series kept its own; it also left two shapes it could not settle, a day a poll kept while its series was away and put under the series made anew (it kept the old series' tags), and a deleted series brought back by a PUT. Each was a site someone had not reached. Else: fix the three sites in place (the pattern that produced them); compose links at read time for a day that owns none (a second reading of the same rule); a check in `Undo::write` that follows every touched series (an entry would then not say everything its write changed). What it costs: one extra query per tag or people write on a row that is not a series, and per series a writer touches. The one writer that still left a day's row with no own list is fixed: a poll that makes an edited day whose series' record it does not carry gives it the list a synced row gets against its series' stored master when that master stays (its record skipped as unreadable), and `[]` when no series stays, as migration 063 did for a day with no master. Rows with no own list that every release from 1.5.0 to 1.5.4 could leave that way are given their list by migration 078 (the entry below), and there is one reader of a stored list, `OwnFields::stored`, which every site that reads one calls. Verified: link-ownership (the review's probe, an owned set kept through a PUT and its undo, a deleted series revived by a PUT, an import under a tagged series, Finish with the series' tags and people changed since, Finish and Put back of a rename, a clock change and a split with a day owning its tags, and a table of every writer here, on Google and from a feed, each undone and redone, with the rule asserted after each step by raw SQL over all the account's data), each of which fails on the tree before this change where it names a site; its last block reads `server/src` and fails when a statement writing links or making a row is not named in its inventory. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **An entry is taken back only through one loader, which refuses an older format; Finish asks it before anything.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; completes "Journal entries carry their format". Chosen: `Undo::entryToTakeBack` is the only way code gets an entry to undo, redo or finish undoing. It reads the live row and refuses an older format with 409 `not_undoable` and the entry's reason; with the live row gone it takes the copy a pending undo kept (`plan.entry`) only when the copy says it is in the current format, and a copy without `format` (every copy written before this, and a plan from before copies were kept) is refused, never defaulted. `undoById` (and everything over it: the bare Undo, Activity's Undo and Undo anyway, the toast, redo, a plugin run's undo, a proposal's undo), the series undo's own re-read, and Review's Finish of a pending undo or redo (`GoogleUndo::entryToFinish`) get their entry from it. Finish asks it before the pending row is marked running and before any request to Google, so a refused Finish leaves the row exactly as it was, half done in Review, with nothing sent or written; Review lists such a row with Put back alone and says why. The copy a pending undo keeps now carries `format`. `Undo::restoreSnapshots`, `Undo::apply` and `GoogleUndo::finishUndo` refuse an older entry and treat one without `format` as a journaling mistake: a backstop for a caller that skips the loader, not the gate (on the recovery path they come after Google's writes). Put back is unchanged: it takes no entry back and reads only the pending row; when it refuses because Google changed since and Finish is refused too, its words say so instead of pointing to Finish. Why: the review of cb2c6082..63a96467 (R3) found Finish of an undo stopped before the upgrade completed it at Google, left the rows here as the change made them (077 had dropped the snapshots it would restore from), settled the pending row and said `ok`; Finish loaded its entry with no look at the format, preferring the live row, and the copy had no format to look at. Each earlier fix had reached the reproduced path, so this one is by construction: one loader every take-back path uses, and a check that runs every such path. Else: a format look added in `recoverTx` alone (the next path would be the next finding); reading the copy's format with a default of 1 (refuses every Finish whose entry retention removed, current ones too); Finish at Google alone, as a forward change (completes taking back an older entry, which the rule forbids); a "Leave as Google has it" action for a pending row (new scope, put to the owner). What it costs a person: an undo or redo started before the upgrade and stopped half way can't be finished; Put back takes it back and the change stands, log only. A limitation pending the owner's choice: if the series also changed at Google since, Put back refuses too, and the series stays as it is here, taking no other change, while Review lists it with Put back alone; nothing in the app ends that state yet. A pending row whose plan was written by 1.5.5 code before this change and whose entry retention has removed (90 days) is refused the same way, though that entry was current. A 1.5.4 pending row (no plan) names no entry and is unchanged: Finish completes it at Google as a forward change. Verified: journal-gate, one row per take-back operation (undo by id, forced, the bare Undo, the toast's, a redo, a run's undo strict and not, a proposal's undo, a Google series and a Google day undo, Finish of a pending undo and of a pending redo, each with the live entry and with only its copy left, older, without `format` or with none, and Put back), each built with today's code then made older, with 077 run and with snapshots kept, asserting the code and words, no request to Google of any kind and every journal, link and pending table unchanged; Put back goes through and leaves the entry log only; the reviewer's probe (Finish refuses, the pending row kept, the control restores the original tag); Review's listing; the open case pinned with its words; a check that fails when a public method taking an entry or a pending row is missing from the table; and the mutation `journal-gate-finish-bypass` (Finish loading its entry as before) failing all 40 of its named assertions. Inventory: `workfolder/reviews/audit-155-gate.md` and the worker's report `worker-155-rule3.md`. Settled 2026-11-10. After the sibling hunt (G3): the loader is also asked by `GoogleSeries::finish` itself, the lowest public way to complete an undo, before the row is marked running or Google is asked, so a caller that skips Review's Finish still meets it (bypassing Review's ask alone now trips nothing); it costs one more read of the pending row and the loader asked twice on Review's path. Put back works out its own refusal words the same way, so no caller has to pass them. The check over operations scans every class under `server/src` for a public method taking a pending id, an entry or an entry id (a probe class shows a new one fails it), and two mutations show the gate holds: `journal-gate-loader-open` (Finish gets its entry without the format look: 55 named assertions fail) and `journal-gate-finish-direct` (`finish` called directly without the ask: 12 fail). And (G2) what Review offers that ends in an undo follows one test: an accepted proposal's "Undo, remove these again" is offered only when every entry of its run can be undone now by the test Activity lists by (`Activity::undoability`, whose format part is the loader's `Undo::olderFormat`), and otherwise the item says why in Activity's words; a plugin run's "Undo this run (n)" counts by the same test (`Activity::runUndoability`), so a run that only made rows is counted and an older one is not. Before, Review offered that undo on every proposal accepted before the upgrade or more than 7 days ago, and it always refused without a reason. One answer comes before the loader, by the owner's choice (2026-11-10): an undo of an entry that is already undone answers `already_undone`, whatever version recorded it, because that is the true answer to the request; the flag is one column in every format, so nothing of the entry is read to give it. The first build of the loader answered `not_undoable` there.
- **An instance at Google is its series id, its form and its key, named by one function pair.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (rule 1, step 1). Chosen: `GoogleWriter::instanceId(series id, key, allDay)` is the only way an instance id is made: `<series>_<UTC basic time>Z` for the timed form, `<series>_<YYYYMMDD>` for the all-day form, the date of the key, which under an all-day series is that date at 00:00:00 UTC. It takes no zone. Its inverse `GoogleWriter::instanceOf(id)` gives back the series id (everything before the last `_`), the key and the form, or null for an id that names no instance (a master's, a single event's), and `instanceOf(instanceId(s, k, f))` is `(s, k, f)` for both forms. Two ids are equal exactly when series, form and key are, so a timed occurrence at 00:00 UTC and the all-day day with the same key are two instances. Removed: `GoogleSync::droppedInstance`, which returned the key and dropped the series id and the form, and `GoogleSeries::instanceId`, a second name that fixed the zone at UTC while every other caller passed the master's `tzid`; that pair of names is how two conventions came about. Every caller (the "this event" writes, the move to Google, the poll's `googleIdAt`, `useSeriesValues`, the series steps, the pending gate, the undo of one day, the interleaving harness) goes through the pair. Why no zone: migration 035 (0.9.16) stored every all-day row with `tzid` UTC, keys and skips included, and every writer since does the same, so the two conventions gave the same id on every row that can exist; they differed only on an all-day key stored as a midnight in a zone, which no current writer makes. Else: keep the zone parameter and pick one convention for callers (two readings of one thing stay possible), or return only the key from the inverse (what lets a dropped item and a skip on an equal key read as one). Costs: none for stored data, and behaviour is unchanged; an all-day row stored in a zone, if one existed, would be addressed by its UTC date. This step decides nothing about identity yet: `toParsed`, `applyChanges`, `afterMaster`, `held`, `stepsFor`, `Feeds::sync`, `Feeds::ofSeries` and `GoogleUndo::dayAt` still compare as before and are the next steps. Verified: the unit suite's round trip over both forms (a timed key, a midnight-UTC key in both forms giving two ids, a series id with underscores) and `instanceOf` on ids that are not instances; the database suite, the probe's stand-in test, and the series harness in Google mode and the interleaving mode unchanged. The audit is `workfolder/reviews/audit-155-identity.md` (rows 1 to 6 and 31 to 35, "The all-day zone convention"). Settled 2026-11-10.
- **The link rule is checked over all data after every step, not at the writers someone listed.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. Chosen: one invariant, by raw SQL that calls nothing in `server/src` (`linkViolations` in `server/tests/db/lib.php`): every live day row (a parent and a key, not deleted, its series live) holds, as stored `event_tags` and `event_people` rows, its own set when its own list names `tags` or `people`, and otherwise exactly its series' set as it stands; two companions are reported apart, no live day row with a NULL list (the pre-063 form no writer may leave) and no live day row outliving its series (structure, not links). The database suite runs it after every `scenario()` block, failing the block that left a new violation with the rows named, and after every file as a backstop for code outside blocks; a block that builds an older form on purpose names its reason as `scenario()`'s third argument. The series harness runs the same queries after the case's "this event" edits, the edit, each option committed, Undo, the edit applied again, the second edit and its undos, and every sequence step (`l-links`, `l-list`, `l-series`), in both modes. The interleaving oracle's x-own compares tags and people as sets against the series' row in the same snapshot. Why: every earlier fix of this rule was verified at the sites someone enumerated, and the next review found a writer nobody had listed (a CalDAV PUT making a day under a tagged series, review R2 of 1.5.5); an invariant over all data after every step does not depend on anyone's list of writers. Else: a check per writer (what missed the sibling each time), or a read-time composition of a day's links from its series (a second reading of the same rule). Cost: the database suite reads four queries after each block, a few seconds over the whole run. Verified: `link-rule.test.php` (raw-SQL damage reported kind by kind: a day under a tagged series with no links, without its series' person, holding another tag or a superset, a NULL list, a deleted or missing series; a day owning its tags may hold any set; a block that leaves damage fails its own link check naming the row); the x-own probe that adds a tag to a day that doesn't own its tags (caught); the mutation `answer-day-without-series-links` (the row an answer for one day makes, without the link copy, fails the link check of link-rule.test.php's block that answers one day of a tagged series, and nothing else) and `this-day-without-series-links` (the row a "this event" edit makes, without the copy, trips the series harness's l-links, with f-undo). Settled 2026-11-10.
- **A day row's stored Google id is derived whenever the row is placed, and an undo addresses a day by its id now.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (rule 1, fix list items 6 and 7). Chosen: a day row's `google_event_id` always equals `GoogleWriter::instanceId(series id, its key, the master's form)`, derived through `Feeds::googleIdAt` every time a writer places the row, on a key that moved or not: a poll and Finish (`Feeds::ofSeries`, and through it `GoogleSeries::placeKept`), a change for all events (`SeriesPlans::seriesPlanWriter`, every row the plan writes and every edited day it makes, which until now carried the series' own id until the store's sync replaced it), and an undo placing a day (`Undo::overCurrent`). The undo of a "this event" change (`GoogleUndo::dayAt`) builds the day's id from the series id, the day's occurrence now and the series' form now, and never returns the id the entry journaled. Before, all four derived the id only when the key moved, so a series timed at 00:00 UTC made all-day (or the reverse) kept each day on its key under the old form's id; a day holding only a tag kept `…_20270111T000000Z` where Google now answers `…_20270111`, and its next rename was refused with "This event was deleted at Google; nothing was changed", made here or at Google alike. Else: derive at the moment of sending (the "this event" writes and the undo) and leave the stored id as a hint, which keeps two readings of one thing alive and leaves `rowByGoogleId` finding nothing; or fix the four sites separately per case. Costs: no stored form changes; every place of a row now writes its id, which equals the stored one except across a form change, so no other behaviour moves. The undo of a rename made before a form change at a coinciding key is refused as stale today (the change's own entry touches that day, because the change loses the renamed day, review R1, fixed apart); once R1 is fixed it goes to the date-form id. Verified: google-day-identity (a tag-only day through a flip each way, then a rename to the new form's id; the same flip made at Google and read by a poll; the undo of a tag across a flip; the undo of a rename across a flip never reaching the timed id; invariant I1 by raw SQL at the end of each), 19 of its assertions failing on the tree before, and the mutations `ofseries-id-only-on-key-move` and `dayat-journaled-id`. The audit is `workfolder/reviews/audit-155-identity.md` (rows 25 to 35, invariant I1). Settled 2026-11-10.
- **Provider identity is checked over all data after every step, not at the writers someone listed.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (rule 1, the check side). Chosen: invariants by raw SQL that calls nothing in `server/src` (`identityViolations` in `server/tests/db/lib.php`), the id spelled out from the rule and never asked of `GoogleWriter`: I1, every live day row with a Google id whose master is live and has one stores the id of its key under its master's form (`<series id>_<YYYYMMDD>` all-day, `<series id>_<UTC basic time>Z` timed); I2, no row is all-day in a zone; and I2's key companion, no live day row's key and no skip of a live all-day series has a clock. Each is its own kind and label. The database suite runs them with the link rule through one hook (`dataRulesCheck`, renamed from `linkRuleCheck`) after every `scenario()` block and after every file, new violations only, and one opt-out argument (`scenario()`'s third, now `$rulesOptOut`) covers both rules. The series harness runs the same queries at every point it runs the link rule (`id-google`, `id-zone`, `id-clock`), in both modes. The interleaving mode adds x-identity in `checkStructure`, over the snapshot, skipped only while a change is half done, with its own two-line id function (`Interleave::googleIdOf`). Why: every earlier identity fix was verified at the sites someone listed and the next review found a sibling (audit-155-identity found three writers deriving a stored id again only when the key moved); an invariant over all data after every step does not depend on anyone's list. Else: a check per writer, or I3 alone (the stand-in compared by id), which sees only the stand-in and only the suites that run against it. Cost: four more queries after each block and after each harness step, not timed apart. Opt-outs: none by argument; the series harness's documented legacy case (an all-day series stored at local midnights in its zone, the pre-035 form, built on purpose) now also fails `id-zone`, and `edits.test.php` lists it among the case's expected failures. What the checks cannot see: a row on the right identity for the wrong day, a row with no Google id, and real Google's ids. Verified: `identity-rule.test.php` (raw-SQL damage reported kind by kind and nothing else: a day row on the other form's id in both directions, on another day's id, an all-day series and an all-day day row in a zone, a clock in a day row's key and in a skip of an all-day series; a day row with no id, a deleted day row, a timed series in a zone and a timed skip with a clock are not reported; a block that leaves the three kinds of damage fails its three identity checks under its own name naming the rows, its link checks passing); the x-identity probe that changes one day's stored id to the other form's (caught; 38 probes, none missed); the mutation `all-day-id-in-timed-form` (`GoogleWriter::instanceId` building an all-day id in the timed form fails the identity check of the block that tags one ordinary day of an all-day Google series, and nothing else). On the tree at 397def78 the database suite, the series harness at seed 7 (1,000 cases, both modes) and the interleaving mode at seed 7 (500 cases) found no violation by a writer; none of them reaches the audit's expected I1 failure (a tag-only day of a series timed at 00:00 UTC, the series then made all-day), which the fix workers' cases own. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **A list from Google is merged by identity, whatever its order.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (rule 1, steps 3 and 4). Chosen: `GoogleSync::toParsed` gives every instance item its Google id and its form (`instance_all_day`): a record or a cancelled record from the shape of its `originalStartTime`, a dropped item (cancelled, no `originalStartTime`) from its id through `GoogleWriter::instanceOf`. The parsed shape is in memory only. `GoogleSync::applyChanges`, the merge every poll, every write's answer and every series store goes through, relates each item to a row or a skipped day by series, form and key, by seven rules. (1) One item per resource, the later of two standing, which is the only use of order. (2) The final master of a series is the list's when it has one, else the snapshot's, and its form is the series' form; a cancelled master removes the series. (3) A stored row's identity is its key under the form of the master it was stored under, and a stored skip's the same. (4) A dropped item removes the row and un-skips the skip of its own identity and touches nothing of the other form on the same key. (5) Only the final master's form counts: a stored row or skip of another form leaves whether or not a dropped item names it (a change between timed and all-day made elsewhere), and a live or cancelled record of another form is left out with a log line. (6) Within that form a key has one item, so nothing depends on order. (7) A master's skips are its EXDATE lines, the cancelled records of its form and, in a delta, the stored skips of its form that no live record and no dropped item of that form contradicts; a write's answer is not a delta, as before. The result is a function of the set of items. Restated while building, each as a rule: what an item is comes before what it says (an instance is what Google marks with `originalStartTime` or `recurringEventId`, or a cancelled item under an instance id, and one whose day cannot be read is left out and logged, never read as its series cancelled or as its master); the app's own tombstone (`GoogleWriter::tombstone`, a key and no form) is of the series' form; a series with no master has no form to judge by, so each item acts on its own identity; the size limit is checked on the result and not while the list is walked; the skips are a set, written in key order when the list changes it and left as stored when it does not (`Feeds::sameValue` compares them in order, and the list's order had decided theirs), and a new row is appended in the order of its uid and key. Before: every item was keyed by uid and key. Of a dropped item and a cancelled record on one key the later listed won, so after a change of form the skip sent again in the new form was cleared in the orders that listed the old form's dropped item last; a dropped item removed the row at its key whatever form its id named; stored skips were merged whatever form they were stored under; and a change of form made elsewhere left the old rows and skips unless Google listed a dropped item for each. Else: keep deciding by key and fix an order (dropped items first is right for two items on one key and wrong for a dropped item listed alone in a later delta, and keeps a second reading of identity alive); compare stored Google ids instead of the master's form (a row's stored id is not derived again on every path yet, audit rows 25 to 28, so a stale id would make its row read as the other form's); give the tombstone a form at its callers (a second way to say what the master's form already says). Costs: a record Google lists in a form other than its master's is not stored (the stand-in lists none and real Google is not known to; the log line is how one would show); a whole read's new events are stored in the order of their uids, not the list's, so the titles a poll's Activity entry names come in that order; a series whose skips were stored in another order reads as changed once, with nothing else different, at the next read-back or whole read of it (those have no stored list to keep; a delta that changes no skip leaves the stored order alone); the limit is checked once the list is walked, so a list over it is read to its end before it is refused (the listing's own page budget bounds it). No stored form changes. Verified 2026-11-10: the audit's ten cases, each over every order of its list (seven failed before, four of them only in some orders; all ten give one result, the expected one); `server/tests/google-merge.php`, 50 checks (the id and form of a record, a cancelled record and a dropped item in both forms, and 33 merges each over every order of its list, the whole result compared: a record beside the other form's dropped item, a read-back after a change of form, a change of form made elsewhere, records of another form left out, a row stored under a stale id, the stored skips of a delta with and without its master, the skips' order, an EXDATE line beside a dropped item, a tombstone, a cancelled series, a series with no master, a moved day, an all-day day of a timed series, the limit), 35 of which fail on the tree before; the mutation `merge-dropped-by-key` (a dropped item related by its key alone) fails 17 of them through the mutation runner, which reads them through `server/tests/db/google-merge.test.php`; the unit suite, the database suite, the probe's stand-in test, and the series harness in Google mode and the interleaving mode against the tree before. Where: `GoogleSync::toParsed`, `GoogleSync::applyChanges`, `GoogleSync::mergeSeries`. The audit is `workfolder/reviews/audit-155-identity.md` (rows 3, 4, 7, 8 and 21, "The model for `applyChanges`").
