Settled 2026-11-10. **Rule 7 restated the same day (the round's hunt): which lists say everything about a series' skips.** Was: a list that was not a delta was taken as everything about the skips of each series whose master it carried, "since the master was just sent with every skip as an EXDATE line". That is false for most writes: Google holds a skipped day as a cancelled record of its own, never as a line (probe 2.4), and a write that sends no recurrence gets back the series' own resource, which names no skipped day. So answering a repeating invitation, and undoing that answer, brought every skipped day of the series back until the next poll, and so did undoing an edit made before the event repeated. Now: a list says everything about a series' skips only when it is whole for that series: a whole read, a read-back, or the answer to a write that itself sent the series' recurrence. Any other list (a delta, the answer to a write that sent none, a tombstone) adds and removes skips only by the items it carries, and the stored ones stand. The caller says which: `applyChanges` takes `$whole` with no default, `GoogleWriter::apply` takes `$sentRecurrence` with no default, and `GoogleWriter::sendsRecurrence(body)` is the one reading of what a body sent, so no list clears a skip by saying nothing. Every caller, with what it sent and what its answer carries: the poll's whole read and `GoogleSeries::store` (whole); the poll's delta (not); an insert (`Events::googleCreate`: the body with its recurrence, whole); a PATCH of an event that is no series' master (`Events::googlePatchAll`) and the undo of an edit (`GoogleUndo`): whole only when the body sent the recurrence; one day's write (`Events::googlePatchThis`, `SeriesPlans::useSeriesValues`), the undo of a delete, the day deletes with their tombstones, an answer to an invitation (`Rsvp::answerAtGoogle`) and its undo: not. Else: a default of "not whole" (safe, but a caller that should say whole would then keep skips its write removed, and nothing would make it say); deciding from the answer alone (an answer cannot say what was sent). Costs: a write through `apply` that sends the recurrence with a skip left out while Google keeps that day as a cancelled record (a bound change alone, probe 3.8) is still read as putting the day back until the next poll; no caller does that today. Verified: `server/tests/db/google-answer-skips.test.php` (an answer to a repeating invitation with a day the organizer cancelled, and the answer undone: the day stays skipped and the calendar shows its neighbours only; both failed before), the unit check "the answer to a write that sent no recurrence leaves the stored skips", and the mutation `answer-read-as-whole`. Two things a person or a reader of the data can notice, both accepted: the events of a first read are stored in UID order, not in the order Google listed them; and a series whose skipped days were stored in another order reads as changed once, at the next whole read or the app's next change to it, since the stored list is compared in order.
- **What a master write leaves at Google is predicted kept only by identity and proof.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; since 2026-11-14 the prediction has one reader, the expectation a series step journals before its request, compared with only where the run never read Google after that write ("A step's expectation is what the run last read after it", below), and nothing is sent, kept or said on it (rule 1, the prediction and the steps: fix list items 2 and 5 of `workfolder/reviews/audit-155-identity.md`, its "The model for `afterMaster`"; review R1 of cb2c6082 to 63a96467). Chosen: `GoogleSeries::afterMaster`, the one prediction of what a master PATCH leaves of a series' edited days and skips, keeps a record or a skip exactly when three things hold. S1, identity: its id under the old master's form is the id of an occurrence of the new master under the new form, through `GoogleWriter::instanceId`. An id carries its form, so a change between timed and all-day drops every record and every skip, in both directions, at every clock, whatever keys coincide. S2, Google's own resets as the probe found them: a cancelled record goes with any change of the rule's days (3.7), a status sent makes a surviving cancelled record a live one (3.15), a skip held as an EXDATE line goes with the rule line sent alone (3.16). S3, proof: everything the write changes is of a class the probe saw keep records (the length, 3.2; the bound, 3.8 to 3.10; a weekday added to a weekly rule, 3.7; the start moved along its own pattern, 3.5; the title, notes, place or status, 3.12 to 3.16), and a class it has not seen keep them is predicted dropped. Three classes are on the dropped side for lack of proof, listed in one function (`GoogleSeries::unproven`): **the zone of a timed series, a zone change that moves no instant included (10:00 Los Angeles to 11:00 Denver), predicted dropped pending a live probe (case 3.17)**; the link (Google's `source` has no probe case); and a change of the rule's days other than weekdays added (a new frequency or interval, a weekday swapped while others stay). A class moves to the kept side by leaving that function once a live probe shows it. Proven classes sent together are predicted as each alone, which is an assumption: 3.16 sent the whole body, and no other combination has a case. Probe cases 3.32 to 3.35 (written, not yet run against real Google) ask about the link, a new frequency, a new interval and a rename with a new length in one body. Since Google may keep the days through an unproven class all the same, `rebase` reads what Google holds now against both answers (`afterMaster` with `$ifKept`: everything S1 and S2 allow kept, each with the write's effects), so a kept record whose end followed a new length is the write's own effect either way and the day's own length is sent again; without that, the dropped prediction itself lost a day's own length when Google kept the record (series harness seed 911 case 630, found while building this). `rebase` also reads "the day before" by identity: the row's own before where the plan carries the day's row, else the day at the same key only while Google holds the series in the form the change found it in; across a change between timed and all-day that has landed, a day with no row is read against what the master write left there, never against the day the old form had on that key (west of UTC that is the day before). The steps follow the same rule: `held` leaves out a record in another form than the master read with it (the form the sync reads for every instance item, `GoogleSync::toParsed`'s `instance_all_day`: one reading of an item's identity) and keeps each record's id; `stepsFor` sends each step to the id Google lists the day under, else to the id its key has in the form of the master as read, never the plan's; and `run` sends no step while the master it read back is not in the plan's form, stopping half done (502 `google_write_failed`, "Google kept the series starting ...; this change had ...") so Finish reads again, where before each step answered 404 and was passed over as a day past the 730 Google lists. Nothing stored changes form: a master step's `expect` is still one digest of the predicted state under the same key. A pending row journaled before this for a change between timed and all-day holds the old prediction's digest, so its Put back is refused as it was, and Finish settles it. No exception for Undo: an undo of a series change is a forward change through the same function. Before: survival was read from the new master alone (the date of the key for an all-day master, the instant for a timed one), so a series timed at 00:00 UTC made all-day, or the reverse, was predicted to keep its records and skips; `rebase` then read a dropped record as put back to the series at Google and let the day's own notes and place go, and read a dropped skip as kept, so the skipped day came back, with no word in Activity (R1). Else: add the form term and keep predicting kept for every class the old code kept, which leaves the zone alone and the other two able to lose a day's fields if Google drops them; or journal the digest of the read-back as an answered master step's `expect` in place of the prediction (the audit's proposal, not adopted: it changes what rule B's digest is read from and needs its own model and check); or an exception for Undo (refused: the prediction is one function). Costs: through an unproven class the stand-in keeps the days (real Google is not known), so a change stopped right after its master step on a series with an edited day or a skip has its Put back refused (409 `google_changed_since`) and is settled by Finish; and a day put back to the series at Google between the app's read and its read-back is not noticed there, so its record is made again as the plan had it. A change made at Google in the old form while a change between timed and all-day is stopped before its master write lands has its own entry, the next one. **The same rule for what the condition believes a record holds** (added after the hunt of 20f77fe9): a day's row is read with the series' value for every field it does not own, which is what Google's record holds only where Google writes a series' field into its records. That is proven for the title, notes, place and status (3.12 to 3.15) and a new time resets or drops the records; for the link (`source`) it is not (3.32 asks). So for the link, a record holding either the series' link or the link its row stores is "as stored" (`GoogleSeries::sameAsStored`, the constant `UNWRITTEN`; the sync writes a record's value into its row's column whether the day owns it or not, so the row does keep what the record held). Before, a link given to a series with an edited day left that day's record, which the stand-in leaves without the link, reading as changed at Google: Finish of the change answered `google_conflict` every time, Put back `google_changed_since`, every other change `google_pending`, with no way out; and with no half-done change at all, a link set on the series at Google and read by a poll made every later "this event" edit of the series a conflict for good. The steps still send each such day the series' link, so under each answer real Google may give (leaves the record, writes the link in, drops the record) the series ends as the plan has it. Every reader of "what Google holds for a day's unowned field" was read against this: `exceptionOf` and `stateOf` build what is sent and what is journaled (the series' value, rightly); `sameAsStored` and through it `condition` are the fix; `heldAfter`, `stepsFor`, `differences` and `rebase` read Google's own records or what a step just sent, and hold; the sync keeps a record's link in its row without making the day own it, and holds. Verified: `google-link-unproven.test.php` (the hunt's probe F1: Finish completes with the second write refused or the network down, the series takes a rename and a deletion after, and the poll-side case), the unit suite (hand-built read-backs under each of the three answers, and the controls: another link on the record is a change, an owned link gets no latitude, nor does a proven field), mutation `google-condition-unowned-link-as-series`. Verified: the unit suite (`server/tests/google-series.php`: a form change in each direction at 00:00 UTC, 10:00 Los Angeles and 19:00 New York drops everything; each probe case 3.1 to 3.16 gives the answer it gave; the three unproven classes drop; `held` and `stepsFor` over a list in two forms, in both list orders; `rebase` returns the plan whole across a form change read before the master write, right after it and after every step, and keeps a change made at Google since on the right day); `google-series-identity.test.php` (102 assertions, 47 of them failing before); `google-series-identity-recovery.test.php` (a change between timed and all-day stopped right after its master write is put back, or finished, both directions; the zone alone stopped there has its Put back refused and Finish completes it, which states today's decision and changes when case 3.17 answers); mutation `google-after-master-form-term` (the record's id read in the new form: those 47 fail and no other); the series harness in Google mode at seeds 7 and 911, 1,000 cases each, with no forward-edit failure, and since the sync merges a list by identity none after the harness's whole-series Undo either (seed 7 clean; seed 911 down to cases 407 and 766, the two known Undo-only ones); the interleaving mode at seed 7, 500 cases, clean. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **The stand-in for Google reads whether a date is a day of a rule from the rule, not from its listing.** Status: choice, 2026-11-14 (#278). Chosen: when a series write decides which records are kept ("while still a day of the master as changed", probe D1), `FakeGoogle` reads the rule through the latest record held; its listing still stops at 730 occurrences (probe 7.3). Else: membership by the capped listing, as it was, which dropped a cancelled record years out of an unbounded rule and then answered 400 to the app's cancel of it (seed 14671, Google case 925), a state real Google is not known to reach. Not changed, each unprobed: which days a switch of form hides is still read from the listing, and a write of `status: cancelled` to a dropped record still answers 400 as a write of `confirmed` does (3.48 probed only that one). Verified: the case replayed, and the recorded and the reviewer's seeds in both modes.
- **A change made at Google while a change's first write waits is named, not carried, where the change gives the days other keys.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; restated 2026-11-10 on the wider axis (was: a change of form only, "A change made at Google while a change of form waits is named, not carried"; the restatement is the last part of this entry). (rule 1; `workfolder/reviews/worker-155-rule1a.md`, section 12, with the twelve measured runs). The case: a change of a series between timed and all-day stops with its master write sent and not taken (the network down at that request), so Google still holds the series in the old form; someone then renames a day at Google, deletes one, gives one another time, or changes the notes of an edited day; then Finish. Chosen: in `GoogleSeries::rebase`, exactly when Google's master is in the form the change found the series in and the master as Finish will send it is in the other, nothing Google holds is carried across. The question "did this day change at Google since" is asked in the old form, what Google holds now against the series as the change found it, where each key is one instance on both sides; the plan's days stand whole; and each day changed at Google since is named once (`GoogleSeries::notKept`): "While this change was waiting to be finished, Fri 8 Jan was renamed in Google Calendar. That was lost when the event became all-day. Make it again there if you still want it." The day is named by its date as it showed under the old form (the evening of Fri 8 Jan in New York is Fri 8 Jan, never its key's date), with what was done: renamed, deleted, brought back, given other notes, another place, status or link, given another time (a timed series), moved to another date or given other dates (an all-day one); "now all-day" or "no longer all-day" for the form the series takes. One guard; every other call of `rebase` is as it was. Why nothing can simply be kept: the master write drops every record and skip Google holds, no id of the old form names a day of the new series, and the new form cannot hold every old value (a clock under an all-day series is refused with 400). Before: such a change was written into the plan under the same key in the new form. Measured: west of UTC (daily at 19:00 New York made all-day) a rename or a deletion landed on the next day with no word, and a change to the edited day's notes became two edited days; an all-day series made timed sent every such change to an id that is no day, lost it, and said so in sentences naming a day and a time the series does not have; at 10:00 Los Angeles made all-day it landed on the right day only because the key's UTC date is the local date there, under two sentences that contradicted each other; and a day given its own time under a change to all-day was sent as a clock to an all-day instance, so Finish failed with 400 every time while Put back was refused, with no way out in the app. Else: carry each change to its day by the series model's image (`Feeds::ofSeries`, as the sync places a row under a changed series), which keeps a rename or a deletion the plan does not touch; not built now because that image can disagree with the planner's own carrying where the plan also changes the rule or the time, it needs a collision rule, and it would ship without the generator reaching it; it can follow without undoing this, since it only narrows which changes get the sentence. Or refuse Finish while such a change stands: rejected, since Put back is refused there too (the series changed at Google since), which would leave the change with no way out. Costs: a change made at Google in that window (a stop with the master write sent and not taken, and a change of form in the same plan) is dropped with a word and has to be made again; the sentence says what kind of change, not its value. Changes to fields only Google holds (a colour, guests) on such a day are dropped by the same master write and not named, as `rebase` names none anywhere. Not covered: the series' own time changed at Google in that window; the master's three-way then keeps Google's time, the form is not changed, and the days are read as before. Verified: the unit suite (`server/tests/google-series.php`: three clocks, both directions, a day renamed, deleted, given another time or date, and the plan's edited day given other notes, each expecting the plan whole and the one sentence by date; a skipped day brought back and several things done to one day; all 25 fail on 38635cc2; the eighteen "the plan stands whole" reads unchanged); `google-series-form-finish.test.php` (the twelve measured runs through the stand-in: Finish completes, exactly the master and the plan's edited day are written, Google and the rows hold the plan alone by date, the exact sentence, a poll after changes nothing); mutation `google-rebase-carries-by-key-across-forms` (the guard removed: 38 of the test's assertions fail, in every run its words and writes, in the runs made all-day what Google and the rows hold, and "Finish completes" on the two own-time runs made all-day). **Restated on the wider axis** after a hunt of 20f77fe9 proved the form was one case of it (a new clock time, a zone change, a drag along the pattern and a "this and following" rename, each stopped with its first write sent and not taken and a day changed at Google meanwhile: a deletion came back, a rename was lost under "left as Google has it", a step went to an id Google no longer held and its 404 was passed over, and under a drag two days along the pattern the deletion landed on the day carrying the plan's notes, which went with it unnamed). The axis is not the form: it is that the change's first write has not reached Google, so Google holds the series keyed as the change found it. Chosen: `rebase` reads what changed at Google since in that keying (`keying`: form, zone, start, the rule's days; never the bound or the length), Google now against the series as found. Where the master as Finish will send it keys the days as Google does, a day changed at Google is carried exactly on its key, by rule A as before. Where it keys them otherwise (`rekeyed` gives the reason a person reads), nothing is carried by key and each such day is named with the same sentence form and a true reason: "because the series is now all-day", "is no longer all-day", "the series' zone changed", "the series' time changed", "the series moved to another day", "the series' rule changed". A key that is no day of the series as it will be, where the plan puts nothing (past a new end; a day a split hands to the series that follows), is never carried in this series and never sent to: a change made there is named "That was lost when this change ended the event before that day", or, where the change is a split and the day lives on in the series that follows, "That was lost when this change split the event before that day". So no step of a Finish is built for a key of another keying or for a key that is no day of the series, which the tests assert by asking Google about every id written to. `run` still passes any 404 on a step over, as it did: stopping the change on one was tried and withdrawn, since it turned a wrong key the whole-series undo sends for a series anchored before a skipped hour (fixed case `anchored-start-before-a-skipped-hour`: a step an hour off the series' day, behind `google_series_undo`) from a step passed over into an undo left half done with every Finish stopping the same way; that key is the undo planner's, for 1.5.6. And once a master write that moves a row onto another key has landed, a day deleted at Google on that key since stays deleted and is named ("... was deleted in Google Calendar while this change was waiting to be finished; it stays deleted."), where before it was kept in silence with the plan's edited day gone (the test for "the change changes this day" asked only about the row's own fields, not whether the change puts a row on that key). Why not carry across a re-keying: the target holds the new key only of the rows the plan carries, an ordinary day's new key is the planner's mapping, and a guess by date or by shift is what put the deletion on the wrong day. Not built, by the same reasoning: a change made at Google on a day a split hands to its new series is named rather than carried into that series, because a part that kept its keys and a part dragged along its pattern have the same master and only the planner tells them apart; and any change of the rule's days names, though adding a weekday keeps every existing key. Else: carry by the series model's image or by the plan's own moves (filed with Option 1); carry a split's days into its new series when the part starts at the first day past the old series' new end (a test on the two targets together, in `finish`, which is in flight elsewhere). Costs: as above, the change made at Google is dropped with a word. Verified: the unit suite (each of a new time, another zone, a drag and a new rule names with its reason and leaves the plan whole, and says nothing when nothing changed; a rename and a new length carry exactly; a shorter series names a change on the day it drops and carries one on a day it keeps; a landed drag keeps and names a deletion on the edited day's new key); `google-finish-first-write.test.php` and `google-finish-drag.test.php` (the hunt's probes F2 and F3 as tests, by date; where the rule names instead of carrying they assert the sentence, the plan whole, nothing on a neighbouring day and nothing sent to an id Google does not hold); three fixed cases of the interleaving mode under the stand-in's sent-and-not-taken stop (a new time, another zone, a "this and following" rename); mutation `google-rebase-rekey-guard-form-only` (the guard read for the form alone: the three sentences of the new-time rows change their reason). **Known limits of 1.5.5, on the same paths.** Put back does not restore what only Google held on one day (a colour, a guest) when the change's master write dropped that day's record, and it names nothing: the befores it sends hold only the fields a column here holds, and the digest it checks against is of the series after the master write, with the record already gone. The uninterrupted change loses the same by Google's own behaviour; what is wrong is the claim of a complete reversal. Telling what was lost needs the Google-only fields of each record journaled with the master step, a stored shape 1.5.5 does not add. No check in the suite shows this limit: the hunt first saw it as x-putback on interleaving seed 73129 case 200, which is clean since that check's reading of "changed at Google since" was corrected, and the probe that pins it by hand (a colour or a guest on one day, a master write that drops the record, a stop, Put back) is not in the suite, since it fails by design until the journal holds those fields. And Put back is refused (409 `google_changed_since`) when nothing of the change reached Google and Google changed since, though there is then nothing to reverse and nothing of Google's to overwrite; Finish is the way out, and completes. Closing such a change from Put back is written out in `workfolder/reviews/worker-155-rule1a.md`, section 17, and left for after 1.5.5. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **A record from elsewhere is for the row of the same day.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (rule 1; `workfolder/reviews/audit-155-identity.md`, point 2, row 24 and fix item 8). Chosen: one function, `Feeds::standing(day rows, series as stored, series as the list brings it)`, says which stored row each record of a list is for, and every list rows are matched against asks it: `Feeds::sync` (an ICS feed, a Google delta, a whole read, a recovery's store) and `CalendarBackend::putObject` (a CalDAV client's PUT). While the series keeps its form, timed or all-day, a row stands at its own key, because the key is the instance's name at the other end as well (Google's instance id is the series, the form and the key; a RECURRENCE-ID is a date-time or a date), so nothing changes for a list that changes no series' form. When the list changes the form, the names of the two forms are disjoint even where keys coincide, so a row stands at its image under the incoming series (`Feeds::ofSeries`), or nowhere when the series no longer has its day; the record at a key is given to the row standing there, and a row at an equal key whose image is elsewhere follows its own image. Rows that leave their keys let go of them (`Events::parkInstanceKeys`) before any record is written and end with the key they are given, or removed. The sync's second question, whether the list carried a record for a row, reads the set of rows the first loop gave a record to instead of looking the row's key up in the list again, and a row's image is read from the key it had before the poll. Why: a timed key is a UTC instant and an all-day key a date at 00:00 UTC; at UTC and east of it the two are one local day, and west of it the timed one is the evening before, so across a change of form the row at an equal key is the day before or after. Matched by the equal key, a record took that row with its tags, people, reminders and own list (shown on the base tree: a reminder of 7 January ended on 8 January with the 8th's notes; an ICS feed's override took the tagged row of the day before; a CalDAV PUT moved one day's tag to the next and deleted the other's). Else: match by image for every change of a series (it would change which row a record takes after a clock or zone change within one form, where the key is still the instance's own name at the other end and today's behaviour is not known to be wrong); or keep the equal key and repair afterwards by moving what is held here to the right day (two rows touched per day and a second reading of the same question). Costs: a series whose form changes has its moving rows written twice in one transaction (key to NULL, then its key); a series the poll leaves because one of its records was unreadable is not placed, as it follows no other change of its master either. Not covered, filed: an import over a series already in the calendar in another form (`CalendarImport` adds, never updates). An undo or redo across a change of form, which read its entry's old key under the new form (west of UTC a tag of the 6th came back on the 7th), has its own entry, "An undo acts on a day only where the journal says exactly where it is". Verified: `server/tests/db/sync-form-match.test.php`, whose expectations are written from the rule with its own key function and read by SQL (a Google series at 19:00 New York, at 00:00 UTC and at 09:00 Tokyo, made all-day and back, with and without records in the new form, by a delta and by a whole read; an ICS feed and a CalDAV PUT west of UTC in both directions, and the PUT's undo; two rows of one day with a record of that day; an unchanged feed and a same-form PUT as controls): 9 of its first 159 assertions failed on the tree before the fix, each one a record in the new form meeting rows stored in the old one: eight west of UTC, with one day's data on another day, and the ninth a new row made for the record where this rule gives it to the day's first row; none after. `server/tests/sync-standing.php` (the function alone, every expected key written by hand: both directions west of UTC, at UTC, at 09:00 and 19:00 Tokyo, the form kept, a day cut off, a day skipped, two rows of one day). `server/tests/db/sync-form-recovery.test.php` (a change of form made here west of UTC, in both directions, stopped right after its master write and stopped with the edited day's write taken and its answer lost, then Finish, and separately Put back: the tag and the notes are each on their own day after the stop, after Finish and after Put back, which leaves the rows and Google as they were). A stored skip of the old form is not kept on the new master when a delta lists the master alone (`GoogleSync::applyChanges`, only the final master's form counts), so it cannot hide the neighbouring day and send that day's row away: `sync-form-match.test.php` builds that list by hand, the stand-in having no setting that leaves the dropped item out. The mutations `sync-match-by-equal-key` (the lookup back at the equal key: fails the sync's five assertions about a record in the new form and no control) and `sync-match-by-equal-key-in-a-store` (the same patch under a recovery's store: the stops with a record taken fail, Put back is refused, and the stops without one pass); the database suite's counts unchanged in every other file; the series harness in both modes with no failing check (seed 7, 1,000 cases); the interleaving mode, which polls after most steps, with the same summary line for line before and after (seeds 7 and 307, 500 cases). Settled 2026-11-10. **Which day a row is: by identity first, by the day second (the round's hunt, the same day).** Was: `Feeds::ofSeries` asked whether the row's key was among the series' occurrences on the local day the key had under the series as it was. A zone rewritten at the other end with no instant moving (a daily series at 19:00 New York republished as 00:00 UTC or 09:00 Tokyo) changes that local day and nothing else, so a day holding only a tag was carried to the neighbouring occurrence and given its Google id, though its own key was still an occurrence and still the same instance at Google. Now: a key that still names an occurrence of the series, in the form it was written in, is that occurrence, whatever local date it had under the old zone: the row stays at its key, or is gone when that occurrence is skipped. Only a key that names no occurrence any more is carried to the series' one occurrence on the day it showed, and is gone when there is none. A change between timed and all-day changes every name, so it goes by the day as before (`standing`). This is what real Google was seen to do with a record when the start moves along its own pattern (probe 3.5: kept at its instant), and what the series model says of a zone change at the same moment (every row keeps its instant). Callers: the sync's removal loop, `GoogleSeries::whereNow` (store and the recovery helpers through it), `GoogleSeries::placeKept` and the series undo's check of removed days now keep a row at its key through such a rewrite; `standing` calls it only across a change of form, and the callers that give no series as it was (the undo's placements, the model's) get the answer they got, since the day a key has under the series itself is the day the series yields it on. Else: compare instants only when the zone changed (a branch for one case of the rule). Costs: a rule several times a day whose key is skipped no longer moves the row to the day's one other occurrence (it is gone, as on a rule once a day); a daily series moved between two zones exactly 24 hours apart at the same clock keeps a row on the instant, which is the neighbouring local day (the other end names the instance by its instant too). Verified: `server/tests/db/sync-zone-same-moment.test.php` (Google under both settings for a zone alone, and an ICS feed: the tag stays on its occurrence and its Google id; five checks failed before), six pure checks in `server/tests/google-merge.php` ("which day"), and the mutation `ofseries-day-before-identity`.
- **An undo acts on a day only where the journal says exactly where it is.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (rule 1; `workfolder/reviews/worker-155-rule1d-match.md`, the fourth pass). Was: "An entry's key names a day under the series it was written in", the image by form and zone, built 2026-11-10 and changed the same day because an independent read found it placing wrongly (`workfolder/hunt/`, six probes with hand-written days). That reading took a series' form and zone for what a key meant, so it could not tell a zone change that keeps every instant from one that keeps the clock (after the first, the redo of a delete went to the neighbouring occurrence, where the equal key had been right), and it placed a day with no row by its calendar date while the planner carries a day with a row by its own mapping (a daily series dragged a day later, 23:00 moved to 02:00, Tuesday moved to Wednesday: the same redo landed on two different days depending on an unrelated tag). A reading that works out where old data belongs can write the wrong thing without a word; a gate cannot. Chosen: an undo or redo that acts on one day of a series asks one function, `Undo::dayNow`, where that day is, and it answers a key only where the journal fixes it exactly: (1) no later change re-placed the series' days, so the key is the day; (2) the entry's own row for that day is still there (a day row at that key in its snapshots, or the row a Google entry about that day is recorded on, live and still this series'), so the day is that row's key now: the planner and the sync carry a row with its id, and a zone change at the same moments leaves its key and records no move, which is why this stands beside the next; (3) every re-placing change since recorded a move from the day's key, which is followed, or, for a delete's own skip alone, left it standing: the entry left the day skipped, nothing since was moved onto that key or named its skip, and the skip is still in the series' list. After any of the three the key must be an occurrence of the series as it stands, exactly. Otherwise the undo is refused before anything is written or sent, forced or not. Nothing is placed by an equal key across a re-placing change and no image is worked out in `dayNow`. A change re-places a series' days (`Undo::replacing`, `carriesDays`) when its grounds on the series' row name the zone (`tzid`, whichever reading: only the start's instant is kept, later days follow the new zone's clock, so the equal key is right in January and wrong in April), the form (`all_day`), a day it carried (`move:`), a day it brought into being by date (`added:<date>`, `added><date>`: the planner's word that its reading did not keep every day on its date, which "move every 15th a month later" records while every column of the series stays as it was), the start (`start_utc`) unless the planner kept every day on its date (bare `added`) at the same clock, which is a bound, or the rule (`rrule`) unless the planner kept every day on its date or the entry's own snapshots show the pattern as it was (a bound). A poll keeps no row of the series as it was, so its change of start or rule re-places. A length, a bound, a calendar and every field re-place nothing. Checked against the planner before building: a rule change offers "Move edited and skipped days with their events, as a drag would" (preselected), and a monthly member can be moved a month with the start, the zone, the form and the rule all unchanged, so `rrule` alone could not be left to the exact-occurrence check; the planner's own record of whether it kept every day on its date is what tells. The refusals, all `not_undoable`, the contract's code for what `force` does not pass (the app answers `stale_undo` with "Undo anyway", which would loop): `reason: day_unplaced`, "Can't undo "{change}". This repeating event changed between timed and all-day after that, and Better-Cal can no longer tell which of its days the change was on. Make the change again on the day you meant." ("changed its time zone", "was moved to another time or day", "changed how it repeats" or "had its days moved" by the re-placing change's own grounds; "redone" for a redo); `reason: day_gone`, "That day is no longer part of the series this change was made in (a later change ended the series before it, moved it into another series, or changed how it repeats), so this can't be undone." The gate is asked before staleness everywhere (`Undo::undoById`, `Undo::apply`), so a gated undo never answers as stale. On a calendar here the gate is the one definition for H3 to H5, not staleness: a snapshot is written as it was, so a row it brings back or a skip it restores comes back only where nothing re-placed its series since (`Undo::snapshotStands`: "Can't undo "{change}". This repeating event ... after that, so the saved copy of that day no longer fits it. Make the change again on the day."), with no composition; a row brought back stands in the calendar its series is in now (a move of calendar re-places nothing); a row is not brought back to a key the series no longer has (the exact-occurrence check, a rule change and a bound included); and the undo of a re-placing change itself is refused once a later change touched a day or a skip of that series (`Undo::noDaysSince`, `reason: days_since`: "Can't undo "{change}" yet. Days of this repeating event were changed after it. Undo those newer changes first, or change the time back by hand."). `Undo::staleFor` compares grounds by key along recorded moves, as before the image. The undo and redo of a day's own time asked the forward edit's refusal before anything is sent (`Events::refuseTimedUnderAllDay`, `google_all_day`), where Google's raw 400 reached the person; that refusal went on 2026-11-13 ("One day of a repeating event on Google may take the other form"). Kept from the image: `GoogleUndo::dayBack` hands the row it brings back at the day's key now, which is `dayAt`'s exact answer; the old key read against the series as it stands was an image by date. Removed: `Undo::writtenIn`, `framesSince`, `keyFrame`, `carry`, `conflictsByDay`, the composition in `snapshotOnto`, `Calc::dayOf`, the `$day` of `Feeds::ofSeries`. What a person loses, a changed behaviour: the undo or redo of a change to a day that had no row and no skip when its series' time, start day, zone or form later changed is refused, in every zone, where before this round it went through (right at some clocks, on a neighbouring day at others; `undo-day-identity.test.php`'s first scenario asserted the redo of a delete landing two hours later on the same day, and now asserts the refusal). On a calendar here, "Undo anyway" no longer forces a change that brings a day's row or skip back across such a change, nor the change of time itself once days were changed after it; five assertions in `undo.test.php` and one in `google-series-undo.test.php` that expected `stale_undo` for these now expect `not_undoable`. And after a change of start or rule made at Google the same undo is refused though the day may be where it was. What stays: every undo with no such change in between; a day whose row is still there; a day or a skip whose move was recorded. Else: keep the image and mend its two readings (it guesses, and the next reading it gets wrong is silent); or let staleness cover a calendar here (force passes staleness, and H5 is force). Filed for the owner, not built: a re-placing change made here could record, when it is made, a move for every day an earlier entry still stands on (the planner has the mapping then), which would let those undos go through exactly; and a stored form and zone on each entry. Both are journal questions. The whole-series undo on a Google calendar asks the same `dayNow` (no second reading for it). Verified: `server/tests/db/undo-day-gate.test.php`, the hunt's probes as tests with their hand-written days (each lands on that day or is refused with nothing written and nothing sent, never another day): 108 of its 520 assertions failed on the tree before the image, none after the gate; `undo-form-day.test.php` rewritten to the rule (2,064 assertions: the cells that were "lands on its own day" for a day with no row are refusals, forced too; the days with a row or a recorded move still land); the mutations `undo-gate-places-a-day-by-its-key` (49 named assertions fail) and `undo-snapshot-back-across-a-re-placing-change` (20); the interleaving check `x-day` restated from the rule with three probes; the gates of the round. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **An import leaves out what was deleted here before, and says so.** Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-10, built for 1.5.5; it replaces "An import brings back a deleted event of the same UID, as a CalDAV PUT does", built earlier the same day and never released. Chosen: importing a file into a calendar, each record is matched by UID and key against the calendar's rows, deleted ones included. A live row is already there and is left as it is (counted as `alreadyThere`). A record whose row is deleted, or whose series is deleted in that calendar, is left out and counted as `deletedBefore`; the import's answer carries the count, the app says "N left out because you deleted them here before", and the Activity line of an import that brought anything in says "N left out, deleted here before". Nothing deleted is changed, and the import's entry holds only the rows it made, so its undo removes exactly those. Why: deleting was the person's own act and a file cannot know of it; an old export imported again should not quietly undo deletions. The first version brought the deleted rows back on the precedent of a CalDAV PUT, where a client names the one event it writes; an import is a whole file, and the owner chose not to re-add from it. What it fixes either way (an independent hunt, F2): the lookup did not say whether it meant live rows, so a deleted series counted as already there and a day of the file went in as a live day under the deleted series; and a record deleted here was counted as there, with no word. Costs: a person who wants a deleted event back from a file has to undo the delete (within the undo window) or import into another calendar. Else: bring it back (built, reversed); ask in Review for each previously deleted event (the owner's suggestion, filed, not built). Siblings, every lookup by UID: `Feeds::sync` reads every stored row, deleted ones included, and brings a deleted row back when the feed carries it (a feed is the source of its calendar; follows); a CalDAV PUT brings back the event it names (follows); the mail ingest counts a deleted event as there on purpose (follows); Review's invite gate names a deleted series on purpose (follows); plugins' sync reads live rows and inserts a row of no key beside a deleted one, which the key allows (follows); quick add has no such lookup. Verified: link-ownership ("importing a series and its edited day after the series was deleted ...", "importing an event deleted here ..."), the first failing on the tree before the hunt's fix. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **An event made out of another keeps where it came from, and an import says where a skipped day lives now.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.9 (#255); the link is the owner's ruling, 2026-11-16 ("track more than less, as long as the overhead is not significant"), made general the same day, as a base for tracing an event's changes later (#247, #248, #265, none of them built). Extends "An import leaves out what was deleted here before, and says so", whose words it changes. The import: whether a record of the file was taken out of this calendar before is answered by one function (`CalendarImport::importIcs`, `$takenOut`), which says how: removed (the record's own row deleted, its repeating event deleted, or, for a day, its key among the stored repeating event's skipped dates with no live event linked to it), moved or kept (the key is skipped and a live event of the account links to that repeating event and day, `how` moved or kept). Before, the import read only the first two, so a file holding the repeating event and an edited version of a deleted day brought the day back as a live row on a date the event still listed as skipped. A day of the file under a repeating event the calendar has is read under that event as it stands (`Ics::under`), as mail and feeds read one. Words: removed, "N left out, removed from this calendar before" (the app: "N left out because you removed it/them from this calendar before"; were "deleted here before"); moved, by the calendar the days are in now, "1 left out because it is now in 'Work'", "2 left out because they are now in 'Work'"; kept, "1 left out because it is now an event of its own", "2 left out because they are now events of their own". The link's model. Stored: three nullable columns on `events` (migration 081, which only adds them): `came_from_uid`, the source's uid; `came_from_key`, the day's key where the new event was one day of the source, exactly as the source's skip stores it (a UTC instant, or a date at 00:00 UTC for an all-day one), else null; `came_from_how`, one of a closed set (`Events::CAME_FROM_HOWS`). The uid, not the row id: the import matches a file by uid, a uid names the event to a CalDAV client and in an export, and it outlives the row (a repeating event moved whole keeps it). Written: through one function, `Events::cameFrom`, by each writer that makes an event out of another, after `copyForChild` (which clears it, so a day row or a series' own row made from another never carries one): `detachToCalendar`, moved, key the day (both entries: an ordinary day, a day with its own row); `SeriesPlans::seriesPlanWriter`, kept, from the series as it was before the write, key the day (a row it detaches: that row's key; a row it creates for the day acted on: that day's key read from the request under the series); `SeriesPlans::splitWriter`, split, key the day the later part starts at (the day acted on, under the series it was cut from), which names where the cut was; `copyTo` through `create`, copy (another calendar) or duplicate (the same calendar), key the day where one day of a repeating event was copied (its own row or not), else null. A second hop links to its immediate source (a moved day copied: the moved event, key null). Writers that correctly write nothing: a day row made by an edit (`ensureOverride`, the local and Google one-day edits) and the rows a plan makes edited days, which are part of their series, not events out of it; the series' own row; an event created from a request, quick add, mail, Review, a plugin, a feed, Google, CalDAV or an import file, which is made out of no event here; moving across the Google boundary, which the client does as a copy and a delete (the copy is linked, the delete is not a writer); a move of an event with no series to another calendar, a column update of the same row (the link it has is kept, as on a second move, and a move back to the original calendar keeps it too; what that move should do is not decided here). Read: the import (above) and the full record (`cameFrom`: uid, day, how, and the source as it stands, so a client can name it). Cleared: nothing on the row; it goes with the row. An undo of each writer removes the row it made, or restores the row it changed from its snapshot (a detached day goes back to its series with no link); a local undo records no entry of its own, so there is no redo, and making the change again writes the link again. No row is rewritten and no link is inferred for older events (not from the Activity entry either): a day taken out before the upgrade stays indistinguishable from a deleted one and counts as removed. Else: count a skipped day apart with no link (unknowable); read the change's undo entry (gone after its window); the source row's id; a link for moves alone (the first version of this entry). Costs: three nullable columns, one lookup by account per skipped day an import meets, and one per full record that has a link. Upgrade loses: nothing; events made before it carry no link. Siblings of the import's check: a file into a new calendar and every file of a zip meet no stored rows (follows); a feed poll writes its own skipped dates, the feed being the source of its calendar (follows the earlier entry); a CalDAV PUT replaces the whole object it names (follows the earlier entry); an emailed change to one day is held in Review or gated on the organizer (`MailIngest::applyImipEvent`), its own mechanism, listed. Verified: import-skipped-day (removed days of three shapes, moved days counted by calendar in the singular and plural beside a moved day whose event was deleted, kept days in the singular and plural, the link from each writer and both entries of a move, cleared by undo of each, written again, kept on a second move, a second hop to the immediate source, and `cameFrom` served); the edit harness's untouched check now allows the three columns on a row that leaves its series (`PlanUntouched::DETACH`), which it flagged in both modes until then.
- **Why a day is skipped: the model, not built.** Status: planned, 2026-11-16, the owner's ask; stopped after the model and the inventory, because routing every writer through one function reaches the series engine. The model: beside `exdates_json`, whose form and readers stay as they are, one nullable column on the repeating event's row (`skip_reasons_json`), a map from a skipped key to `{how, by, at}`: how (deleted here, moved, kept, an outside calendar app over CalDAV, Google Calendar, a feed, an import file), by what (the client, calendar or file where known), when. One function writes a row's skips and their reasons together by comparing the list before with the list after: a key added gets this write's reason, a key kept keeps its reason, a key gone loses it; so the same function adds, removes and replaces, and a writer that sets a whole list (a poll, a PUT) needs nothing else. A key with no reason reads as not recorded, never inferred. Undo restores both columns from its snapshot, as it restores every column. Why not built in part: a writer left out would leave a reason on a key it removes, and a later skip of the same key by another unrouted writer would show the old reason as this one's, a silently wrong answer; so it is all writers or none. Every place that adds or removes a skipped date, and what each takes: `Events::deleteThis`, `deleteOverrideInstance`, `detachToCalendar` (add one key: one call each, how deleted or moved, no risk); `Events::createLocked` (a copy of a series carries its list: one call, how copy, the reasons copied from the source or none); `Feeds::columnsOf` callers, `Feeds::sync` for feeds and Google polls, `Dav\CalendarBackend::putObject`, `CalendarImport` (set a whole list: one call at each write, how feed, Google, CalDAV or import file; the Google poll path is the other worker's area); `GoogleUndo` (sets a list on an undo of a Google one-day change: one call, how by the change undone; Google paths); `Undo` snapshot restore and its edit of a before-snapshot's list (`Undo.php`, the skips list rewritten for an undo: the reasons must be rewritten with it by the same keys); `SeriesPlans` (the writer applies the plan's master columns once: one call at that write, but its how must come from the plan's own outcome per key, deleted, kept or moved by the change, which the plan does not name per skip today: a change in `Series\Model`'s writes); `Series\Split` (the old part's and the new part's lists are cut from one: the reasons must be cut by the same keys, a change in `Split`); `GoogleSeries` (a new or replaced series' list from Google's answer: one call per store, Google paths). The engine items (Model, Split, SeriesPlans) and the Google items are what the owner's rule reserves; the rest are one call each. Next step, on the owner's yes: the column in a migration of its own, the function, and every writer above in one change with a check that fails on a writer that bypasses it.
- **What a store of a half-done change takes from a row is given back by recovery.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5. The rule, as it applies to recovery: a change, however it ends, leaves every day holding exactly what it held here alone before the change began, wherever the day still is and still shows what it showed; what it cannot keep it names. Finish and Put back are not exempt because a store ran in between: what a store of the half-done change took from a row is the change's own passing effect, never something that happened at Google. The one thing a store takes from a row that stays is the coordinates of its place, which go when the place text changes (`Feeds::followed`, and the sync's record path), and with them the day's ownership of a place it held by coordinates alone; a row left holding nothing goes whole and comes back whole from the snapshot, and tags, people, reminders, an answer, an icon and what refers to a row are never written by a store. Chosen: one decision, `GoogleSeries::placeBack(the snapshot's row, the row now)`, for every way a snapshot's row comes back: left standing (Finish's `rewind`, on the rows it puts back before the change's writer runs, and Put back's `placesBack`, once Google's state is stored) or made again under another id (`relink`). A row showing the snapshot's place text has the snapshot's coordinates and where they came from; a row showing another text keeps what it has (A1: they belong to the place it shows now); and so does a row whose coordinates a person or a client placed here since for the same text, as every field held here alone changed since is kept (a lookup's are not kept over the snapshot's: the geocode sweep fills what a store emptied). Before, `rewind` gave a standing row back the columns a sync writes, its place text among them, and left the coordinates as the store had left them, so a day that stood through the stores for a tag or its reminders showed its own place again with no map spot and no word (seed 61349 case 272, seed 60611 case 153); Put back had the same fault where the row stood, and lost the series' own coordinates when a new place for all events was put back. Else: keep the sync from emptying them while a change is half done (a second reading of a place inside the sync, for one caller); record what each store took in the pending row (a stored form, for one group of columns); or let the snapshot win always (a place picked here since would go back to the earlier pick). Costs: a pick made here since is told from a store's effect by where the coordinates came from, so coordinates copied in with no source would read as a store's leftovers and go back to the snapshot's; no write reaches such a row today, the series taking none while its change is half done. Verified: google-series-recovery-held (every kind of thing held here alone, a tag, a person, reminders, an answer, an icon, coordinates, a trip link, a duplicate decision and plugin data, on a row that stood through the stores and that the plan moves, by Finish and by Put back; the two seeds' shapes stopped after the master write and with its answer lost; a new place for all events overwriting the day's text at Google; a row a store removed; a day past a new end, put back; the series' own coordinates, put back; a lookup filling the place while half done; a pick made here since), the interleaving fixed cases `a-day-tagged-then-given-coordinates-a-place-for-all-then-a-flip-stopped-after-its-master-write-then-finished`, the same `-then-put-back` and `a-day-with-reminders-then-its-own-place-with-coordinates-a-time-change-whose-second-answer-was-lost-then-finished`, and the mutations `finish-leaves-what-a-store-cleared` and `put-back-leaves-what-a-store-cleared`. Where: `GoogleSeries::placeBack`, `placesBack`, `rewind`, `relink`, `putBack`. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **Finish's entry records what the change did with the days by the function the commit uses.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5; completes "Finish's ground is what the change it completes changed" and "The days a change brought into being are its ground". The rule: a series change's entry records, as its grounds, what it did with each day (a move for every row and every skip whose key it moved, none for a key that stays, and the days it added) by one function, whether the change completed in one go or was completed by Finish. Chosen: `Undo::planGrounds(series, the plan's writes)` is the planner's record as grounds (each skip's move, the days added), `Undo::changeGround(before, after, that record)` is an entry's ground (what the snapshots show, read with the record so a skip the plan carried is its move and never two skips changed, then the record itself), and every entry of a change for all events goes through both: the commit here, the commit on a Google calendar, an undo's or redo's plan, and Review's Finish, which reads the record from the plan its pending row kept (`plan_json`: the change's kind, its series and the planner's writes, `skipMoves` and `added` among them, so it lacks nothing the commit has) and the rows before from the same pending row (`local_json`). A row's move needs no record: rows keep their ids through a recovery, so the snapshots show it. The rows Finish leaves are read as the commit reads them: those of every series the pending row named, and of every series and event of its own the change inserted, by the uid Google gave each when Finish ran; before, they were named from the pending row as it stood when Finish began, which holds no uid for a series not yet inserted, so a split stopped before its insert left the new series out of the entry, and a day kept as an event of its own read as removed with its tags. Before, Finish's ground was the rows' difference alone, so a skip the change carried read as the old key and the new key both changed, and the undo of an earlier delete of that day was refused "Newer changes exist for this item" after a change finished from Review, where the same undo went through after the same change in one go (seed 61349 case 359, seed 82447 case 139); and the days a finished change added were not its ground. A split records none by either path, as before: the days before its cut stay, and those from it on leave the series for one the entry makes whole, which the snapshots show. Put back's entry stands on the half-done change it closes and on no row. Finish and Put back stay log only. Else: record a finished change through the commit's own recorder, with its snapshots and what to send back, which would make it undoable from its Finish entry (a change to api-contract.md, Activity); or work the skips' moves out again from the rows at Finish (a second derivation of what the planner recorded, with nothing to say which skip went where when two could pair two ways). An entry says what the change did, not what its plan meant to do: a recorded move stands only where the rows show it (`Undo::shownMoves`: the series skipped the old key before and skips the new one after, or the same row stood at each), and what happened to a skip the rows don't show carried is read from the rows (gone, or made); skips carried in a chain, each onto the key the next one left, are their moves alone. Before, the planner's moves were recorded whole and a skip's move was paired only with a skip gone and a skip made, so where Google kept a day the plan skipped, or Finish completed over a clock changed at Google meanwhile, the entry held a move to a key the series did not have beside the skip gone, and two neighbouring skipped days dragged a day later read as two skips changed beside their moves. Costs: none known. A commit on a calendar here writes its plan, so its record changes only for a chain, which no longer counts the two skips it never changed. Verified: undo-grounds in the unit suite (a move shown, a move not shown, one of two, a chain, a skip made where one was carried from, a row's move, an entry with no rows), google-series-recovery-grounds (a tagged day and a deleted day under a time change, a change to all-day from 17:00 Los Angeles and from 00:00 UTC, a zone change at the same clock time and at the same moment, and a count raise, each in one go, stopped before its master write landed and finished, and stopped after it and finished: Finish's grounds equal the commit's and hold the moves worked out from the dates, none where a key stays, for a row held here alone, a row Google holds as an edited day, and a skip; then the undo of the delete and of the tag; a split and a count cut that keeps a day as an event of its own, finished, with the commit's grounds; a time change finished over a clock moved at Google meanwhile, its entry holding the skip gone and no move), the interleaving fixed cases `a-day-deleted-then-a-flip-whose-master-answer-was-lost-finished-then-the-delete-undone` and `two-days-deleted-then-a-time-change-whose-third-answer-was-lost-finished-then-a-delete-undone`, and the mutation `finish-entry-from-the-row-difference`. Where: `Undo::planGrounds`, `changeGround`, `shownMoves`, `skipsChanged`; `SeriesPlans::plannedGrounds`, `recoverTx`. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **Every day's row has its own list: the rows a feed poll left without one are filled once, and the inferring readings go.** Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-10 ("Yes, backfill"), built for 1.5.5. Chosen: migration 078 gives every events row with a series parent and no `override_fields` (live or deleted, the series row deleted or not) the list migration 063 would have given it, by running 063 again unchanged: the fields that differ from its series' row, tags and people compared, and `[]` where that row is missing, with `updated_at` kept, in batches of 500, idempotent. It is a correction of live rows, not a rewrite of a stored form: the form is the list every writer has written since 1.5.0, and the rows came from the one writer that did not (a feed poll whose series' record was unreadable or absent, fixed in 1.5.5). After it the code has one reading of a stored list: `OwnFields::stored` returns it as stored; a day's row with none is a defect, reported through the journal's misuse channel (a failure in the suites, a log line in production) and read as owning every field, so nothing it holds is overwritten or dropped. Gone with the fallback: the inference that read a stored row with no list (`storedIn`, `Feeds::ownOf`'s and `Model::ownOf`'s, and the snapshot helper), `afterSync`'s second parameter, and the guards for a database ahead of migration 063 (`OwnFields::hasColumn`, `ownFieldsColumn`, `withOwnColumn`, `$ownColumn`), whose test schemas now carry the column; `infer` stays where a list is computed for a row written from outside (a new row from a feed, a PUT or an import; a Google answer; a planned placement). The grounds an entry records (`Undo::ownOf`) read through the same reader, so the exception named before is gone. Where the old form can still be read after 078: a journal snapshot of an entry a 1.5.5 development build recorded over such a row in the week before 078 (released installs have none: every entry from before their upgrade is format 1 and log only); `Undo::entryToTakeBack` refuses such an entry before anything, and a row kept in pieces is refused where it is restored, inside the undo's transaction, with "Recorded before this day's own fields were filled in by the 1.5.5 upgrade; it can't be undone." A half-done Google change's snapshot (`google_pending.local_json`) from before 078 can put such a row back through Finish or Put back; a gate there would freeze the series (there is no discard), so it is recorded as a limit: the row comes back with no list and reads as owning every field until it is next edited, and nothing is lost. What the upgrade loses: nothing on a released install; on a development install, the undo of a change from the last week whose snapshot holds such a row. Else: keep both readings and file it (two readings of one stored thing, which AGENTS.md forbids); a format gate on day rows (refusing rows that hold data the person sees). What it costs: one migration run over the day rows with no list, bounded at about 3 MB above its start for 20,000 of them. Verified: day-lists-backfill (lists by the rule for a day equal to its series, one owning tags, one owning people, one owning its title, a deleted day, a day of a deleted series and one whose series row is missing; `updated_at` kept; a second run fills nothing; 20,000 days within 16 MB; an entry whose snapshot holds such a row refused with its reason and nothing changed), link-ownership (a day left with no list filled and then taking the series' new tags; the hunter's answer over such a day, now filled first), and the suite's NULL-list check after every block with no opt-out anywhere. Settled 2026-11-10. After 1.5.5 (issue 240): the last inferring reading went, `GoogleSeries::sameAsStored`'s `decode() ?? infer()`, which now reads the list through `OwnFields::stored` as every other reader does; google-local-follow checks the list a Google "this event" edit that sends nothing stores (the tags it gave, the title it set to the series' own value).
- **A refusal to undo names the change, and a change made in Google Calendar that a finished change lost says so in plain words.** Status: choice, words approved by the owner 2026-11-10, built for 1.5.5. The day-placing refusals (`day_unplaced`, both of its sentences, and `days_since`) open with "Can't undo" or "Can't redo" and the change in double quotes: the `summary` of the entry being undone, or for a redo the summary of the change the undo took back (the entry its `details.google.undoes` names), since the undo's own summary ("Undid: ...") is not what the person is redoing (`Undo::named`). The sentences rebase writes for a change made at Google while a change waited now open with when ("While this change was waiting to be finished," for Finish, "while this change was being sent" for the write path) and say "in Google Calendar" and "the event" where they said "at Google" and "the series"; the reason a change is not kept is a past event read after "That was lost when". A list of fields in those sentences reads "title and notes" with "were" (and "notes were" alone), by one function each (`GoogleSeries::listed`, `GoogleSeries::were`). In the read-back differences "the plan" became "this change" where that reads as plain English; "Google kept ... skipped; the plan had it." kept its words. The `stale_undo` toast names Activity only as a page: "... use Undo on the Activity page." Else: keep "since this change stopped" in its old place (reads as if the change had stopped working); name the undo entry's own summary for a redo ("Can't redo "Undid: ...""). Cost: none in behaviour; no code, reason or condition changed. Verified 2026-11-10: the unit and database suites, both of which state every sentence in full (`server/tests/google-series.php`, `server/tests/db/undo-day-gate.test.php`, `undo-form-day.test.php`, `undo-day-identity.test.php`, `google-finish-first-write.test.php`, `google-finish-drag.test.php`, `google-series-form-finish.test.php`).
- **An iCalendar name of an instance is read under its series.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (the hunt of 20f77fe9, fault C1). Chosen: a RECURRENCE-ID or an EXDATE value is a name as its sender wrote it (a DATE, a date-time with a zone or a Z, a date-time with no zone), and its key is that name read under the series' form and zone by one function (`Ics::keysUnder`), never under the form of the record that carries it. Under a timed series a date-time is its instant, one with no zone is that clock in the series' zone, and a DATE is the series' occurrences on that date in its zone. Under an all-day series a DATE is that date, and a date-time is the date of its own clock as written, or of its instant in the zone the series' days were sent in when the same text sends them as zoned midnights. Where the key is decided: the parser reads one VEVENT at a time, so an EXDATE is read where it stands (its VEVENT is the series); a RECURRENCE-ID is kept as written and keyed under the series of the same text once all of it is read (`Ics::keyed`, which `Ics::parse` and the mail reader both call); and a reader that holds the series only as a stored row asks with that row (`Ics::under`: an emailed change to one day, and a poll that keeps a series whose own record it could not read). A record with no series anywhere is named alone: a DATE, or a midnight on a record itself sent as zoned midnights, is a day, and any other date-time an instant. A value type RFC 5545 forbids there (3.8.4.4 and 3.8.5.1 ask for the type of DTSTART) is read, not refused: `EXDATE;VALUE=DATE` and `RECURRENCE-ID;VALUE=DATE` under a timed series come from real senders and can only mean that date's occurrence, while read as 00:00 UTC they named the occurrence of the day before west of UTC (a skipped 7 January hid the event of the 6th) or none at all. Why: the key was read from the record's own DTSTART, so a day of a 19:00 New York series made a whole day as zoned midnights landed on the key of the day before (that day's event gone, its tag on the wrong day, the changed day shown twice), and a day of a whole-day series given a time was keyed with a clock under an all-day series; the same through a feed, a CalDAV PUT, an import and an emailed update, where Review named Tuesday for a change to Wednesday. Else: refuse a record whose name's type is not its series' (an event would vanish from a feed that has listed it for years, with nothing the person can do about it); decide the key in each reader (five readings of one question); or keep the record's form and repair the rows afterwards. Costs: the parsed shape carries two more values (`recurrence_name`, `days_tzid`), never stored; a DATE under a timed series asks the expander for that day; a date-time naming a day of an all-day series stored here, from a sender that wrote its days as zoned midnights in another zone than the name's (a Z time for a Tokyo midnight), is read in the name's own zone, since a stored all-day series keeps no zone. In Review: a held change to one day keeps its occurrence as a key, read under the series as it was when the message came, and when a change of the series' form is accepted from Review each held day of that series goes to its own day's key under the new form (`ReviewQueue::reconcilePending`, by `Feeds::ofSeries`, as a stored row goes), and one whose day the series no longer has is closed saying so, since its key could name another day of the new form; so an organizer's change of form and change of one day are right in either order of accepting; before, they were right in one order (the series first) and changed the neighbouring day in the other. Not covered, filed: a held day whose series changes form any other way while it waits, and a day held before its series is on the calendar, keep a key that can then name another day or none; right in every case means the item holds the name as sent, which changes a stored form and is the owner's to choose. Also found and not changed (`CalendarImport` belongs to another change): a day's record imported alone into a calendar that holds its series is stored with no series above it and the day shows twice, and a file whose series has another form than the one in the calendar is still matched by equal keys. Feeds are read afresh once by the release's new version, so no parse version is raised. Verified: `server/tests/ics-keys.php` (every name form under every series form, with the record's own form varied: 58 of its checks fail on 20f77fe9 before it reaches a function that tree lacks); `server/tests/db/ics-instance-names.test.php` (both mixed forms through a feed, a PUT, an import and an emailed update, a DATE as EXDATE and as RECURRENCE-ID, the held day in both orders and a held day the series loses, each by calendar date: 25 of 107 fail on 20f77fe9, the suite's own "no clock under an all-day series" among them); the mutation `ics-key-under-the-record-form` (25 named assertions). Where: `Ics::names`, `Ics::keysUnder`, `Ics::keyed`, `Ics::under`, `Feeds::sync`, `MailIngest::parseImip`, `MailIngest::applyImipEvent`, `ReviewQueue::reconcilePending`. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **A CalDAV PUT settles the rows it names no record for as a poll does.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (the hunt of 20f77fe9, fault C2). The model: a PUT is the whole object as the client now has it, the series and every day the client holds a record of, and a client holds none of what is kept here alone. With a poll it shares the match of records to rows (`Feeds::standing`, since rule 1) and, from this change, the reading of what a record leaves a row owning (`Feeds::ownAfter`) and what becomes of every stored day's row no record was given to (`Feeds::settle`, the sync's removal loop made a function both call, moved whole): kept on its day with its tags, people, reminders, answer, picked coordinates and what refers to it while the series has the day, at its image when the series' clock or zone moved, merged into the row a record made there; an ordinary day again when it holds nothing; gone and named when the series no longer has the day. Chosen: `putObject` asks `settle` about the rows it gave no record to, in place of its own delete. What stays different, and why: a row a PUT's record was given to is as the record says, even a record equal to its series (a poll makes that an ordinary day again; a client that sends the component holds it, and whether such a row should go is the write rule of Own Fields, no part of this fault, so it is as it was); a PUT is one undoable entry whose snapshots hold every row of the UID with its links before and after, where a poll's entry is a log line; a row that goes holding something is named by id and date in that entry's details, and in its words when what it held went with it. Why: a client moving a daily class from 19:00 to 20:00 sends its edited days again under the new RECURRENCE-IDs; each made a new row, and every stored row no record was given to was deleted outright with its tags and reminders, though docs/caldav.md says they are kept untouched and the same change through a feed kept everything. Else: match a PUT's records by image within one form (it changes which row a record takes for every source, decided against in "A record from elsewhere is for the row of the same day"); or copy the kept fields inside `putObject` (the second implementation again). Costs: a PUT of a series reads the image of each stored day's row it names no record for; the removal loop's counts come back from a function. Sibling: `CalendarImport::importIcs` adds rows and brings deleted ones back and never removes a live day's row, so it has no such fault. Verified: `server/tests/db/caldav-kept-here.test.php` (the clock moved, the zone moved, the clock moved with no day sent, a day's record dropped, a day removed and named, each PUT undone with every row and tag as it was, and the same clock move through a feed: 10 of 64 fail on 20f77fe9); the mutation `put-deletes-unmatched-rows` (10 named assertions); the whole database suite with every other file's count as on main; the series harness in both modes with no failing check (seed 7, 1,000 cases) and the interleaving mode, which polls after most steps through the moved loop, with main's summary line for line (seeds 7 and 307, 500 cases). Where: `Feeds::settle`, `Feeds::sync`, `CalendarBackend::putObject`. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **A day held before its series is adopted by it.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (the hunt of 20f77fe9, fault C3). The case: the calendar knows one occurrence of a series it does not have (the account was invited to one day; a feed lists a changed day and not its series), stored as a day's row with a key, an empty own list and no series above it; the person tags it; then the whole series arrives. Chosen: the arriving list's record of that day is for that row (the same key and, at Google, the id that names the same form), and the row is its series' day from then on: put under it, its own fields read by equality with the series as a row never seen is (its list was written with no series to read it against and says nothing; `Feeds::ownAfter`, by `OwnFields::infer`, the reading a new row gets), its tags and people its own where they differ, and settled like any of the series' days (`Feeds::settle`: a row with a key whose series the list has, a series above it or not). So a record that holds something of its own keeps its row with what was given here; one equal to its series leaves a row only for what is held here; and a list that brings the series without the record leaves the row following the series with what is held here. A CalDAV PUT adopts the same way, by the same two functions. A held day Google knows in the other form than the arriving series (its id names its form) is no instance of that series: no record at an equal key is given to it, and it goes named, as before. Why: the row's empty list read every unchanged field as not its own, so its record read as an ordinary occurrence, no record was given to the row, and the removal loop, which took a row with no series above it for an event no longer in the calendar, deleted it with its tag; Activity said "it is no longer in the calendar" while Google held it, and a delta with nothing in it did not bring it back. Else: give the held row a list by equality when it is first stored (there is no series to compare it with); or find held rows through `standing` (they are no rows of the stored series, and their key is already their record's). Costs: one read of the series' row per adopted day. Not covered: an ICS record held before its series whose series then arrives in another form than the record's name gave alone keeps a key that can name another day (the limit named in the entry above). Other readers of a row with no series above it, checked: `OwnFields::follow` and the suite's link rule read only rows under a series, so the row follows from its adoption on; `GoogleSync::applyChanges` reads its form from its Google id and hands it to the sync as a stored copy; `Events::parkInstanceKeys` leaves it at its key; an import of a record alone still makes one beside a series the calendar has (the entry above). Verified: `server/tests/db/day-before-series.test.php` (Google in both list orders, a feed and a PUT; the record first and the series first; a record equal to its series, tagged and not; the series without the record; a held day of another form: 26 of 107 fail on 20f77fe9); the mutations `sync-removes-a-day-held-before-its-series` (22 named assertions) and `held-day-of-another-form-takes-the-record` (2). Where: `Feeds::ownAfter`, `Feeds::heldBeforeSeries`, `Feeds::settle`, `Feeds::sync`, `CalendarBackend::putObject`. Settled 2026-11-10.
- **A start or end sent over a resource Google holds names the held form's fields as null when the form changes.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.5 (`workfolder/reviews/worker-155-form-nulls.md`).
