The case: a change between timed and all-day of a series, a single event or one day. Google merges a PATCH's `start` and `end` into the ones it holds, so a start naming only the new form's fields leaves a date and a clock there together, and Google refuses it with 400 "Invalid start time." and writes nothing; the app sent exactly that from every writer, so every such change failed against real Google while the stand-in took it. Chosen: one function, `GoogleWriter::overForm(body, held form)`, adds the held form's fields as null to a start or end sent in the other form (`dateTime` and `timeZone` going all-day, `date` going timed) and leaves a start or end in the held form, or a body without one, as it is. Every writer of a start or end over an existing resource goes through it, each with the form of what Google holds at that moment: `GoogleWriter::patchOf` (the before body is the resource as held: an edit of an event in no series, of an edited day, of an ordinary day built from its series' occurrence, "use the series' value", the revival of a cancelled event a series insert finds), `GoogleSeries::masterBody` (the master as stored, which the read just before the write matched), `GoogleSeries::stepsFor` (each day's resource as the read-back lists it, a day with no record, or only a dropped one, taking its master's form), `GoogleUndo::undo` for an edit (the event as it is now) and for a delete (the cancelled resource as read), and `GoogleMove`'s occurrence write after the import (its master's form). Inserts send whole new resources and are unchanged. Else possible: nulls always, on every start and end sent (simpler, but a null where the form does not change is a body no probe has sent, and the owner wants only what is proven); PUT instead of PATCH (replaces the resource whole, so no field is left behind, but every write would have to carry every field Google holds, attendees and reminders among them, which the app never sends). Cost: each writer has to know the held form, and reads it from the resource its write is conditional on (that read's etag is the If-Match), so a form changed at Google since that read answers 412 and nothing is written; a writer with no start to read sends no nulls. The stand-in (`FakeGoogle`) now merges start and end as Google does, removes a field sent as null and answers 400 "Invalid start time." for a start or end that would hold both forms. Verified by the probe of 2026-11-10 ([research/google-series/probe-2026-11-10.md](research/google-series/probe-2026-11-10.md)): the new form alone refused in 3.18, 3.20, 3.22, 3.24 (a series both ways at 10:00 Los Angeles and 00:00 UTC), 3.26, 3.28 (a single event both ways) and 3.30 (one day of a timed series made all-day); the same with the nulls taken in 3.19, 3.21, 3.23, 3.25, 3.27, 3.29 and 3.31; by `server/tests/google-form-nulls.php` (in `server/tests/run.php`, "Form nulls:"), which builds each probed change with the app's builders and compares it with the accepted body written out from the probe; by `server/tests/db/google-form-nulls.test.php`, each flip through the app to the stand-in (a series both ways, a single event both ways, one day made all-day and that undone, and a clock move that sends no null); by the stand-in's change turning ten database test files red before the rule (249 assertions, listed in the worker's report) and green after; and by the mutation `form-nulls-dropped`, which trips 22 named assertions.
- **A change to all events carries the skips Google holds as EXDATE lines.** Status: choice, built after 1.5.5 (issue 252; `workfolder/reviews/google-156/leftovers-phase1.md`). The case: a skip Google holds as an EXDATE line on the master, which a series made here with a deleted day has (`Events::create` → `GoogleWriter::body`, the hidden day before a first day in the skipped spring hour among them) and which every skip of a series moved to Google has (`GoogleWriter::importBody`). Google replaces the whole `recurrence` a PATCH sends (probe 3.16), and the master write of a change to all events sent the rule line alone, so the day came back at Google, and `rebase`, which predicts that write's effect from the rows here and can't tell an EXDATE line from a cancelled record, read the live day as Google's own and stored it here. Chosen, the rule: a change to all events never drops a skip it keeps. When the master write sends `recurrence` it sends the rule and an EXDATE line for each of the target's skips that Google held as one in the read `run()` makes before the write (`GoogleSeries::carriedExdates`, `masterBody`'s third argument), in the target's form and zone (`GoogleWriter::recurrenceLines`). A skip Google holds as a cancelled record stays the record's, out of the rule; across a change between timed and all-day no line is carried, since every skip of the old form goes with its form and the target's skips go as cancelled instances by the steps that follow, as before. `afterMaster` says the same (an EXDATE line stays exactly when the write carries it), so the journaled `expect` of a master step is true; `rebase` needs no change, because it reads a skip kept on both sides by the target as the change's own either way. Finish needs nothing new and no stored form: nothing is dropped for it to recover. Else: send the rule alone and have the step after it cancel the day as an instance (the design before, which `rebase` defeated, and which costs a request per day); or predict the loss from the read and journal each skip's form with the pending row (a stored form and a format number, for Finish). Cost: the master body is longer by the lines it carries; a pending change journaled before this, whose master write dropped a line, is still read the old way by Finish (the day stays live and is named nowhere), a loss bounded by the changes left half done across the upgrade. Sites that send `recurrence` to Google: the forward run, Finish, Put back, a split's trimmed series and an undo or redo of a change to all events all build it in `masterBody` through `run()` (fixed); a split's new series and a series inserted by `GoogleSeries` (`insertBody`) carry no skip in the rule, their skips go as cancelled instances (follows: nothing held to drop); a series made here (`GoogleWriter::insert`) and Move to Google (`GoogleWriter::import`) send the whole row with every skip as a line (follows: they make the lines); a single event's or a day's edit and their undo (`Events::patchBody`, `GoogleUndo::bodyOf`) send `recurrence` only when the rule changes, and a day or single event has none (follows). Verified: `server/tests/db/google-series-exdate.test.php` (a series made here with a deleted day renamed for all events; the same stopped right after its master write, then Finish; a series as Move to Google leaves it, two deleted days, renamed), each failing on the tree before the change; the fixed interleaving case `a-daily-starting-in-the-skipped-spring-hour-its-notes-changed-for-all-events-keeps-its-hidden-first-day` (issue 252's seed 76016 case 5 shrunk to its one step), failing x-write-asked before the change. The stand-in already dropped EXDATE lines a PATCH leaves out (`FakeGoogle::patchMaster` replaces `recurrence` whole), as Google does.
- **Each day's write in a change to all events carries that day's own etag, read just before it.** Status: replaced after 1.5.5 by "Inside a change to all events a day is written with no condition" (below); was a choice, built after 1.5.5 (issue 256; the probe of 2026-11-13, `docs/design/research/google-series/probe-2026-11-13.md`). The case: a change to all events that writes more than one day (a switch between timed and all-day, a clock time changed and back, any day restored and another cancelled) stopped half done at its third request with "This event changed at Google since you last saw it", and Finish completed it. The cause, measured: each day step's `If-Match` was the etag the step before it answered with, on the reading that every resource of a series carries one etag (probe 5.1 to 5.3, 2026-11-07). Google gives each resource its own once a day has a record: a write to one day moves that day's etag and may leave the master's and the others' as they were, and even a first write to an ordinary day can answer with an etag the master doesn't take (3.36, 3.37, 3.40, 10.2), so the chained etag named nothing the next day held. Resent with that day's own listed etag, every refused write was taken. Chosen, the rule: a write to one day of a series is conditional on that day's own etag, read just before the write, never on an etag chained from another resource's answer (`GoogleSeries::dayCondition`). The read is the series' list (a day with a record, live, cancelled or dropped, is listed under its id), else a GET of the id (an ordinary day). Before the etag is used, the day must hold what the change read back and has sent since; a day changed at Google meanwhile is refused with `google_conflict`, so that sentence stays true wherever it is shown, and the If-Match guards the moment between the read and the write. The read-back after the day steps no longer waits for the master's etag to equal the last answer's, which a day's write may leave unmoved. Else: chain from a fresh read of the master's etag (Finish's way, which worked live in these cases only because the target's etag equalled the master's; 3.36's first change shows a record with an etag the master didn't have); or make the conditional on the whole series' digest alone with no `If-Match` (refused: every write carries one). Cost: one more read per day written (the list, and a GET for a day with no record). Sites, from the leftovers inventory: the day steps of the forward run, Finish, Put back, a split's carried days, and an undo or redo of a change to all events (one code path, `GoogleSeries::run`): fixed. Already reading the target's own etag just before, so following: a series removal's record steps (`found()`), an insert with a chosen id (`insertChosen`), a "this event" edit and delete and their undo (`GoogleWriter::patch` and `delete` through `condition()`, the listed etag under the id or a GET's; 3.39 live: taken), a day's fields back to the series' (`SeriesPlans`), Move to Google's edited days, and every master write (the change's condition read). Seen live: switches both ways (3.36, 3.37, a first switch from all-day to timed included), a clock change and back (3.40). Not seen live: Put back, and a bound or split taken back (10.2 and 10.3 sent each day with its own etag and were taken). Verified: google-series' sequences (a read of each day before its write); `google-series-back-again.test.php` (timed to all-day and back, all-day to timed and back, 10:00 to 11:00 and back, each with a renamed and a deleted day, each change in one go), failing on the tree before against the stand-in with per-resource etags (`tools/google-probe/test.php`, 3.36, 3.37). Refined after: a dropped record listed under an id is not what a write to that id addresses, so its etag is a GET's, never the listed one.
- **The stand-in is checked against Google by replaying the app's own requests.** Status: choice, built after 1.5.5 (`tools/google-probe/README.md`, "Replaying the App's Requests"). The case: the stand-in (`FakeGoogle`) was twice more forgiving than Google and a fault shipped (the timed and all-day switch, the cancelled leftovers of #256), and the probe's cases are hand-written, so nothing asked Google whether it answers the requests the app actually sends as the stand-in does. Chosen: a recorder wraps the stand-in's transports (`GOOGLE_TRACE`, through `GoogleStand`; the stand-in itself is unchanged) and writes each scenario's requests and answers in order; `replay.php` sends the same requests to a target through the probe's `Api`, maps what the target issues (event ids, instance ids through their series id, iCalUIDs, etags by event then by value, sync and page tokens), compares each answer after a fixed list of normalizations (each listed in the README as a place a real difference could hide) and stops a scenario at its first divergence. A change the harness makes to the stand-in outside a request is recorded with the PATCH that repeats it when it touches an event with no exception records yet (its adopt of a series it just made), and otherwise makes the trace unreplayable. One process per scenario, so a trace holds one scenario on a fresh stand-in. Else possible: a hand-written probe case per app path (what the probe is, and what missed both faults); recording inside `FakeGoogle::handle` (simpler, but another worker edits that file, and a wrapper keeps the stand-in free of tooling); comparing answers raw (every etag, timestamp and offset would differ); continuing past a divergence (every later answer would differ for the same cause). Cost: a difference inside a normalized field (an offset, rule part order, a cancelled item's other fields, an error's reason) is not reported as a divergence; a read that differs is asked again up to five times, so a difference that clears within ten seconds is not reported; a list's items of events the scenario doesn't know are left out; requests after a divergence and paths the recorded scenarios don't take are not checked. Verified on the stand-in only: `tools/google-probe/replay-test.php` (traces of every probe group and of hand-made scenarios replay with no divergence; a stand-in omitting dropped records diverges at the first request whose recorded answer lists one; dropping records on a zone change, a tampered answer and a changed error message each diverge at their request; a list in another order does not; nineteen mutations of the replay's mapping, normalizing and cleanup each turn it red), and the trace set of 49 scenarios and 1,147 requests (`record.php`) replays on fresh stand-ins with no divergence. The live replay against Google is the maintainer's, not yet run. The set is recorded in the zone of the calendar it replays on (`record.php --zone`, through `GOOGLE_STAND_ZONE` in `GoogleStand`): a set recorded in UTC and one in America/Los_Angeles differ only in the top-level `timeZone` of list answers (575 of 1,147) and in ids the app picks at random, as every timed event the app sends names its own zone. The same recording checks the app never mails guests: every POST, PATCH, PUT and DELETE it sent to an event carries `sendUpdates=none` (`TraceRecorder::mailing`; `record.php` exits 1 naming any that doesn't; 459 writes in the set, none without), and `replay-test.php` checks the check on a trace with three such writes. The first live run (2026-11-13, [research/google-series/replay-2026-11-13.md](research/google-series/replay-2026-11-13.md)) diverged in all 49 scenarios at the first insert: Google leaves out a field sent empty, adds `reminders` and `eventType`, and writes every `dateTime` with the calendar's offset. Chosen: the stand-in answers so (`FakeGoogle::shapedAnswer`, one function on every answer's way out, the store unchanged), and the replay stops ignoring default reminders, `eventType` and offsets, since its normalizing is only for what differs between any two runs (ids, etags, times of writing). Else possible: widening the replay's normalizing, which would hide the same shapes from every later run.
- **What a stopped change is compared with is what Google held when the change read it at its stop.** Status: choice, 2026-11-13, by the owner's rule ("Last write wins", the entry above); restated 2026-11-14 as "A step's expectation is what the run last read after it" (below: every read the run makes after a step, whether or not a prediction was journaled with it), and the reading it left in place (`rebase`'s explained values in the forward run and in Finish) ended with "After the series' write, the days are set from what Google holds" and "Finish sets the plan's days where it can't tell its own change from another"; it replaces, the same day, "A step's expectation is Google's answer to its write, over the prediction of what it did not write" (was: the resource a step wrote was expected as Google answered the write, everything else as predicted, chained from the change's condition read, never a later read; built in `7fdb83e6` to catch a stranger's change inside the window, which the rule no longer promises). The case: Put back (and every later "changed since") compares Google with what the stopped change's steps were expected to leave. That was a prediction, which did not know Google sets `reminders` itself on an all-day resource (replay of 2026-11-13, F), so Put back after a switch between timed and all-day was refused when nothing had changed (21 assertions, and on real Google too). Chosen: the read the run already makes at its end, or where it stops, replaces the expectation of the series' last step (`run()`'s `settled`); the prediction is used only when that read never happened (the process died between a write and the read), and it carries Google's proven side effect (`googleOwn`, reminders false once all-day). Why: it is the rule said directly (what differs since we last read Google is a change; inside our own window the last write wins); it cannot produce a false "changed since" from a side effect of Google's we have not met yet, where answer-plus-prediction mended only the one known; and it deletes code (`withAnswer`, the answered hook and its chain). Cost, the one the owner accepted: a change someone else makes inside the window, before the stop read, is not detected, and Put back goes ahead over it. What the prediction is still for: the expectation of a step whose run never read Google after it; and `rebase`'s explained values in the forward run and in Finish, which keep a stranger's change made mid-run where the change doesn't touch the field (more than the rule promises; left as it is, its deletable parts listed in the hand-back of 2026-11-13). Verified: `google-series-back-again.test.php`: Put back runs after a switch to all-day stopped after its master write, Google setting the reminders itself; a day renamed or the series' place changed at Google inside the window, stopped with the stop read made: Put back goes ahead (last write wins, named so); the same rename made after the stop: refused. `google-series-identity-recovery`'s zone change at the same moments, stopped after the master write: Put back now goes through (3.17 shows Google keeps the records; the refusal was the prediction's). The 21 assertions on the shaped stand-in pass.
- **A cancelled day survives a rule change as a live one does, by identity alone; and Put back with no answered step asks whether Google still holds the series as before, by content.** Status: choice, built after 1.5.5 (the probe of 2026-11-13, 3.42 to 3.47; found by the edit harness's Google and interleaving modes on the stand-in made as Google). First: `afterMaster` (S2) predicted that a cancelled record goes with any change of the rule's days, from 3.7 (2026-11-07). The live runs show the rule is identity: kept exactly while its key is a day of the new rule (daily, a weekday added, every fifth week), and 3.7's cancelled day went only because it fell past that rule's tenth occurrence. With the stand-in made so, rebase read Google's kept cancelled record as a change made at Google and kept it, and the series ended with a skip the plan does not have (Google mode seed 7, cases 197 and 892). Chosen: the pattern clause goes; a cancelled record is predicted as a live one is. The rest of the deferred prediction (`unproven()`: a zone alone, a link, a change of the rule's days other than weekdays added, still predicted dropped though 3.17, 3.32 and 3.33 to 3.47 show them kept) is unchanged; rebase's other reading (Q) explains Google keeping them. Second: Put back, when no step of a series was answered, judged "nothing changed since" by the master's etag. A day's write leaves the master's etag unmoved once the day has a record (3.36, 10.2), so a day changed at Google while a change waited passed as nothing, and Put back went ahead (interleaving seed 7, case 71: a switch to all-day cut off at its master write, a day's notes changed at Google, then Put back). Chosen: for a series that existed before the change, Google must hold it as it was before (the digest `condition()` compares, by content); a series the change inserted keeps its insert step's reading. Same rule as issue 256's: an etag is never the measure of whether another resource changed. Cost: one list read in place of a GET; a series whose records hold a link they don't own may now be refused where the condition's own relaxation would let it pass (a refusal, Finish the way out). Verified: Google mode seed 7 (1,000 cases) and interleaving seed 7 with every fixed case, clean after; each case above failing before.
- **The last write wins while one of our changes is being sent; between changes, Google is read first and a write is conditional on what was read.** Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-13 ("Last write wins! We document that explicitly"; on guarding the seconds while our own change is sent: "don't make our app bend over backwards to deal with things the user could handle"). The rule: while one of Better-Cal's own changes is being sent to Google (a few requests, a few seconds), the last write wins: Better-Cal does not try to detect a change someone else makes to the same event in that window. Between its changes (before a change, before Finish, before Put back, before an undo) it reads Google first, and Google itself refuses a write to something that changed since it was read (`If-Match`); what differs is reported in plain words. A change at Google to a field Better-Cal is not changing is no conflict; only a change to the same thing is. Why: the north star the owner set is to tell the person, plainly, what happened when something can't be handled, not to build mechanism for seconds-wide windows. The other calendar apps surveyed (`docs/design/research/google-series/caldav-apps.md`, `small-handlers.md`, `docs/design/google-series-survey.md`) check each resource's ETag before writing and, on a conflict, ask (Thunderbird), let one side win whole (vdirsyncer; Evolution's keep-local, which sends no ETag) or report it (Akonadi); none describes a guard between its own requests. Else: catch a stranger's change inside the window by journaling what each of our writes was answered with and comparing every later read with it (the form built in `7fdb83e6`): replaced by this rule, see the next entry. Cost: someone renaming a day in Google Calendar in the same seconds as a change from here may lose the rename; what the calendar shows is what Google holds. Where it applies: `docs/google-calendar.md`, "When Someone Else Changes the Same Event"; `docs/google-behavior.md`, "What Better-Cal Does Instead".
- **One day of a repeating event on Google may take the other form.** Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-13 ("Remove refusal, fix this, of course"). Was: a day of an all-day series given a clock time on a Google calendar was refused before anything was sent (`google_all_day` for a "this event" edit, its undo and its redo; `googleAllDay` for a plan option keeping such a day), on the strength of probe 2.2a (2026-11-07), whose 400 was the merge of start fields (3.18 to 3.30), not a limit. Evidence: one day of an all-day series given 15:00 with its date sent null is taken (3.38, 2026-11-13); one day of a timed series made all-day with its clock fields sent null is taken (3.31, 2026-11-10). Chosen: both are allowed, sent by the one body builder every day write uses (`GoogleWriter::patchOf` through `overForm`, the held form's fields null), no second path; the model offers every plan on Google as locally (no refusal is Google's own); the harness's invariant i is that rule alone, and Google mode now runs what it counted not applicable. Nothing else about the other form is refused, because no probe showed Google refusing anything else of it. Verified: `google-series-back-again.test.php` (each direction, one PATCH with the held form's fields null, the day stored in its own form; the first failing before), `undo-day-gate` H7 (the redo of a day's time after the series was made all-day goes through), `series-model` Acceptance 5 (the Google plan is the local plan, a timed day under an all-day series included).
- **An undo under a repeating event deleted at Google says the event was deleted.** Status: choice, 2026-11-13, by the owner's north star (say what happened and what the person can do). Under a series deleted at Google every day reads cancelled (probe 10.5), and the undo of a day's edit with no row here read that as "changed at Google since". Chosen: a day read cancelled is checked against its series; a series deleted at Google refuses with `google_deleted` and a sentence that says the repeating event was deleted in Google Calendar, that the change can't be undone here, and what the person can do (refresh; make it again or restore it in Google Calendar); any other cancelled day keeps the conflict. Cost: one GET of the series on that refusal. Verified: google-undo-series-deleted, its edit-undone-and-redone variant.
- **A change to one event or one day on Google goes through over a change made there to another field, and asks over the same field.** Status: planned, 2026-11-13, by the owner's rule ("less cautious and more helpful"; compare by field). Model: [google-conflict-choice.md](google-conflict-choice.md). Chosen: before the write, Google's read (G), the row here (B) and the request (W) are compared field by field by one pure function; the fields with G different from both B and W are shown with Google's value and the person's, and Cancel or Overwrite chosen (Overwrite sends again with the values shown and is compared again); with none, Google's read is stored here as a poll stores it and the write runs as today, sending only the person's fields, and the toast names what came in from Google. A delete wants every field; an edit over an event deleted at Google, or a day its series no longer has at that time, is refused with a sentence. Else: the refusal of today, a digest of the whole event or the whole series ("Refresh and try again", which named nothing and refused a day's change over another day's); or fold Google's values into the write without storing the read first, which would make a day read its series' new value as its own. Cost: no extra request in the common case (the read is the one the condition makes); one more round trip when asked. No stored form. Revised the same day after an independent read: one pure function (`GoogleSeries::threeWay`, over the `fieldsOf` map `condition` already digests) serves this and the kind 1 undo of a change to all events; the read is stored journaled as a poll before the write, and survives a 409 by the deferred error; the invitation answer is never asked about. Revised again after a second read and the owner's rulings: the base is the row, with the value the server last served echoed back for each changed field; a delete over a change at Google asks; the step uses the access model's first read and runs only at writer or owner; news the person has no reason to expect goes in a question at the moment of the action. Slice 1 for 1.5.7, after a stage 0 read against 1.5.6 (2026-11-14; the page at the top of the model): a change or delete of an event in no series only, behind the setting `google_conflict_choice` (on as shipped, by the owner's choice of 2026-11-14 in the entry below; exactly 1.5.6 when off); the read is 1.5.6's first read, taken through `GoogleWriter::readNow` and handed to the write, so nothing is read twice; the function is `GoogleConflict::threeWay` in a class of its own, since `GoogleSeries::threeWay` already names a helper of the series outcome words; six fields compared by `fieldsOf`'s equalities alone (notes by `Sanitize::description`, no reading as text, the time as start, end and all-day without the zone); a field sent at its base value is not wanted and not sent; Planned or Maybe never asks (two values); a single event made repeating at Google keeps 1.5.6's refusal; the check is an exhaustive table and hand-listed request scenarios, not 200 seeded cases. Simpler than the model by the owner's guidance in the 1.5.7 brief (when in doubt ask; nothing for a seconds-wide window). Not verified: not built yet.
- **A switch between timed and all-day that Google would hide days with is refused, naming the days.** Status: choice, built after 1.5.5 (replay round 4, cause 1; probe 2026-11-13, 3.48 to 3.51 and 3.63 to 3.65). What Google does: when a repeating event changes between timed and all-day, every day of the new form at the same instant as a record of the old (an edited or deleted day, or a record at the series' values; an all-day day's instant is its date at 00:00 UTC) is hidden: not listed, not readable, not writable, not insertable, and nothing tried brings it back. A dropped record there hides nothing, so moving the time first and switching afterwards keeps every day (3.63, 3.64); making the days ordinary first doesn't (3.65). Chosen, one predicate (`GoogleSeries::hiddenBySwitch`: the days of a switch whose record shares an instant with a day of the new form) applied twice before anything is sent: by the planner from the rows here, every edited day holding a field Google holds and every deleted day (`Model::compute`, refusal `googleHidden`); and by `GoogleSeries::run()` over its first read of Google, after that read is held against the rows here (a series changed at Google since the last poll is refused as that, "changed at Google since", before anything else is judged; verified by google-hidden-after-condition, failing before), every record including one at the series' values, which the rows here can't show (3.65), so Finish, Put back and an undo meet it too. The sentence (`GoogleSeries::hiddenWords`, and the client's `hiddenWords` in planwords.js, the one place on each side) says it is Google Calendar's own fault, since the owner, reading the first wording, could not see why Google would "hide" a day nobody cancelled: "Google Calendar has a fault here: if this event became all-day at its current time, Google would stop showing some of its days (Mon 11 Jan, Mon 18 Jan), though nothing deleted them. Move the event to another time first, then make it all-day." It names the days it would hide, as they would show after the switch (a daily series' record can hide the next day: 19:00 New York is 00:00 UTC the day after), up to three and the rest counted; a hidden day that is deleted here (the person deleted the day of that date, read in the old form, never judged by the record at its instant, which where the clock is 00:00 UTC the next day is the day before's: the second independent read, F4, verified by google-hidden-predicate, 27 cases in seven zones by date arithmetic, four failing before) is left out of "though nothing deleted them", and where only deleted days meet the sentence says Google would lose track of days deleted from it, so Better-Cal could no longer tell they were deleted. To timed it ends "Make it timed at another time first, then change the time to 09:00." From a half-done change the advice goes through the other way out first, since such a change takes no other change until it is finished or put back (the second independent read, F5): a Finish refused says "Put the change back instead; afterwards, move the event to another time first, then make it all-day.", a Put back refused "Finish the change instead; afterwards, ..."; only one of the two can switch the form, so the other is never refused for hidden days (`hiddenWords`' `$from`, set by `run()` from what it is sending; verified by google-hidden-recovery, two checks failing before). The refusal carries the days, those deleted, the direction and the clock, so the client words it in the person's formats. Else: send the switch and name the lost days after (the day is gone at Google for good); a mechanism to rebuild the days (none of seven ways tried works, 3.48). Cost: such a switch takes two changes; a deleted day held as an EXDATE line is counted as if a record (the rows can't tell them apart), which may refuse a switch Google would take. Where it applies: only where a series Google holds changes form. The planner on a Google calendar for the series itself (not for a split's part, which is inserted at Google under a new id and holds no record of the old series, so nothing of it can be hidden; the old series only gets an end: the second independent read, F3, a regression of this branch, fixed); every run (forward, Finish, Put back, undo), for each target whose master Google holds (an inserted part has none, so is passed over). Inventory of `hiddenRefusal`'s callers: `Model::compute` for the series' own plan (follows), for a split's part (fixed: passed over); `GoogleSeries::run` per target (follows). Verified: google-split-switch, a daily 20:00 New York series split to all-day over a renamed day, by date here and at Google (four checks failing before), with a control refusing the same switch for all events. A change planned before this rule and left half done meets the run's check at Finish. An undo of a change to all events that would hide days is refused the same way, by the run's check, with the same sentence; the harness's undo oracles expect that refusal from their own reading of the stand-in (`GoogleStand::hidesOnSwitch`: a record of the series at the instant of a day of the series the undo restores), and an oracle probe fails when the refusal is accepted where no day would be hidden. A switch made at Google by someone else at such a time is Google's own fault (the owner saw it in Google Calendar itself: a daily 4 pm event with one renamed and one deleted day, made all-day there, lost two days, one of them untouched); by his rule the app does not make up for a remote's fault, so it is filed, not built for, and no test asserts what the app then reads.
- **A half-done switch that Finish would refuse for hidden days says so in Review first** (#271, 1.5.8). Status: choice. A switch between timed and all-day left half done by 1.5.5, before the refusal above existed, is still in Review after the upgrade, and Finish stops on that refusal. Chosen: Review judges it as the planner judges a new change, by the rows here (`GoogleSeries::recordKeysByRows`, now the one reading of which days Google holds a record for, used by the planner too), for each series of the change whose own write (master, insert or remove) has not been sent; the rows keep the series as it was until Finish saves the change. Where the switch would hide a day, the item ends with Finish's own sentence (`hiddenWords`, from Finish: "Put the change back instead; afterwards, ...") and offers Put back and Dismiss, not Finish, the way an undo of an older format already did (`finishRefused`). A series whose own write was sent may hold the new form at Google already, and is left to Finish, which reads Google. Turned down: reading Google from the Review list (a request per half-done change on every listing, for a state only an upgrade from 1.5.5 can leave). Cost: where Google holds a record the rows here don't show, Review offers Finish and Finish refuses with the sentence, as before. Verified: google-half-hidden-review (a pending row as 1.5.5 wrote it: Review's sentence and actions, and Finish through the API refusing with the same words; with nothing hidden, or the series' write sent, Finish offered), which failed before the change.
- **Inside a change to all events a day is written with no condition, and only to a day of the series.** Status: choice, built after 1.5.5, by the owner's rule ("The last write wins while one of our changes is being sent", above); its two sentences for a day not sent and a day Google would not take went on 2026-11-14 with "What is said of a change to all events is made from three states, and never from a step" (below), which records such a day from the last read; it replaces "Each day's write in a change to all events carries that day's own etag, read just before it". Was: before each day's write the run read the series' list and the day (`dayCondition`), refused the change mid-way when the day had changed since the read-back, and sent the write on the day's own etag: 3N + 4 requests for N days, a mid-run conflict the rule says we don't make, and a read that could fail on its own. Evidence: Google takes a day's PATCH with no If-Match in every state a run meets: a live record, a revived one, a cancelled record made an edited day, a record put back to the series' values, a record cancelled, an ordinary day cancelled (probe 2026-11-13, 3.66), and the days sent in a run's own order (3.63, 3.64). Chosen: after the series' conditional write and its read-back, each day not as the plan says is sent once with no If-Match and no read (`GoogleWriter::sendDayStep`, the writer's one named exemption from "no write without the etag of a read"; every other PATCH and DELETE stays conditional): N + 4 requests again. A step goes only to a day of the series as Google holds it after the master step (`GoogleSeries::named` over the read-back's master); an edited day at any other key is not sent and is named from the last read ("... is not a day of this event in Google Calendar; this change asked for ... there."), since Google refuses a write to such an id when a dropped record sits under it (400, 3.48) and Finish after a clock moved at Google sent one. A day's write answered 404 (past the 730 occurrences Google expands, or hidden) is not sent again and is named too, from the last read as a day Google Calendar holds otherwise than asked, never passed over in silence; the 730 case had no sentence of its own before (the read-back's "kept ... as an ordinary day" covered it). Cost: a stranger's change to a day inside the seconds of our own change is written over, as the rule says. The interleaving harness's x-conditional allows exactly this: a PATCH to an instance id in a step that writes a series conditionally or recovers one (Finish, Put back). Verified: google-series' sequences (no read before a day's PATCH, the master's write alone conditional), google-series-recovery's "the clock moved at Google" Finish (stopped at every write on Google's 400 before this, passes by the filter).
- **A link set on a series and a zone change at the same moments are proven to keep the series' records.** Status: choice, built after 1.5.5, by the rule that a class leaves the unproven list once a live probe shows it (`GoogleSeries::unproven`). Evidence: a zone change that moves no instant keeps every record, a deleted day's included (3.17, 2026-11-10; 3.54 and 3.60, 2026-11-13); a link set keeps every record and is written into each (3.32, 2026-11-10; 3.52, 3.58, 3.61, 2026-11-13). Chosen: `unproven()` keeps the zone only where the instants move, and no longer lists the link; `afterMaster` predicts the link written into every kept record, as a title is; `sameAsStored` drops `UNWRITTEN`, its tolerance for a record without the series' link, so such a record now reads as changed at Google (Google would have written it). The stand-in's `zoneAlone` "dropped" setting, which modelled the other answer to that question, is no longer run by google-series-identity. Cost: a Put back or Finish whose stop read failed compares with this prediction, which is now the answer Google gave rather than the losing one. Verified: the unit suite's afterMaster and condition cases (rewritten from "predicted dropped" and "as stored either way" to the evidence), the database suite.
- **A plan that leaves no undeleted day is refused as leaving no events.** Status: choice, built after 1.5.5, on every calendar (replay round 4, cause 3; probe 2026-11-13, 3.62). Google cancels a series whose rule change leaves it only deleted days, and a later status sent doesn't bring it back; the app then read the series as deleted and deleted it here too. The planner refused a plan that left no day at all (`empty`) but counted a deleted day as one. Chosen: the same refusal, with its sentence ("This would leave the series with no events. Delete it instead?"), for a bounded series whose every day is deleted, in the one place the planner lays the new series' days out (`Model::map`), and for "this and following" a COUNT reading whose part keeps only deleted days (`Split::countReadings`). (An earlier part left only deleted days was refused here too; that was wrong, see the next entry: it is the whole series' change.) A split's own refusal says it in words for its reach: "This would leave no events from this day on. Delete this and following instead?" (`Model::EMPTY_FROM_DAY`, `Split::REASONS`, the client's `SPLIT_REFUSALS`), since the days before it stay. On every calendar, since the sentence is as true locally and one rule is one function. The harness's own oracle (`Expect::outcome`, `empty`) says the same. Else: refuse on Google only. Cost: none found; such a series showed nothing anyway. Not covered: deleting the last live day on its own (a "this event" delete), unprobed at Google, filed. Verified: series-only-skipped (a rule change locally and on Google, delete following), failing five checks before.
- **From the first day still showing, this and following is the whole series.** Status: rule, built after 1.5.5 (the second independent read of the 1.5.6 branch, F2, a regression of that branch). Which day is the series' first: the first day still showing, not the first date of its rule. A change to "this and following" (an edit, a delete, an answer) from a day all of whose earlier days are deleted is the whole series' change, as from a first day with none deleted: one function, `Split::plan`'s whole-series test (every key before the day acted on is a deleted one), which every "this and following" path reaches (`Events` patch, delete and answer through `splitPlanned` and `previewSplit`; the client takes `whole` from the plan). Before, the branch refused such a change as leaving no events ("Delete it instead?"), false for a rename or a new time and silent on the way out; 1.5.5 split it, leaving an old part of deleted days only, which Google cancels for good (3.62). Else: split and keep an all-deleted old part (Google cancels it). Cost: the earlier deleted days go with a whole delete, as they do from a first day. Inventory of the sites asking "is this the first day": `Split::plan` (fixed); `Events::patchLocked`, `deleteEvent` and the answer's following path, `SeriesPlans::previewSplit` and `committedSplit` (follow: they read `whole` from it); the client (`serieschange.js`, follows `whole`); the edit harness's own oracle (`Invariants::isFirstDay`, which Driver and OptionMatch share, fixed: it read the first raw occurrence and failed seed 7 case 2's answer from a day on); its interleaving mode (`Interleave::occurrenceOf`, already the first day not skipped). Verified: series-only-skipped, rename, an hour later and delete from the first day still showing on a local and a Google calendar, by date (twelve checks failing before); a split with an end before its day refused in the split's words.
- **A day's own notes cleared by a master write are sent back.** Status: the outcome stands; its mechanism was replaced 2026-11-14 by "After the series' write, the days are set from what Google holds" (below): the notes go back because the read back shows the day is not as planned, by no rule about notes, and the prediction's notes clause is read only as a step's expectation. Was: choice, built after 1.5.5 (replay round 4, cause 2; probe 2026-11-13, 3.52 to 3.61), by the owner's direction that a field we know the value of is ours to keep. What Google does: on a series with no notes (as the app creates every series without notes), any master PATCH that leaves `description` out clears the notes of every kept day record (a link, the start along its pattern, the zone at the same moments, the title, the length, a longer COUNT: six of six); with notes of its own the series' days keep theirs (3.58 to 3.60), and sending the empty notes keeps them too (3.61, one case). Released 1.5.5 read the cleared notes after its master write as a change made at Google and kept it: the day lost its notes, silently. Chosen: the prediction of a master write's effect (`GoogleSeries::afterMaster`, the one function every run, Finish's comparison and an undo use) has every kept record's notes cleared when the write leaves them out and Google's series has none, so the read-back shows it as the change's own effect and the edited day's step (rule A, `stepsFor`) sends the day whole, its notes with it, as it already did for a title the write sends. Else: always send the series' notes with a master write (3.61 suggests it protects them; one case only, and it would not cover a series changed elsewhere); or change nothing and name the loss. Cost: one more day write per edited day with notes, on such writes. Sites: the forward run's master step, Finish (`rebase`), Put back (`run` with the befores), an undo of a change to all events (a forward change through the same function): all follow. Verified: google-day-notes (a link, a rename, an hour longer, two more events, each in one go, and a link stopped after its master write and finished), failing three of its checks without the prediction against the stand-in that clears notes as Google does; recovery-grounds' "ten events" and "cut to three", failing before.
- **After the series' write, the days are set from what Google holds, with no reading of who changed them.** Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-14, built for 1.5.6 ([the model](google-series-read-back.md)). The fault: after the series' own write the read back went to `rebase`, which compared it with a prediction of what Google does to the days it keeps (`afterMaster`); whatever the prediction lacked was read as someone else's change and kept in place of what was asked. Google leaves a kept day's end where it was when its start moves (probe 2026-11-13, 3.17; 2026-11-14, 3.67), so a day with its own time ended wrong, at Google and here, with nothing said; and a write of one private property, with no rule in it, cleared a day's own notes (3.68), so no list of Google's effects can be complete. Chosen: the plan says what each day should be; after the series' write the run reads Google and sends every day that is not as the plan has it, once, with no condition (`GoogleSeries::days`, the one comparison: a day the plan has as edited, deleted or ordinary against a day Google has as no day, deleted, a record or ordinary, in every field Google holds, the start and the end each exactly). It keeps nothing it sees in place of the plan. The series is written once: a series that reads otherwise than the plan and no longer as it was before our write is someone's later write; it stands and is recorded, whether the difference is a field, the rule or the form (the change is not left half done for it), and a day then takes the series' values as Google holds them wherever the series is not as planned. Else: predict the kept end too (one more case of a list that can't be complete); or go on reading intent and name every mismatch (the same silence wherever the prediction is wrong). Cost: a change someone makes to a day in Google Calendar between our series write and the read after it is written over with nothing said, which is what "last write wins" already costs; and one more day write wherever Google altered a kept day, the day at its own time first of all. Lag: a list by iCalUID carried a write at once after 100 of 100 writes, 91 to 346 ms after the write's answer (probe group 11, 2026-11-14), so no mechanism waits for one; the one stop left there is for a list that still shows the series as it was before the write, where nothing can be compared yet. Requests: N + 4 for N days not as planned after the series' write, and 3 when none is (the read back is then the last read). Sites: every series write of a plan goes through `run()` (a change to all events, the old half of "this and following", Finish, Put back, an undo and a redo); an answer to a repeating invitation and its undo go through `writeBesideDays` ("An answer to a repeating invitation leaves its days as they were", below). Verified: `google-series-read-back.test.php`, written before the build with its expectations worked out by date arithmetic and nothing in `server/src` called for them (100 cases, 70 failing before the build): eleven kinds of change by six things the stand-in does to every record a series write keeps or revives, two of them never seen at Google; `google-undo-kept-end`, `google-day-own-time-zone`, `google-day-walk`, `google-start-along-pattern`, `google-series-request-count`.
- **What is said of a change to all events is made from three states, and never from a step.** Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-14 (the last write wins, "and the way to be clear about it is in the post-Google-send where we presumably read back from Google what actually landed: then we can update/correct our local record of what actually happened and leave it as 'last write won', with an accurate local record/note/message"), built for 1.5.6. Chosen: the rows here are set from the change's last read, as before; one function (`GoogleSeries::outcome`) compares what Google held before the change, what the plan asks and what the last read shows, and returns data, for the series by field group and for each day; the Activity entry, the Review notice and Finish's answer are composed from that data in one place (`outcomeWords`). Where the last read equals the plan nothing is said. Where it differs, a line says what Google Calendar holds and what the change asked for, with both values, and never who or what caused it: "On Mon 29 Mar 2027 Google Calendar holds the title 'Sample weekly, renamed'; this change asked for the title 'Sample weekly, special'."; for a field of the series the change did not ask to change: "The event's title is 'Zebra crossing' in Google Calendar; it was 'Sample series', and this change did not change it."; for a day whose key is no day of the series Google holds, the key's time is named with its date, and a deleted day is recorded by its date too, so a day the series shows on that date at another time is said to be on the calendar. Went: `differences()`, two states worded "Google kept", and every sentence made from a step that was sent, skipped or refused ("... was changed in Google Calendar while this change was being sent; Google's ... was kept", "... is not a day of this event in Google Calendar now, so this change's own version of it was not sent there", "Google Calendar would not take this change for ..."). Left as it is: the words "Google kept something else:" that open this part of an Activity line, which stored entries are cut on (`GoogleUndo::KEPT`). Cost: a notice no longer says that a day was passed over or refused, only what Google holds there and what was asked. Verified: `server/tests/google-series.php` generates every combination of the three states for a day (72) and for a field of the series (40) and holds each line to the data alone; the six cases of the read-back check where the series is changed by another client right after its own write (the rows equal Google's end, and nothing said is false of it).
- **Finish sets the plan's days where it can't tell its own change from another.** Status: choice, 2026-11-14 (the read-back model, its first open choice), built for 1.5.6, by "promise less"; it narrows "Finish applies the change's deltas over what holds now". The case: a change stopped part way, and Google does not hold the series as the change last read it. Once the series' own write has landed, what Google did to the days with that write (a kept end, cleared notes, whatever has not been seen yet) can't be told from a change made in Google Calendar since: the stored expectation is a digest, and does not say whether it came from a read or from a prediction. Was: Finish read the days against the prediction (`afterMaster`, as the values `rebase` took for explained) and kept whatever it did not explain, so a change whose read back failed (the network gone right after the series' write) was finished with the day at its own time wrong and nothing said. Chosen: Finish first asks Put back's question, by the one function (`asLeft`): does Google hold each series as this change last read it? Where it does, the plan stands as journaled and the run compares and sends. Where it does not and the series' write has landed (a field group the change changes is no longer as it was before), the days are the plan's (`planOver`), the run sends every day that is not as planned, and Finish's answer says, under one lead sentence ("This change had stopped part way, and Google Calendar no longer held the event as it was left."), for each day Finish changed, what Google held at that read and what Finish set, with the values ("On Mon 8 Mar 2027 Google Calendar held the notes 'Sample remote note'; Finish set the notes 'Sample notes', as this change has it."; "Mon 8 Mar 2027 was deleted in Google Calendar; Finish put it back as this change has it (10:00 to 11:00)."), made from Finish's first read and its last and from nothing else (`replaced`, `replacedWords`); never a claim that someone changed it. Where the series' write has not landed, nothing of Google's own doing is in what it holds, and the reading by three states stays (before, now, plan; data only): a day or field changed there since, in something the change changes, is kept and named. A field of the series the change did not change stays as Google has it either way. The promise dropped, plainly: until the larger model is built, a day changed in Google Calendar between a stop and Finish, on a change whose series write had landed, is replaced by Finish and named in the record; it is not kept, and Finish does not ask first. The half the owner accepted that is not built (Finish offers first, with a list, when Google really changed since the stop's read) needs the stored fact of whether the stop read Google, a change to a stored form, and stays with [the larger model](google-change-under-last-write-wins.md) ("Carried In From 1.5.6", A2). Else: go on guessing (the wrong end stays, in silence); or refuse Finish there (a change that can be neither finished nor put back). Cost: that promise, on ten assertions restated: google-series-recovery 4, google-finish-first-write 3, google-finish-drag 2, and google-series-recovery-grounds 1 in its words. Verified: those tests; `google-series-stops`, its expectations now worked out by date arithmetic; the 24 stop cases of the read-back check.
- **The journaled plan is written once.** Status: rule, built for 1.5.6; the fault it ends is in 1.5.5 too. The rule: a pending change's plan (`google_pending.targets_json`) is the change's plan. Its content is written before the first request and never replaced, by a run of either side. A run of the change's own side writes into it only what identifies a thing at Google: the UID of a series or of an event of its own that it inserted, and that the insert is done (`run()`'s `$identify`). A Put back run writes nothing there: its targets are the befores, journaled with their ids before the first request. Was: a run that stopped wrote its own targets over the plan, a Put back's befores or Finish's reading of the plan over what Google held then. So a Put back that failed part way left the state before the change as the plan, and a Finish after it had nothing of the change left to finish: on the tree before this build a day's own title was lost and the change stayed half done, with nothing said of the day. The mid-change reading hid it wherever it kept what it saw. What writes the pending row, every site: `targets_json`, `befores_json`, `local_json` and `plan_json` by `apply` before the first request, `targets_json` after that by `$identify` alone; `steps_json` by each step and by the read that settles its expectation; `made_json` by each store; the status and the error by a stop, a Put back's refusal and a recovery's start. Cost: none known. No stored form changes, and a row an older version left with its plan written over is read as it stands. Verified: `google-put-back-fails.test.php` (a rename and a time change, each stopped, a Put back that fails part way, then Finish and, apart, Put back again; the whole event deleted the same way), failing before the rule; `google-hidden-recovery` (a Put back refused for days Google would hide leaves the plan as it was, and Finish completes the change).
- **A step's expectation is what the run last read after it.** Status: rule, 2026-11-14, built for 1.5.6; it restates "What a stopped change is compared with is what Google held when the change read it at its stop". The rule: what Put back and Finish compare a stopped change with (`asLeft`, the one question both ask) is what Google held when the run last read the series after the step: after the series' own write, at the run's end, or where it stopped. Whether a prediction was journaled with the step before its request makes no difference: the read is the expectation of the series' last step that takes one (a write to the series, an insert of one, a day's write). Was: only the run's last read replaced an expectation, and only where one had been journaled with the step. A write to a series with no row here journals none, since the prediction is made from the row (a Put back bringing a deleted series back), so a Put back that brought the series back and then stopped could not be put back again: with no expectation the fall-back asked whether Google held the series as before the change, which it did not yet. Steps that journal no expectation before their request: a series' write where the series has no row here, no UID or no first read; and the steps that take none at all, a removal (read by whether what it removed is still gone) and an event of its own (read by its etag). The fall-back, "is Google as it was before the change" by content, is reached in two cases only: the run made no read after such a step (the network or the process gone right after the write), and a pending row an older version wrote whose step carries no expectation; a row written by 1.5.5 is read exactly as before. There Put back's refusal says what is true: "Better-Cal has no record of 'Sample series' as Google Calendar held it after this change's last write, so it can't tell whether it changed there since." What a stop still can't tell apart: a step whose expectation is the prediction journaled before its request, no read having followed, from a change made at Google since; the stored form does not say which it holds, so Put back is refused there as changed since, and Finish sets the plan's days (the entry above). Else: journal a prediction for every step (another list to keep complete). No stored form changes: a step's `expect` is the field it was, and no older row is rewritten. Verified: `google-put-back-fails.test.php` (the whole event deleted, stopped, a Put back that brings the series back and then fails, then Put back again and, apart, Finish; the mirror where nothing is read after Put back's series write; a row with its expectations taken out, as an older version wrote them); `google-upgrade-from-155`.
- **A run gives its read only to a step it journaled itself.** Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-14, built for 1.5.6; it corrects the build of "A step's expectation is what the run last read after it" (above) to what that rule and the model say ("every read the run makes after a write of its own"). The fault (the independent read of the read-back build, its R1): a run that stopped read Google once more and gave that read to the last step in the journal, whichever run had journaled it. So a Finish that was refused at its first write, or before sending anything, after someone had changed the event in Google Calendar, made that change the change's own expectation: the next Put back ran and went over it with nothing said, and the next Finish replaced it and named nothing. The Put back half was in the tree before the read-back build too. Chosen: one condition in `GoogleSeries::run` (`$own`, the journal's length when the run began): `$settled` gives a read only to a step of index `$own` or later. A run that has written nothing leaves every expectation as it was, so in that sequence Put back is refused as changed at Google since, which is true, and Finish replaces the day and names it with both values. What it does not close (the second read of the release branch, finding 1): a Finish that has one write taken and its next refused has journaled a step of its own, its stop read holds the other person's change, and that read becomes the step's expectation; the next Put back then goes over the change unsaid, as before. The condition closes the fault for a recovery that wrote nothing, not for one that failed part way; the rest is carried (issue 274, A5). Cost: one state loses its accidental way out. A switch between timed and all-day left half done by 1.5.5 at a time Google hides days for (issue 271), where someone then changes the event in Google Calendar: Put back is refused because Google changed, Finish is refused for the hidden days, and before this the refused Finish laundered the change so that Put back then ran over it. Now neither completes and the notice can only be dismissed. It was first written here as needing an upgrade-time leftover; that was wrong (the same read, finding 2): a switch made by this version whose series write goes out as the connection drops and is not taken, on an event where someone then renames a day in Google Calendar, reaches it too. Three uncommon things in a row either way. It is carried to the rebuild of recovery (issue 274, A5) and named in the 1.5.6 notes. Else: leave it and name in the release notes that Finish can replace a change made in Google Calendar without naming it (a known loss with nothing said); or give the stopped change a third action. No stored form changes. Verified: `server/tests/db/google-finish-fails.test.php`, written by the reader as probes (80 checks; 7 fail without the condition: the refused Finish leaves the journal's expectations as they were, Put back is refused again and the other person's note and place are still at Google, the second Finish names the day with the note Google held); the whole database suite.
- **An answer to a repeating invitation leaves its days as they were.** Status: choice, 2026-11-14 (the read-back model, its second open choice), built for 1.5.6. Any write to a series can clear a kept day's own notes (probe 2026-11-14, 3.68: one private property, no rule in it). A write of the guest list alone was never probed, and under the rule it need not be. Chosen: an answer for all events, and its undo, go through one function (`GoogleSeries::writeBesideDays`): the series' write, a read back, every day that is not as it was before the answer sent once, a last read, stored as a poll stores it. Its plan is the series as it was. Where Google holds a day otherwise at the end, a Review notice says what it holds and what it held before the answer, by the function every other record uses. Else: file it until a live case shows a guest-list write clears something. Cost: one request for each day to put back and one last read when there is any; none otherwise (the read back takes the place of the list of etags). Not covered: an answer has no half-done state, so if the read after it can't be made the answer stands, nothing is compared, and the next poll stores what Google holds ([the larger model](google-change-under-last-write-wins.md), "Carried In From 1.5.6", B3). Verified: `google-answer-notes.test.php`; the four answer cases of the read-back check; `google-answer-skips`.
- **A read a change makes after its own write, answered below reader, is not compared with the plan.** Status: choice, 2026-11-14, made where the access work and the read-back rule met (their merge); recorded in the commit after it. The case: access drops to busy times only between a change's write to the series and the read after it. The read-back rule decides what to send from that read, and the access rule says a listing below reader shows no titles or notes and is never kept ("After the independent read of the access build: four rules", rule B). Read together a change would compare its plan with a blank listing: a series with an edited day, moved an hour, completed with nothing sent for that day, where the access build's own test has the change stop half done and Put back go through once access is back. Chosen: such a read decides nothing and nothing is said from it. In `GoogleSeries::run` the change stops there by the access rule's own decision on that list (`comparable`, through `GoogleWriter::admit`), half done, and Finish reads again once access is back; the read is not kept as the step's expectation and not stored, as before. For an answer to a repeating invitation, which has no half-done state, nothing is compared, sent or said from it, and the answer's own resource is stored (`writeBesideDays`). At view only a listing shows what Google holds, so it is compared as usual, and a day's write Google then refuses stops the change with the access refusal. Else: compare with the blank listing and let a refused day write stop the change (it stops only where a day write happens to be sent); or wait for access (a wait). Cost: the step keeps its prediction where the read was not kept, so once access is back, with nothing changed at Google, Put back can be refused as changed at Google since and Finish can say Google no longer held the event as it was left, where the prediction differs from what Google did (a zone change over a day with its own time); the end state is right both ways (the second read of the release branch, finding 3; a seconds-wide window). And at busy times only the read after a write is asked four times, about two seconds, before the change stops, because that listing never shows the write's etag. With the same merge an answer's first read is the one list `readNow` makes, which `writeBesideDays` takes in place of a read of its own: an answer for all events sends the list, the write and the read back, three requests. Verified: `google-access.test.php` ("a change stopped at busy times only keeps no read made at that level as what Google held: given back, Put back goes through"), failing on the merge without it; the answer cases of `google-series-read-back.test.php` (two lists, no read of the event by its id).
- **A change to one Google event reads Google first and asks only over the same field (1.5.7, slice 1).** Status: choice, built 2026-11-14 behind the setting `google_conflict_choice`, on as shipped (the revision "on as shipped, by the owner's choice on 2026-11-14" below). Chosen: every write that sends Google something for an event in no series (an edit, a drag or resize, Planned or Maybe, a delete, an answer to an invitation, and Review's and a plugin's edits and deletes, which come through the same `Events::patch` and `deleteEvent`) goes through one step, `Events::googleChoice` (the answer through `Rsvp` on the same read): `GoogleWriter::readAndStore` reads the event as every write's first read is, stores the read as a poll stores it (`apply` with `journal: true`, so it has its own Activity entry and an older entry's undo sees it), and compares six fields three ways with `GoogleConflict::threeWay(base, now, wanted)`, the one comparison, pure, its equalities the page's table through the one normaliser that exists (`GoogleSeries::fieldsOf`, now public). The base is the request's `over` (the fields as served, parsed as a request body is), else the row. An `ask` answers 409 `google_changed` with `fields`, `kept` and a whole sentence, left for after the transaction (`googleDeferred`) so the store commits and nothing of the request is written; otherwise only the fields to send go, whole (a time with its start, end, all-day and zone), over the stored row, and the write's condition is given the read and finds the rows equal to it. A delete is every field wanted at a value no map holds; an answer to an invitation is the same read with nothing wanted, so it never asks and only reports `kept`. Overwrite is the same request with `over[field]` set to `now`; there is no `force`. Else: the model's base from several sources, a normaliser for notes, a guard for the seconds between our read and our write, a separate path per kind of change; none built, by the owner's guidance for this release (last write wins while our own change is sent; when in doubt, ask). Costs: one read and, when Google changed something, one Activity entry per write with the setting on (the read replaces the condition's own read, so the request count of an unchanged event is as in 1.5.6); notes that differ only in formatting may ask once; a change at Google in the seconds between the read and the write is Google's 412 and 1.5.6's `google_conflict`; an API caller that sends no `over` overwrites a change a poll already stored. No stored form changes. Verified: `google-conflict-served.test.php` (for each field, over four events of every form a field takes, serve then parse from `over` is the identity, together and alone); the existing database tests near the change with the setting on; the independent tests of `threeWay` and of the request-level scenarios are written from the page by another worker. Revised the same day, after the checks met the product and after the independent read: Overwrite sends what the question showed as yours for each asked field, the time whole (sent again unchanged, a drag over an event Google made all-day became all-day while the question said six o'clock); with the setting off the answers carry no `kept` key, the one key of the conflict choice (a PATCH answer for an event in no series carries `occurrence` either way since (R2) below); the database suite ran as shipped, with the setting off, unless an environment switch turned it on (reversed below); one name per field wherever a person reads it, and Planned or Maybe is never called status. The edit harness runs with the setting off and has no expectation for it on (reversed below, (4)): the setting on is checked by an exhaustive table of `threeWay`, hand-listed request scenarios and eight mutations, by the rule that a generator earns its keep only where the code under it is not a few rules. Left, each rare, in #283. Revised again the same day, by three rules. (1) What needs no answer from Google is decided before Google is asked, and one change makes one read: an edit that also sets or clears the trip flag ran the trip rules after the conflict choice's read, so a refusal had already read Google once (the second read the suite showed was the test's call list carried over, not a second read: the choice's read already goes to the write as its condition). The trip rules now run before the read and set the flag back once they pass (`tripRules(putBack: true)`), so the read is stored over the row as it stands, a question commits no flag, and the commit writes the flag with the other local columns; else: run them after the read and take the flag back on a question, which would leave the store's Activity entry holding a flag nobody set. Every other single-event path checked against the rule: a delete (`googleDeleteAll`) refuses nothing locally after its read; Planned or Maybe on my own calendar is a patch and takes the patch's path; an invitation answer (`Rsvp::answerAtGoogle`) checks pending changes and the binding before its read, refuses after it only on what Google says (no longer a guest), and passes the read to its write; series paths (`SeriesPlans`) are not on the conflict choice and keep their flag written before the send. (2) One vocabulary and one way of writing a value wherever a sentence about the choice is composed: the 409's `message` is now the dialog's own sentences for the same fields (`GoogleConflict::question`, ported from `googleChangedAsk`: one noun per field, Planned or Maybe by its value, two values never joined by "to", the year on both times or neither, long notes cut where they differ), then what was not done; the app's sentences about a change to a repeating event name Planned or Maybe instead of "status". Held together by one table both suites read (`server/tests/fixtures/google-changed-words.json`, every field for a change and a delete); else: a server sentence of its own shape, which had drifted twice ("changed to 'Standup'", "to Maybe", a range joined by a second "to"). Cost: the server says times on the clock each value is served on, the app on the device's, so the app's test gives each timed value the same wall clock on the device's clock and checks every row in every zone; which clock a time is shown on is not what the table checks (filed separately). The app's own timed checks fix their clock the same way, so the test passes in every zone CI runs; the page's draft sentences for what is built gave way to a pointer to the table. (3) The tests exercise the conflict choice on, because it is the product being built: `freshUser` turns it on (superseded below: on by default, `freshUser` stores nothing), the environment switch is gone, and the test of the off path turns it off itself (`google-conflict-served.test.php`: no `kept` key, and an invented `over` changes neither what is sent nor what is written). Verified: unit, smoke, the full database suite with the setting on, and the eight `choice-` mutations. (4) The edit harness runs with the setting on too, reversing the sentence above that it has no expectation for it: a quarter of the interleaving cases, taken from their own last steps so the run is no longer, put an event in no series under a change at Google not yet read, and `x-choice` works out each field's outcome from three states the harness records (what was shown, what the stand-in holds, what was asked) and checks the answer, both sides, the one read and the Overwrite after a question; it calls nothing in `server/src`. The 1.5.6 refusal stays checkable with `--conflict-choice off`, which the gates do not run. Else: leave the setting on to the table and the scenarios alone, which checks the comparison and not what a run of steps leaves behind. Not reached by its draws: an answer to an invitation, an edit that also makes the event a trip, and the unlikely list (#283). Verified: the recorded seed clean in all three modes and 64 of 64 oracle probes. Revised again the same day, in the app, after a walk through the question in a real browser, by four rules. (A) The question interrupts the action rather than following it: a delete (from the editor, the panel, the keyboard, a trip's own delete) takes the event off the calendar and closes the panel or editor only once the server answers with no question or the person chooses Delete, so Cancel leaves them exactly where they were; a drag or resize keeps its moved block while the request is on its way, as before, and at a question that block goes and the event shows as stored (B). Else: keep every action optimistic and put back the editor and the panel on Cancel, which needs each caller to remember what it closed; or wait for the answer only where a question is possible, a branch per kind of event; costs: every delete now waits for its answer before the panel closes, typically well under a second, a series at Google longer. (B) At a question the server has stored what Google holds, so the app reads the event back (`GET /events/:id/occurrence`) and puts that copy in place of every copy held (`store.js adoptEvent`) before the question opens, after taking the caller's own local change off once (`revertOnce` in `actions.js`, so a failure after Overwrite cannot put back the copy from before the action either); an open panel follows the event to its new instanceId. The bug it fixes: on Cancel the drag's `revert` put back the copy from before the drag and then only the view's window was fetched again, so when the drag had crossed days and edge scrolling had moved the view, the stale copy sat on a day outside that window and nothing replaced it until a reload. Else: refresh the window alone at the question (what failed); costs: one more request per question. (C) Every button of the shared dialog (`.bc-stepup-actions`, which the question and the password step-up both use) is 44 CSS px tall at phone width, 31 on the desktop as before; else: one rule for every `.bc-btn` on phones, which would also grow toolbars and rows not reviewed here. (D) A toast after a change sent to Google Calendar names it once, in one function (`whereWords`: "Title changed, here and in Google Calendar", "Moved, here and in Google Calendar"), and the kept line under it begins "A separate change there was kept: ", or names Google Calendar itself when the line above does not (decided in `toast` from the line's own words, never by caller); the answer to an invitation says "Google Calendar passes your answer to the organizer"; the edit toast's list form became "Title changed" for every calendar. Else: one sentence per caller; the rest of the app's and the server's bare "Google" wording is listed for the owner and unchanged. Verified: `web/tests/google-changed.mjs` (a delete from the editor and from the panel, an edit, a drag, a resize, a failed Overwrite after a drag, Maybe: what every copy of the event holds while the question is open and after Cancel; the toast and both kept lines; the first B check fails with `adoptEvent` taken out); smoke in four zones; a walk at 1280x800 and 412x915 against the stand-in, the cross-day drag with edge scrolling included. Revised again the same day: on as shipped, by the owner's choice on 2026-11-14 after the dialog was walked through in a browser. `Settings::DEFAULTS` holds `google_conflict_choice => true`, and `Settings::googleConflictChoice` reads the stored value through `withDefaults`, so an account whose settings lack the key reads on and only an explicit `false` reads off; nothing stored is rewritten, and no migration. The setting stays an internal switch (not client-writable, not in the Settings page) so an operator can turn it off for one account, and is to be removed in a later release. `freshUser` no longer stores it; the test of the off path still turns it off itself. Else: off for one release, switched on in the next. Costs: an install that updates gets the question at once, with no release in which it is off; a change that 1.5.6 refused over a change at Google now goes through, keeps Google's other fields or asks. Verified: `google-conflict-served.test.php` (a new account stores no key and reads on, an empty settings object reads on, an explicit false reads off), unit, smoke and the full database suite. Revised again the same day, after the question was walked through against real Google in a desktop browser, by two rules (the page's (g)). (R1) An open editor's form is the person's until it closes: the drawer seeded its form again whenever the store gave it a new editor object, and the read-back at a question gave it one (the way back to the panel followed the event), so behind the question the title went back to the value the editor opened with and Cancel lost the person's change. The form, its focus and its dialog now run once per opening (`sameOpening`: the same state apart from where Back returns and what has been shown), and the editor's base becomes what the question showed (`shownAfter`), so a Save after Cancel goes through unless Google changed again: the question showed Google's value, so it was last shown. Else: put the editor's typed values back after each refresh, or let a second Save ask again, which the dialog's "takes you back to your change" does not prepare the person for.
