The panel's time fields close as they send, so from them the question no longer says Cancel takes you back. (R2) What shows after an answer is the answer: `updateEvent` toasted at once but closed the editor only after the window was fetched again, and the grid held the copy read at the question until then, seconds on a large calendar. The 200 of `PATCH` for an event in no series now carries `occurrence`, the event as saved, and `sendEventChange` puts it in place of every copy held as a landed change (so a window asked before cannot put the old copy back), unless a change of the event made here is still on its way; the editor closes, the grid shows it and the toast says it together. A series' answer carries none, and there all three wait for the refetch. Else: read the event back with a second request after each save; or keep the close on the refetch. Costs: the PATCH answer grows by one event record for every edit of an event in no series, on every calendar; a toast after a series edit now waits for the refetch with the close. No stored form changes. Verified: `web/tests/google-changed.mjs` (the opening and the base through a question, Cancel and a second Save; Overwrite in the editor and a drag's Overwrite, each with the window refetch held: the save resolves with the grid and toast already right), which failed before the change; the scenario "Overwrite after the question" (the answer carries the event as saved); smoke in four zones, unit, static, both conflict database files; a walk against the stand-in with the window slowed to four seconds. Revised again the same day, after an outside review of v1.5.6 to the release candidate (its third finding, P2, reproduced), by one rule: what the person wants is worked out from what they were shown. `googlePatchAll` read the request against the row as stored and applied `over` only afterwards, so a request that leaves part of the time out took that part from whatever a poll had stored since the person looked: a timed drag (start and end, no all-day setting) over an event Google had made all-day, once a poll stored it, was read as a move of the day and offered, then saved by Overwrite, as a midnight-to-midnight timed event; a start sent alone borrowed Google's end. 1.5.6 misread the same stale drag (it saved an all-day event); the page's (b) makes the rule explicit. Now one function gives the base, `Events::requestBase(userId, row, over)`: on the conflict choice's path (an event in no series on a Google calendar, the setting on) the row with `over`'s columns, else the row; and the request is resolved over it, once, before anything is compared: `googlePatchAll` passes it to `columnPatch` and to `googleChoice` (which no longer builds its own), the delete passes it to `googleChoice`, and `fromViewer` (whose clock a floating event's times are on) reads it when the body carries `over`. Each field the request touches is then whole from the base (the time as start, end and all-day; this filling reversed below), so the `$body` gate and a write of nothing new see the field as wanted, not a part of it. With no `over` (an API caller, a plugin, Review) the base is the row, as the page says, and every other caller of `columnPatch` (a local edit, a day of a series, a series plan, a trip's move, a plugin's reschedule) passes the row as before. Three neighbours of the same reading, each found by the new tests and fixed in the same change: (1) an all-day setting sent alone left the times as they were, so a timed event made all-day kept its clock times on an all-day row; a change of kind now converts both boundaries whether or not they were sent (`columnPatch`, every path, though with `over` the setting alone is refused since the revision "a time is one value" below: the app always sends the boundaries with it, so this changes only API callers, toward the one stored form of an all-day row); (2) the question's `was`, `now` and `yours` (`servedFields`) were not on the viewer's clock for a floating event, as everything else served is, so Overwrite's `over` was read back as another time and asked again; they now go through `Floating::span` as `serializeSingle` does; (3) `fromViewer` decided that a request's times float from `Floating::is` of the row, which is false for an all-day row whose floating flag a Google sync kept, so a request making it timed again was read as pinned; it now reads the flag, timed after the request. Else: apply `over` after resolving and patch the result per case (a branch for a stale all-day drag), which the rule forbids; or refuse a partial time request over a changed kind, which an Overwrite could not answer. Costs: one more read of the calendar row and one more parse of `over` per request carrying `over`, in the controller (for `fromViewer`), again where the patch begins and again in `googlePatchAll`. The zone is not in `over`, so the base's zone is the row's: after a poll changed the kind, a timed event saved over it keeps its instants but takes the polled row's zone (UTC, or the Home zone when made timed from a UTC all-day row), and a floating event's wall clock is read in that zone (reversed below: `over` and the body carry the zone, and the base's time is `over`'s, read in its own kind and zone). No stored form changes. Inventory, each a request-level scenario in both orders (Google changed and not yet polled; Google changed and polled before the request), the question's `yours` worked out in the test from the shown time and the request and the Overwrite (`over` = now, the body = yours) checked to save exactly that at Google and here: a drag and a resize of a timed event over Google making it all-day; a drag of an all-day event (dates, no all-day setting) over Google making it timed; a start alone and an end alone over Google moving the time; a typed clock; the all-day setting alone, on and off, over Google moving the event to another day; timed to all-day and all-day to timed with both boundaries; a zone change keeping the clock over Google making it all-day; a zone alone (touches no field: no question, Google's time kept and named); a floating event dragged on a device in another zone. (With `over`, each time here sent in parts, a start or an end alone, the all-day setting alone, a zone alone, is refused as 400 `client_outdated` since the revision "a time is one value" below, and that revision's one table replaced these scenarios.) The non-time fields have no equivalent: each is read from the value sent alone, and the base is read only for what a new place clears here (coordinates, the lookup's time), now compared with the place shown. Verified: `server/tests/db/google-conflict-base.test.php` (52 assertions; of the 48 written before the floating case, 15 fail with the base taken back out of `googlePatchAll` and `fromViewer`, every one in the polled order; the floating case failed on `servedFields` before (2)), the reviewer's three probes pass, unit, the full database suite, and the eight `choice-` mutations (two retargeted where their text moved). Revised again the same day, after an outside review of v1.5.6 to the release candidate reproduced two faults in the app, by two rules. (E) An opening of the editor has one base, for each field what the person has most recently been shown, and a Save's fields and its `over` come from it by one function. The editor had two: the form as first opened, which Save diffed against to choose the fields, and `editor.shown`, which went out as `over`. After a Cancel they disagreed, so a title typed back to its opening value counted as unchanged though the person had since been shown Google's: nothing was sent, the editor closed and the value was dropped. Now `baseOf` (`EditorDrawer.js`) reads the event as shown (`editorBase`) and the event as opened through the one reading of an event as form fields (`seedForm`, the same function that seeds the form) and takes, over the opened form, every form field where the two readings differ; `saveOf` diffs the form's request fields against the base's (`formFields`, now covering reminders, the trip flag, floating and Planned or Maybe, which were four separate comparisons against the event as opened) and returns the event as shown for `over`; the unsaved mark, the draft kept for Undo and the discard prompt compare with the same base. `shownAfter` sets Planned or Maybe as the question's status shows it, since the editor reads it from the event's relationship. A poll with no question moves no base, since the editor did not show it; a 200, kept fields and all, closes the editor, so no base outlives it. Else: compare with the first opening and add the question's fields to the sent set, which keeps two bases and resends a field the person put back to Google's value. (Q) Every question is answered exactly once, and every asker's own state ends on every exit. `askConfirm` held one question and a new one replaced it unsettled, so two deletes answered by Google at once left the first waiting forever, its `deleting` guard set, and a retry did nothing until a reload. Now the dialog is a turn (`confirmTurn` in `ConfirmDialog.js`): questions are asked one at a time in the order they arose, each names its event, and `sendEventChange` takes its place in line when the server asks, holds the turn until it asks and gives it back in a `finally`. A question that waited sends its request again when its turn comes and asks from that answer, so it never shows what Google held before another question was answered; if Google holds again what was shown, the change goes through, and if the event is gone, the action fails with the server's words. Else: displace the older question and settle it as Cancel, which keeps one dialog but drops an action the person never saw a question for; or show the waiting question's words unchanged, which can show a value Google no longer holds. Costs: one more request for a question that waited. Every caller checked: `ReviewPage` (subscription remove, `busy` released in `finally`), `SettingsPage` (device removal, no guard), `ActivityPage` (undo anyway, `busyId` released in `finally`), `EventSheetBody` (reply again, asked before `busy` is set), `CalendarSettings` (adopt, no guard), and every `sendEventChange` caller in `actions.js` (the `deleting` set in `finally`; the drag, resize and Maybe copies by `revertOnce`; the trip loops, which stop on Cancel). Verified: `web/tests/google-conflict-callers.mjs`, the real `EditorDrawer` in the stand-in DOM over every field (title, time, place, link, notes, Planned or Maybe, tags, calendar, trip, reminders) and every path (Cancel then Save, Cancel and edit back, Cancel and edit to Google's value, Overwrite, a second question, a poll with no question, a field not asked about), expected values worked out from the base rule (61 rows, 20 of which failed before the change), and two deletes at once, a drag and a delete, a waiting question whose event was deleted, and another question asked behind one (which failed before the change); smoke in four zones, static, unit. Revised again the same day, after a second outside read of the candidate (its R1, P2, reproduced), by the owner's rule that a time is one value. The base the previous revision built was `over`'s start, end and all-day setting over the refreshed row, whose zone it kept: an all-day event shown before a poll that made it timed in a zone west of UTC got a base of UTC-midnight dates in that zone, which `columnPatch` read as an old-form all-day row and converted, so an end sent alone moved the unchanged start back a day, and Overwrite saved it; a start alone moved the end the same way. That was the second finding in two reads from filling a time's missing parts from somewhere. Chosen: a time is one value, its start, end, all-day setting and zone together, whole or refused. On the conflict choice's path, a request carrying `over` that changes the time sends it whole (`start`, `end`, `allDay`, `tzid`), and an `over` naming the time names all four as served; one function, `Events::wholeTime`, says whether a time is whole and is used for the body and for `over`; a time in parts is 400 `client_outdated` ("Better-Cal was updated. Reload the page and make the change again."), from `requestBase`, before Google is read or anything written. The base's time is `over`'s, read in its own kind and zone (`overColumns` reads it over the row with `over`'s kind and zone set first, so the row's kind and zone never enter it); the request's time is read over that base and, being whole, takes nothing from it. The question's time values (`was`, `now`, `yours`) and `kept`'s carry `tzid`, so Overwrite can send `over` and the body whole from them. The clock-time flag is not part of the time: Google does not hold it, a poll never changes it and the API writes it on its own, so whose clock the instants are on stays the event's flag. With no `over` the request is read against the row as in 1.5.6, except that an all-day setting sent alone converts both boundaries. Removed: the filling of a part of the time from the base in `googlePatchAll` (every field touched made whole from the base). Kept from the previous revision: the base applied before the request is resolved, now only for whole values; the question's and Overwrite's values through `servedFields`, the reading of the served record; a change of kind sent with no boundaries converting the stored boundaries (a no-`over` path, which keeps the row valid). Else: keep filling partial times from the base and make the base complete with its zone, the second read's direction; not taken, because that mechanism produced a finding in each of two reads and a third part (the floating flag, a typed clock's zone) would be the next. Costs: a page that sends `over` with a time in parts is refused on its next time change or delete until it is reloaded, and no released version does: a tab left open from 1.5.6 sends no `over`, so it is never refused; its changes are read against the event as stored, as 1.5.6 read them, and it shows no question until reloaded (a 409 `google_changed` reaches it as the server's own sentence); a start sent alone is no longer something a client with `over` can send; one more key in each time value of the 409 and of `kept`. With it, by the owner's rule that the server never serves an event's time without its zone where the app can act on it: the grid shape (`Events::serialize` with `full: false`, which every list answer goes through: `GET /events`, `/search`, `/people/:id/events`, `/events/:id/links`) keeps `tzid` on every occurrence instead of only on a timed context moment, so a drag, a resize or a delete from the grid can send `over` whole from the copy it showed, with no read of the record first; about 28 bytes an occurrence, and the window's tag names the version, so no answer cached before the update is taken for one after. An all-day time's zone is served as UTC in the question's values. A timed moment (end equal to start) is no state here: `POST /events` refuses one, and nothing the app shows or sends is one (a Google row is stored an hour long, the editor will not save one, the grid keeps a quarter hour); the four older readings of a moment (create refuses it, a local patch stores it, a patch on a Google event sends it but stores an hour, an import and a poll make it an hour) are filed separately and unchanged here. Narrowed on 2026-11-15, after a third outside read found the entry check both stricter in words than the API's reading (an all-day time with clock boundaries was read as its dates, an impossible date as the parser reads it) and passable after it (a typed start without a typed end resolved an hour's time to a moment after the check): whole now means only that the four parts are present and of the types the API reads, and their values are read as the API reads any time; the one check of the values is on the resolved time, `Events::resolvedTime`, called by `googleChoice`, which a change and a delete both pass through immediately before Google is read, for the time resolved from the request (after `fromViewer`'s clock, `typedTimes`, the base from `over` and `columnPatch`'s reading and change of kind) and for the base read from `over`: its end after its start, else 400 `invalid_request`, "end must be after start" (the words of `POST /events`), nothing read or written. Two codes, because they say different things: `client_outdated` is a time in parts, which only a client older than the rule sends, and it tells the person to reload; `invalid_request` is a whole time that is not an event, which no reload fixes. Else: parse strictly at the entry (dates without clocks for all-day, real calendar dates), which would promise more than the API keeps anywhere else and still miss what a later reading changes. A request with no `over` keeps today's refusals: `columnPatch`'s 400 `invalid_request` for a timed end before the start, and a moment stored or sent as before; on a request with `over` a timed end before the start still meets that `columnPatch` refusal first (nothing read), and a timed end on the start meets the resolved check, both `invalid_request`. An all-day end on or before its start is neither: the API reads it as one day long, the end the day after the start, and the check sees that (#302). Whether to refuse impossible dates is filed as an API-wide question. No stored form changes. Verified: `server/tests/db/google-conflict-base.test.php`, one table: seven senders of a time (drag, a trip's move; resize and a trip's extend; a resize of the start; the editor's save of a title and a time; a typed clock; the all-day switch; a zone change) and the three senders of none (a delete, Planned or Maybe; an invitation answer reads no `over`), each with `over` whole, `over`'s time in parts (no zone; a start and end alone) and no `over`, the event shown all-day or timed, Google's copy timed west of UTC, east of UTC, in UTC or all-day, polled or not, plus the body's time in seven old shapes, an `over` that is no state in five ways, the window's occurrences, a floating drag and the setting off, every expectation worked out in the test; the reviewer's two cases are refusals with nothing read or written, and the same requests whole ask with yours as sent and Overwrite saves exactly it; the no-`over` drag, start alone and end alone (timed strings and dates) over five row states give the same row and the same Google copy as the 1.5.6-era code, the one difference the all-day setting sent alone, which now sends Google the converted end; the new mutation `choice-time-in-parts` and the other eight `choice-` mutations (one retargeted); unit and the full database suite.
- **A change to one day of a repeating event on a Google calendar is compared as that day, not as the whole repeating event (#268, slice 2).** Status: choice, built 2026-11-16 behind the same setting, `google_conflict_choice`; it builds for a day what the entry "A change to one event or one day on Google goes through over a change made there to another field, and asks over the same field" planned (that entry is now built for one event, 1.5.7, and for one day, here; the undo of plain fields is still planned). Page: [google-conflict-choice.md](google-conflict-choice.md), "Slice 2". Chosen: every path that sends Google a change to one day (an edit, drag or resize by the repeating event's id or by the day's own row, a delete either way, "Use series value", Planned or Maybe on a day, and Review's, a plugin's and the API's through the same entries) goes through the step slice 1 built (`Events::googleChoice`, `GoogleWriter::readAndStore`, `GoogleConflict::threeWay`, the deferred 409, `kept`), given the day. The base is `over`, else the day as the series state reads it (`GoogleSeries::dayStored`); "now" is the day's record in the read, else the repeating event's values there (`GoogleSeries::dayNow`); the whole list is stored as a read-back is, journaled as a poll's (`GoogleSeries::store` with `journal`); "still a day" is `Events::requireInstance` over the row as stored after the read. So a change to another day is stored and neither asked about nor kept. The question carries `day` and, per field, `from` (`series` when the repeating event's own value at that day changed with this read and the day shows it, else `day`), computed by `GoogleConflict::from`. A day's time is one value: with `over`, sent whole or 400 `client_outdated`. An answer to a repeating invitation reads, compares with nothing wanted and stores the same way, and is never asked. Choices made while building, each on the page: a day no longer a day gets `google_conflict` with its own sentence and a delete of it completes with nothing sent; a field the request sets at its base value that Google changed is taken out of what the path writes (for "Use series value", the field stays the day's own); "Use series value" sends the repeating event's value as stored after the read, and a change to that value alone is not asked about; an edited day whose row goes with the stored read is refused as before when nothing was asked, and its Overwrite answers not found when something was. Corrected while building: the page first had a rename of the whole repeating event as no change to a day with its own title; Google writes the new title into every record, so the day shows it and it is asked about or kept. Else: keep the whole-state refusal for days (someone renaming another day refuses a drag of this one), or a second comparison for days (refused: one rule, one function). Cost: one more store on every change to a day whose repeating event changed in Google Calendar since the last poll. Measured on real Google, 2026-11-16: P7 (a link changed on the whole event is written into every edited day's record) and P8 (days a rule change drops stay in the list as cancelled, never live) hold. Added the same day, by the owner's ruling: an opening of the editor has an identity (the event, and for a repeating event the day), fixed, and a base (what was shown), moved only by a question, so a day follows slice 1's rule through the same functions and a Save after Cancel goes through (the page, (l)); the kept line after an answer to one day's row is the day as the series state reads it (`Events::keptOfEvent`); Busy or Free is in the one list the app builds `over` from; and "Use series value" says "the repeating event's" in its toast and its Activity line. Checked in every time form: an all-day repeating event, a day of the other form, a zone with a clock change (a day before it, on its night and after it), and an event that keeps the same clock time in any zone; nothing in the product had to change for them. Verified 2026-11-16: `server/tests/google-conflict-day.php` (672 rows from the page's reading, and rows by hand), `server/tests/db/google-conflict-day.test.php` (the page's scenarios, client-shaped requests against the stand-in), `google-etag.test.php` (the refusal with the setting off, the question with it on), `web/tests/google-changed.mjs` and `web/tests/google-conflict-callers.mjs` (the day's sentences and callers).
- **One mapping of a parsed event to a row, for every source; a CalDAV client's alarms are the event's reminders and its zones are kept** (1.5.8, [#287](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/287)). Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-15 ("An alarm set or changed in a phone's calendar app is kept: it becomes the event's own reminder, as an import's does"). Chosen: `Feeds::columnsOf` is the one mapping from `Ics::parse`'s shape to event columns for a feed's or Google's poll, a CalDAV PUT, an imported file and the CalDAV time-range expander, with what differs by source as a table (`SOURCES`): whether its alarms are reminders (a poll's are not, since reminders on a feed's or Google's events are held here and a poll must not overwrite them; a client's and a file's are). Alarms are read against the row as the source last had it (its stored row, a day with no row its series, nothing for a new event): alarms equal to the ones that row is served with leave its own value, so an event following its calendar's defaults (served with no alarm) keeps following them; different alarms are the event's own list, and none is an explicit none. The floating flag is written by every source that carries it, so a zone a client gives an event with none is kept, and a time a client sends with no zone floats; an import adds the calendar's preset. `DavIcs::eventColumns` and the import's inline mapping are gone; their normalizations (zone names, an empty rule, validated skips, a status) were already `Ics::parse`'s. Else: keep reminders held here alone over CalDAV, as before, which reverted a phone's alarm silently on the next sync. Cost: an imported edited day whose file gives it no alarm under a series that has one now stores an explicit none instead of following the calendar's defaults. Verified 2026-11-15: `caldav-alarms-zones.test.php` (an alarm set, changed and removed; an event following the defaults through an edit; a day's alarm against its series'; a floating event given a zone), failing before the change.
- **Whether an event takes up time is one stored fact per event, read from and written to every source** (1.5.8, [#303](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/303)). Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-15 ("The editor shows the marking and lets a person change it"; an event made here on a Google calendar "follows Google's own default ... send nothing for the field and let Google decide, then store what Google answers"). Chosen: a column `events.show_as`, `busy` or `free`, default `busy` (migration 079), no row rewritten: an event already stored reads as busy until its source is next read, which for a feed or a Google calendar is its next poll. Read through each source's one mapping (`Ics::parseVevent`'s `TRANSP` for a feed, a file and a CalDAV client, `GoogleSync::toParsed`'s `transparency`, then `Feeds::columnsOf`), written to every outgoing form (`Ics::buildEvent`, so feeds, exports and CalDAV write `TRANSP:TRANSPARENT` for a free event and nothing for a busy one, iCalendar's default; `GoogleWriter::body` sends `transparency: "transparent"` for a free event and nothing for a busy one, so a patch from free sends null, which clears it). It is a field Google holds (`GOOGLE_COLUMNS`, `OwnFields` field `showAs`, the body key `transparency`), so it is compared, undone, carried into undo's snapshots and listed in the conflict choice like `status`; like `status` it has two values and so never asks, it is sent or kept. An edited day owns it as it owns its status. Google's documented default for `transparency` is `opaque` with no exception for all-day events (the Calendar API reference, Events resource), while Google Calendar's own page makes a new all-day event free. The owner chose, 2026-11-15, after hearing of the difference: a new all-day event made here on a Google calendar is free, as Google's page makes it, and sends `transparent` on its create; a new timed one is busy and sends nothing; a value the person picks always wins; every other calendar's new events are busy; an existing event keeps its value when it is made all-day or timed. One function holds the rule (`GoogleWriter::newShowAs(calendar, allDay)`), asked by the create path when the request sends no `showAs`, and served on every calendar as `newShowAs: {timed, allDay}`, which the editor reads to show the value a save will store; the editor sends `showAs` on a new event only when the person picked it. A Google series' master written with `transparency` copies it onto every exception record, a day's own marking included, and null clears it everywhere (the live probe of 2026-11-16, series-model.md, Google as a Contract; #318): a series' marking changed in Google Calendar reaches every day here, and a day that owns its marking under a change made here is sent again with it by the read-back (the rule "Exceptions are carried, not trusted"); the master write's prediction of its kept days does not yet model the copy. A body that says an event or a day whole over a resource Google holds (a day of a series sent again, a master written over, a moved calendar's edited days) names every key `GoogleWriter::body` sends only when set (`SET_ONLY`: `source`, `transparency`) as null when the row has none (`GoogleWriter::whole`), since a PATCH leaves out what it doesn't name; before, `source` was cleared by hand in one writer and `transparency` in none, so a day sent again to be busy under a series made busy kept its free (the interleaving seed 14546, case 151; `show-as.test.php` and its mirror, and a unit check that `SET_ONLY` is exactly what `body` leaves out). Every parsed event and every row carries `show_as`: it is set where each source's own data is read (no TRANSP line, no `transparency` key, a request with no `showAs` are the only defaults), and nothing downstream reads a missing value as busy. Else: a boolean column (`busy`), turned down so the value reads as the editor says it; a column on the calendar, turned down since a source marks each event. Verified 2026-11-15: `show-as.test.php` (a feed's TRANSP and an outgoing feed, an import, a CalDAV client's write served back, the API and its undo and refusal, Google's read, a new busy event sending nothing, a write sending `transparency` alone, a change in Google Calendar kept under the conflict choice, a new all-day event on Google free and a choice winning, the rule served on each calendar), the threeWay table with the field added, the conflict words for it on the server and in the app, and the editor's payload and lines in the smoke suite.
- **How an event came to exist is the source of the write that made it, on any calendar** (1.5.8). Status: defect fixed, by the owner's rule of 2026-11-16. `events.created_via` says how the event came to exist here: the request's source (`ActivityContext`: `web`, `quickadd`, `api`, `caldav`, `mail:*`, `plugin:*`, `import`, `review`), and `feed` only for an event that arrived from outside, by a feed's or Google's poll. The defect: an event made here on a Google calendar was stored by the sync from Google's answer, which wrote `feed`, so every event a person made on a Google calendar, by the editor, quick add, mail, a plugin or the API, said it came from a feed; and since the "new" pill (`Events::isNewFor`) counts `feed` as an arrival, an event the person had just made there showed as new for its first day once the calendar was past its first minutes (reproduced by the test below before the fix). Chosen: the one place that inserts a row from a list Google answered, `Feeds::sync`, takes the write's source (`madeVia`) from the writer whose answer it stores (`GoogleWriter::apply` for a write's answer, `GoogleSeries` for a series write), and keeps `feed` for a read of what is elsewhere (a poll, and the conflict choice's read before a change, `apply(journal: true)`); the one writer that makes a day's row itself on Google (`googlePatchThis`, a change Google holds no record of) takes the request's source as the local writers do. Inventory of every path that makes an event row: a create here and on Google, quick add's commit, mail and Review's approval of it, a plugin's proposal (all through `Events::create`, the request's source); a plugin's sync (`PluginHost`, `plugin:id`, plugin calendars only); an import (`import`); a CalDAV PUT (`caldav`; Google calendars are read-only over CalDAV); a feed's and Google's poll (`feed`); a copy or duplicate, and a move to Google, which is a copy then a delete (`Events::create`); a whole calendar moved to Google (`GoogleMove`, keeps its rows and their value); a day made by an edit on a calendar here and the series plans (`ensureOverride`, `detachToCalendar`, `patchThis`, `SeriesPlans`, the request's source); on Google a new event, a new series and a day made by an edit (wrong, fixed here), and a day the series write reads back (`GoogleSeries`, fixed here; a day changed at Google and not yet polled that the read-back brings in takes the write's source, a rare and harmless label); an undo bringing a row back (its snapshot, or on Google the same row brought back by the sync, either way the value it had). Not changed: stored rows are not rewritten. An event made here on a Google calendar before this change keeps `feed`: past its first day it shows no pill either way, and what else reads `created_via` (Duplicates' trusted lineage, local calendars only; mail admission, `mail:` only) sees no difference. Quick add and the editor stay one source each (`quickadd`, `web`), and the text typed is not stored. Verified 2026-11-16: `server/tests/db/created-via.test.php` (the editor, quick add, the API and mail on a Google calendar, a new series and two kinds of day row there, a copy onto Google, an undone delete, a poll's arrival, a create here; the list's `isNew` for each), ten checks failing before the change.
- **Merging two people keeps everything either had** (1.5.8). Status: defect fixed, by the owner's rule of 2026-11-16: a merge treats all data connected to either name as connected to the merged person, with nothing dropped. A rename onto another person's name (`People::update`) merges the renamed person into the one holding the name; before, it moved their events and times and deleted the renamed row with its notes, its show-on-calendar setting and its start. Chosen: one function, `People::merge`, takes each column of the two rows by a stated rule (`MERGE_COLUMNS`): notes joined, the survivor's first and a blank line between, one copy when they are equal apart from surrounding space; shown on the calendar when either was, since a person the owner was looking at should not drop out of view by a rename; the earlier `created_at`. Identity (id, user, name) is the survivor's. Every row of every table that refers to a person (`REFERRING`: `event_people` and `availability`, the schema's two references to `people`) moves to the survivor, a link both had being one link. The journal entry holds both rows and every referring row before and after, so its undo puts both people back as they were. A database test reads the columns of `people` and the foreign keys to it from the schema and fails when one is added without a rule. Else: the survivor's notes and setting alone, as before. The sibling, a tag renamed onto another (`Tags::rename`): a tag's row is its identity alone and `event_tags` is the one table that refers to it, all of which the rename already moves, so nothing is dropped and it is unchanged; the same schema check covers it. Not covered: a half-done change on Google waiting in Review (`google_pending`) names people inside its saved plan, so finishing it after a merge would name the absorbed person (a Google change left half done, then a merge, then Finish: filed, not fixed). Verified 2026-11-16: `server/tests/db/people-merge.test.php` (both rows and both people's events and times after a merge, undo back to the state before, equal notes once, a tag merge and its undo, the schema check), four checks failing before the change.

- **A new version reloads the page when nothing would be lost** ([#323](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/323)). Status: owner's choice of the rule, built 2026-11-16. Chosen: when a new version activates behind an open page (the worker's `sw-updated`), the page reloads into it by itself if nothing would be lost, in sight or not; otherwise the Reload banner offers it, and the page reloads by itself at the first moment nothing would be lost any more. "Nothing would be lost" is one function, `whatWouldBeLost` (`web/src/app/drafts.js`), over the page read where each state lives (`pageNow`): an open layer, top ones included (`openLayers`, `web/src/ui/outside.js`, the same reading the keyboard's layer rule uses, now in one place: the editor, quick add, search, the palette, drawers, dialogs, menus, the day sheet, the welcome, the series panel, a confirm or password step-up); a page form with unsaved changes (`holdReload`, as before); a question open or held for asking (`questionPending`, so the Google Cancel or Overwrite question counts while it is being prepared); a toast still offering Undo or another choice; a drag, a list reorder or reschedule mode; a focused text field holding text; a write sent and not yet answered (`writesPending`, `web/src/app/api.js`, with calendar list writes not yet sent); and an app still starting, whose link or share is not yet where it goes. An open event's panel, a page and the view hold nothing: the reload keeps them (`saveResume`, the same `reloadForUpdate` the banner's Reload and any manual reload's snapshot use). The moments it looks again are events: the page's DOM changing (a layer closing, a toast leaving at the end of its countdown, a drag ending), a field losing the focus, the app being hidden or shown, a page change, a form's hold changing; no timer. Never a loop: the version a tab reloaded for is written in its session (`bc-reloaded-for`); a page reloads by itself at most once for a version, and a page that arrives from that reload still on another version offers it instead (`checkReloadTook`). Else: reloading only when hidden or on a page change, as before, which left an open page on the old code for as long as it stayed on screen; ignoring open layers when hidden, as before, since drafts survive a reload (the owner's rule names an open editor or dialog as something that holds it, so one rule applies in sight and out); a second check beside the layer reading for reload purposes. Cost: a page with an editor left open in a background tab now waits until the editor closes, where before it reloaded with the draft restored; a write or a question in progress holds the reload until the page next changes after it. Verified 2026-11-16: `web/tests/reload-rule.mjs` (in `smoke.mjs`), each input alone holding the reload and the page read for every input, the decision for a version (already running it, nothing lost, something lost, reloaded for it once), and the flow: an open editor offers and holds, its closing reloads once by the banner's path, the page after that reload offers and never reloads for the same version, a later version with nothing open reloads at once. Not verified in a real browser or an installed phone app: the worker's message arriving, the DOM observer firing on a layer closing, and a hidden page reloading. Where: `web/src/app/drafts.js`, `main.js`, `api.js`, `ConfirmDialog.js`, `keyboard.js`, `web/src/ui/outside.js`. Settled 2026-11-16.

## Request Credentials

- **What a request may write, and when its credential is checked.** Status: choice. A write made under a request is one of two things. Either it is a change to what the account holds, made inside a transaction in which the request's session, API key or proved password was checked with a lock: `Undo::write` where the change is journaled, `RequestAuthority::checkedTx` where it is not. Or it is bookkeeping the server keeps about requests themselves, which belongs to no account and is written the same whoever asked. The bookkeeping is a fixed list of tables, `RequestAuthority::BOOKKEEPING`, each with its reason: throttle counters (`rate_events`), admission to the model and to outside actions (`model_admissions`, `external_action_admissions`), lookup caches (`http_cache`, `geocode_cache`) and the server's health record (`system_health`). Nothing else is on it. A write to any other table outside a checked transaction is a journaling misuse: logged in production, failing the suites. The check must be of the request's own credential: a locked check marks its transaction with what it proved (the key's id, the session's hash, the password's hash), and only the request's own among them covers its writes; a check of another account's key, or of the account's own key under a browser session, covers nothing. Signing in is a request made with the password and writes its session as that. Why: a credential revoked after a request signed in then stops every change the request has not yet committed, whichever route it came by, and a new route can't forget the check. A flow that ends its own credential (signing out, a key revoking itself, a password change) checks it and ends it in one transaction and writes nothing after; a password change signs in again and writes the rest as the new session. The account's change marker moves in the checked transaction of a request that may change things, not after the response. A request's work run after its response (a plugin's queued job) runs under the same credential. Verified 2026-11-06: the write-boundary checks in the PHP suite (among them a transaction that checks another account's key, or the account's own key under its session, and then writes, which is a misuse, and the request's credential checked beside another's, which covers the write), `server/tests/db/revocation.test.php`, and the completeness test run with every request operation's credential registered. Where: `server/src/Domain/RequestAuthority.php`. Settled 2026-11-05 for 1.5.1.
- **An undo and its CalDAV journal are one transaction.** Status: choice. The undo's restore, its mark as undone and the `dav_changes` rows that tell CalDAV clients are written in the one transaction that checked the request's credential, the rule above applied to the journal: check and write together. Else: the journal in a checked transaction of its own after the restore commits, which 1.5.1's first candidate had, where a credential revoked between the two left the undo done, the request failing and CalDAV clients never told. Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/db/undo.test.php` revokes the credential the moment another connection sees the undo committed, and the undo still answers ok with its journal row written. Where: `Undo::apply`. Settled 2026-11-05 for 1.5.1.
- **Every CalDAV journal row is written with its change, and a journal that fails fails the save.** Status: choice. Chosen: the `dav_changes` row and the synctoken bump are written in the transaction of the change they tell about (its `Undo::write` or `tx`), whatever made it: the app, CalDAV, a feed poll, a plugin's sync, an invitation answer, a migration; a journal row asked for outside a transaction is refused as a mistake in the code. A failed journal write rolls the change back and the request fails visibly. Else (was, to 1.5.1): the journal's database errors caught and logged, kept for a rolling deploy before migration 003, so a change could be saved with CalDAV clients never told; a feed poll and an answer's journal also ran in transactions of their own after the change committed. Why: deploys now pause traffic and migrate before resuming, so the reason for the catch is gone, and a change a sync client never hears about is a silent divergence, worse than a failed save the person sees. Verified 2026-11-06: `server/tests/db/dav-journal.test.php` refuses the journal's inserts and the create fails with no event, entry or token change, then saves once the journal is writable. Where: `ChangeLog::record`, `ChangeLog::recordMany`, `Feeds::sync`, `Rsvp`. Settled 2026-11-06.
- **An import tells CalDAV clients what it brought** (1.5.8, [#289](https://github.com/Oshyan/better-cal/issues/289)). Status: defect fixed. The import wrote no journal row, so a client holding a sync token for the calendar it went into learned of nothing until something else changed there. Chosen: the import journals in its own transaction, as the rule above asks: each object the file brought as added, each series the calendar had that gained an edited day from the file as changed; its undo journals through `Undo` as every undo does (every object it restores, as changed). An import into a new calendar journals too, so the rule has no exception. Else: journal only an import into an existing calendar, since a client has no token for a new one; one rule was preferred over a saved row per event. The other writers of events were inventoried against the rule (`docs/design/research/code-review-2026-11-14/special-cases.md`, family 1): all follow it or write nothing iCalendar carries, but the direct writes of Finish and Put back of a half-done Google series change (`GoogleSeries`, outside its `store`, which journals), left to the owner. Verified 2026-11-15: `dav-journal.test.php`, "a file imported into an existing calendar, and its undo", failing before the change.
- **A job queue's state is the server's own record.** Status: choice. A request's work run after its response (a plugin's queued job) runs under the request's credential and is checked at its writes; whether the queue claimed it, finished it or failed it is the server's, written as no request (`RequestAuthority::asServer`). The `jobs` table is not on the bookkeeping list, because a job row is queued by a request, under its check; only its claim, done and failed marks are written apart. A credential revoked meanwhile fails the job once, for good: nothing is left running until stall recovery, and the worker never reruns it with no credential. Verified 2026-11-05: `server/tests/db/revocation.test.php` revokes a session and an API key during a run. Where: `Plugins::runQueuedNow`, `JobQueue::markFailed`. Settled 2026-11-05 for 1.5.1.
- **Which API key made a change is kept on its Activity entry** (1.5.9). Status: the owner's choice, 2026-11-16. Chosen: `Undo::record`, the one function that writes journal entries, stores the request's API key (`RequestAuthority::current()->tokenId()`, set by the web API's bearer check and the CalDAV key check) as `mutations.token_id` with the key's name as it is then, `token_name` (migration 081, additions only, no foreign key); an entry made from a session, with a password or by the server has neither. Activity serves them as `key: {id, name, revoked}`, `revoked` read as the key's row being gone (InnoDB keeps auto-increment ids across restarts since MariaDB 10.2.4, so an id is never another key's), and the page shows the name in the source badge, "Agent/API · Sample agent", with "(revoked)" after it once revoked. Revocation is unchanged: it still deletes the key's row. Else: keep revoked keys as rows with a revoked time, so the entry could join the row for its name; not taken, because the key row's existence is what says a key is valid in eight places (`ApiTokens::stillValid` and `assertStillValid`, `SubscriptionAuthority`, `PushSubscriptions::LIVE`, `NotificationDestinations`, `Calendars`, the CalDAV key check, and the cascades that remove a revoked key's push and feed channels), and SECURITY.md's revocation promises rest on it; or store only the id, which names nothing once the key is revoked. Cost: a key renamed later (there is no rename today) would leave older entries with the old name; one read of the key's name per entry. Entries recorded before this have no key, and none is inferred. An event's own `created_via` keeps `api` and `caldav` with no key: the key of the request that made an event is on its create entry for the log's 90 days. Verified 2026-11-16: `server/tests/db/activity-key.test.php` (a change by one key over the API and another over CalDAV, a session's and the server's, the key revoked and the entry still naming it, undone from a session).
- **The address cache stays bookkeeping.** Status: choice. `geocode_cache` is keyed by the location text someone typed, and stays on the list: its row is the same whoever asks (a text and where it is), it belongs to no account and nothing reads it but the same lookup, so a revoked request can at most add an address string to a shared cache. `http_cache`, keyed by a URL, is the same shape. Settled 2026-11-05 for 1.5.1.

## Moving Through the Calendar

- **When a move has arrived, and what a month step starts from.** Status: choice. Chosen: a move (Today, an arrow, a jump, a view switch) has arrived once the view has reported the days it shows with the day gone to among them, and the month grid is not still gliding there; until then what is on screen is on its way, and where you are is the day gone to. One predicate (`arrived`) feeds every reading of where you are: the arrow keys, a view switch, and a step. Next and Prev in the month and agenda views step from the month the view names once it has arrived; until then from the month of the day gone to, so a second press mid-glide steps on from the first. A view whose window cannot frame a month names another month than the one gone to even after arriving: the phone's 3-day ribbon shows about two weeks, so a 1st landed on reads as the month before; the agenda's list shows the next month's events for a month with nothing in it. There the month of the day gone to stands while that day is on screen, in both directions. Else (was, 1.4.3 to 1.5.2): three readings of "still on the way" side by side (the grid's glide flag, a 900 ms timer after a press, the report sequence) with a branch for the ribbon and one for the agenda's Prev only; the timer's window was already covered by the report sequence, and in the agenda a Next after a Prev into an empty month skipped the month on screen. Why: a timer is a guess at an event the store already records, and the step rule follows from what a view's window can show, not from which view it is. Verified 2026-11-06: the smoke tests "next month:" and "prev month:" (before the view has shown the anchor, mid-glide, arrived, scrolled on with the anchor still on screen, the ribbon on a 1st, the agenda's empty month, both scrolled off), and in a browser the view checks "month view: Next five times" and "month view: a glide arrives after a load that changes no heights". Where: `web/src/app/actions.js` (`arrived`, `hereKey`, `stepMonth`), `web/src/lib/glide.js`. Settled 2026-11-06.
- **A glide under a load.** Status: choice. Chosen: a glide (Today or an arrow in a month view, over a near distance) heads for one row and ends in two ways only: the scroller arrives within a pixel of it (or of as far as the scroller goes), or you take the scroller, by an input on it (wheel, touch, pointer, key) or a scroll that moves away from the target, since a glide only ever closes in. Rows changing height under it keep the same week, the same way into it, on screen, and aim the glide again from there; a change that moved neither that place nor the target leaves the scroll under way alone. The browser's scrollend says nothing: it fires for the grid's own writes as for yours. Else (was, three rounds in 1.5.0 and 1.5.1): scrollend among the inputs that end a glide, then a counter of the grid's own writes to swallow their scrollend, which assumed one scrollend per write, then a threshold because an unchanged write still stopped it. Why: the end of a glide is defined by its distance, which the grid measures at every scroll, not by an event whose source it cannot tell. Cost: a smooth scroll stopped short by something that is neither an input nor a move away (a scroll from elsewhere on the page) leaves the glide marked under way until a load aims it again or the next move. Verified 2026-11-06: the smoke tests "glide:" (the distance rule) and "same place:" (the place kept across fixed, fitted and resized rows); the browser checks above. Where: `web/src/ui/MonthGrid.js`, `web/src/lib/glide.js` (`glideDistance`, `samePlace`). Settled 2026-11-06.
- **Which month the month grid names.** Status: choice. Chosen: the month that fills most of the grid's visible day area as a person sees it: every row any part of which is between the bars counts each of its days by the pixels of the row that show, whatever the number of columns, ties to the later month; one function, `visibleMonthOf` (ui/monthmath.js), for fixed and fitted rows alike, read at the scroll position itself rather than at the last change of the rendered range. The toolbar label, the mini calendar's month, the month a Next or Prev steps from, the jump control's first month and the reschedule strip all read it. With the day gone to pinned at the top (gridAnchor 'top') the top row still names the month, as chosen there. Else (was, to 1.5.7): fixed rows counted whole rows from the first one at least half on screen, as many as the screen holds at a whole row's height, so a row 60% on screen had a full vote and one peeking in at the bottom none; and the label was worked out only when the rendered range changed, up to a tenth of a row before where a scroll stopped. On the phone's three-column grid Next to 7 of the 24 months of 2026 and 2027 (January and April 2027 among them) left the month before named over a screen 55% the new one, until the next tap or a 20 px scroll. Cost: one more count per scroll event, over the dozen rows on screen. Where Next lands is unchanged: the 1st 40% down on the phone's ribbon, the 15th in the month view. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/visible-month.mjs` (four screens, 24 months, a sweep of ±210 px around where Next lands, against the area worked out in the test; where Next lands the aimed month has at least 54.1% at 390x844, 54.7% at 412x915, 66.1% on desktop with fitted rows), and the view checks' "Next five times" rows at 412x915 and 1280x800.
- **Day view's all-day bars, and where a drag's glide starts (#206).** Status: choice. Chosen: day view's all-day bars move and resize by week view's code (`dragMoveBar`, `dragResizeBar` in `web/src/ui/TimeGrid.js`), not a copy: the day a drag lands on is whatever `pointToSlot` reads under the pointer, a column side by side in week view, the stacked day's panel in day view, and the drag glides along the scroller the days run on, sideways in week view, down the page in day view. Day view has both gestures: the bar taken into another day's panel moves there, keeping its length; an end taken up or down adds or drops days. It has no gesture week view lacks: an all-day bar let go over the hours stays all-day and moves by whole days in both views. A change goes through the same handlers as week view's, so a repeating event asks This/Following/All and a Google event gets the conflict choice. With it, one rule in the drag controller: the edge glide is armed only once the pointer is clear of every edge zone, so a drag lifted inside one doesn't glide until the pointer has left and come back. Else: a second set of bar drags for the stack (the copy the issue ruled out); or the glide measured from below day view's sticky header (the pointer leaving the bar downward would then enter the top zone at once and glide back). Why: a bar in day view sits in its day's header, which sticks to the top of the scroller, inside the top edge zone: picked up, it glided a day back before it had been moved, and landed a day off. Cost: every drag lifted at an edge (a timed event grabbed at the very top of week or day view, a month chip at the bottom row) now waits for the pointer to leave the edge before gliding; before, it glided at once. Touch works as week view's bars do: a long press lifts the bar, and the ends are the same 8 px handles, narrow for a finger. Verified 2026-11-15: the smoke tests "day view:" under #206 (the real grid drawn into the stand-in DOM: both ends present, picked up at the top edge it stays, moved two days, its end three days down, its start up a day by gliding, a read-only bar without ends), each failing on the tree before the change; the browser checks "day view: an all-day bar dragged into tomorrow", "...end dragged into tomorrow" and "a repeating all-day bar asks" in `web/tests/viewchecks.mjs`, written and not yet run in a browser. Where: `web/src/ui/TimeGrid.js` (`barDraggable`, `dayScroll`, `barDrags`), `web/src/ui/DragController.js` (`armed`). Settled 2026-11-15.
- **What a press on the open panel's outside lets through.** Status: choice. Chosen (#305): anything that opens an event when pressed carries `data-opens-event`, and `opensEvent` (`web/src/ui/outside.js`) answers whether a press opens one: the nearest pressable thing under the press (that attribute, a button, a link, a field, a role of button) must be the marked element, so a control inside it keeps its press. The panel's outside-press rule asks that question and no other about events. Marked: calendar chips, bars, blocks and trip bands in every view, context tokens and marks, holiday labels and flags, the Agenda's span rails, search results, a group's rows, a person's events, linked trips and copies and trip members in the panel, Review titles that open an event, Activity entries. Else: one more selector for search results in the panel's list, which had already missed a holiday once (#183) and missed search results (#305); or `data-instance` on every such element, which also means draggable, hover card, prefetch and where the panel anchors and focus returns. Cost: a new surface that opens an event has to carry the attribute or its first press only closes the panel. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/opens-event.mjs`, the real panel open on one event and one press on each surface, on a desktop and a phone (the search result failed on the previous rule: the panel closed and the press was used up). Where: `web/src/ui/outside.js`, `web/src/app/EventPopover.js`. Settled 2026-11-15.
- **A layer above the panel answers its own presses.** Status: owner's choice of the fix (1.5.8, the neighbour of #305). The outside-press rule (`web/src/ui/outside.js`) is for what lies behind a surface: a press on the calendar or the page closes it and is used up, so it does not also act there. A layer stacked above it is not behind it: every surface that floats above the page (search, the command palette, quick add, drawers, the series panel, dialogs, menus, popovers, the day sheet, the welcome, the phone's bottom sheets, the drop choice and plugin card, the reschedule chip) marks its root `data-layer`, and a press inside one acts the first time. The event panel stays open under the layer, since when the layer closes the person comes back to the event they had open, and a search result picked replaces it. The order between two layers is not known, so a surface inside a layer (a menu in the editor, the series panel beside it) still closes on a press in another layer, except a top layer (`data-layer="top"`: toasts, a confirm, a password step-up), which is above everything; that replaces the selector list that exempted toasts and step-ups. The panel itself is not a layer: a toolbar menu still closes on a press in it. Else: one more exception per control in the panel's rule; or closing the panel when a layer opens, which loses the event the person was on. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/opens-event.mjs`, the real panel open and one press on search's Past, a tag chip, the box and Close, a command palette row, a confirm's Cancel, a toast's dismiss and the day sheet's Close, on a desktop and a phone (search's Past failed on the previous rule), and a menu in a layer closing on a press in its own and another layer but not a top one. Where: `web/src/ui/outside.js`, `web/src/app/EventPopover.js`. Settled 2026-11-15.
- **While a layer is open, no global shortcut acts.** Status: owner's choice of the fix (1.5.8). A layer above the page (`data-layer`: a drawer, a dialog, search, the palette, quick add, a menu, the day sheet) holds the keyboard: `shortcutActs` (`web/src/app/keyboard.js`) lets no single-key or Cmd/Ctrl shortcut act while one is open, whether it would open or replace a layer or move the calendar the layer covers, out of sight. Escape still closes what is on top; the palette's own key still closes the palette when it is the open layer; a top layer (a toast) takes nothing. The event panel is not a layer, so its keys (e, r, Delete, [ and ]) work while it is open. Else: a check for `n` over the New event drawer, which leaves `c`, `/`, `g` and Cmd-K able to cover or replace an edited draft or edit; or letting the keys that only move the calendar act, which changes what is behind a drawer where the person cannot see it. Cost: with jump to date or a menu open, `t` and the arrows wait until it closes. Verified 2026-11-16: `web/tests/smoke.mjs`, "a draft edited" (n over an edited New event drawer started a fresh one and lost the edits on the previous code), and "no layer open". Where: `web/src/app/keyboard.js`. Settled 2026-11-16.
- **The original of an event being moved fades, one rule for every kind.** Status: choice (1.5.8). DragController marks the element it moves (`source`, given by month view, which the day sheet drags through, and by week and day view for blocks and all-day bars) with `is-drag-source` while the drop would move it, takes the mark off while Option or Alt makes it a copy and when the drag ends, and `.is-drag-source` is one rule in the stylesheet. Before, week and day view marked their sources themselves, only blocks had a rule, and month view marked nothing, so an all-day bar, a month chip or a trip band left behind did not fade. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/drag-source.mjs`, a chip, a bar, a block and a band dragged, with a copy, a drop and Escape, and the stylesheet read (failed on the previous code). Where: `web/src/ui/DragController.js`, `web/styles/app.css`. Settled 2026-11-15.
- **Which plugins get a row in the sidebar's Plugins group.** Status: owner's choice (#257). Chosen: a plugin that declares `ranges` and has no `ownCalendars`, read from the manifest (`hasLayerRow`, `web/src/lib/pluginlayers.js`); a stored hidden state counts only for a plugin with a row (`layerHidden`), so a row unchecked before this change cannot leave a plugin's drawing hidden with nothing to bring it back. Else: a row for any plugin with the `ranges` permission, which listed Tides alone among several plugins and gave it a box that switched off only an optional shading already hidden with its calendar; a row for every enabled plugin; a row only while bands are loaded, which would come and go while scrolling. Cost: a plugin with calendars of its own and bands tied to none of them has no toggle for those bands (none exists; the authoring guide asks such a plugin to tie its bands to one of its calendars). Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/smoke.mjs`, "plugin rows" (the sidebar drawn with a tides-like, a sun-like, a trip-like, a plain and a disabled plugin; failed on the previous rule). Where: `web/src/lib/pluginlayers.js`, `Sidebar.js`, `OrganizePage.js`, `App.js`. Settled 2026-11-15.
- **An empty view is one line with its starts inline.** Status: choice (#214). Chosen: `EmptyState` says in one line what the view is for and puts the starts that fill it after the words as small links (`actions`, a list of label and action), with no box, art or padding of its own; every page that uses it changes with it. An empty Agenda offers quick add, subscribing and importing; search says what it finds before a word is typed and, with no match, offers Search all or Every calendar when narrowed and quick add for the words; an empty Review offers subscribing. Else: the dashed box with a primary button, which took a screen's height for one sentence. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/smoke.mjs`, "empty search", "empty Review", "empty Agenda". Where: `web/src/app/PageShell.js`. Settled 2026-11-15.

## Search

- **The limit keeps the first of the exact order.** Status: choice. Chosen: a result's tier is its own fields' tier at the occurrence that stands for it (a generated day the series', an edited day its own), decided before any result is left out; every matching series is resolved first (its next matching occurrence, or under Past its latest ended one, read near now and further only when needed, within the request's expansion budget, a series with none taking no place), other rows take the query's limit as before, and the limit then keeps the first by tier and the start as shown. Else: the limit taken in the query on the series' own tier and first date, then the date and tier of the occurrence put in after, which kept a description match in an edited day over a title match elsewhere at limit 1, a series begun long ago over a sooner single event, and let a series with no matching day left take a slot and return nothing in it (1.5.5 review, N4). Cost: every matching series is read, about six generated occurrences each for daily and weekly series; 300 such series take about 40 ms and 1,800 of the budget's 10,000 occurrences, and well over a thousand fit before the search answers 422 `event_search_too_large`. Under `when=all` the query still picks single events by tier and then newest first, so within a tier its cut is not the shown order. Verified 2026-11-08: `server/tests/db/search.test.php`, "search limit: a series takes its place by the occurrence that stands for it" (limits 1, 2 and 20 against the full order, a series begun long ago under Upcoming and Past, a yearly series, a series with no matching day left). Where: `Search::search`, the [contract](../api-contract.md) Search section. Settled 2026-11-08.
- **All is Upcoming followed by Past.** Status: choice. Chosen (#304): under `when=all`, `Search::search` runs the Upcoming search and then the Past one, with one shared now, keeps every upcoming result before any past one up to the limit, and lists a running series once, among the upcoming. Else: one query ordered by tier and then date, which let a term's past matches in a better tier (55 past meetings titled with the words) fill the limit and leave out the one upcoming match that held them only in its description; or the same single query ordered upcoming first in SQL, which needs a second copy of the rules that decide whether an event on a zone's own clock is upcoming (Floating). Cost: two searches under All; over 20,000 events the timings stayed within a few ms of before, except a query with no match at all (both halves run, 73 to 106 ms). A running series with no matching date ahead is now listed under All at its latest matching ended date, as under Past, not at its first date. Verified 2026-11-15: `server/tests/db/search.test.php`, "search under All: upcoming first, however many past matches there are" (failed on the previous code, 6 of its checks), and the limit checks in "tiers, one field, no address, the groups". Where: `Search::search`, the [contract](../api-contract.md) Search section, [search-ranking.md](search-ranking.md). Settled 2026-11-15.
- **Search opens on All, every time, with no calendar filter.** Status: owner's choice (#304). Nothing about the last search is remembered on the device: the stored `bc-search-opts` and its reader and writer are gone, with no fallback that reads them. A choice made in a search (Upcoming, All, Past, a calendar, "Show them") is part of that search: it comes back with "‹ Search" and after a reload, and a new search starts on All. Else: remembering the last choice, which left a search for last month on Past and the next search for an upcoming event empty. Cost: someone who always wants Upcoming picks it each time. Verified 2026-11-15: `web/tests/smoke.mjs`, "search opens on All". Where: `web/src/app/SearchOverlay.js`, `web/src/app/resume.js`. Settled 2026-11-15.

## Places

- **Ask again before giving up.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.8 (#230, #150). One rule for both place jobs: an answer that doesn't settle the search passes it to the next way of asking, and the search gives up only when no way is left. What settles it is each job's own: the dropdown (`PlaceSearch::usable`, asked by `FallbackPlaces` for each service in turn) is settled by a result with every typed word that is near the person or named exactly what was typed; the background lookup (`Geocode::keeps`, over the forms `Geocode::variants` makes) by any pin for the full text or a form that keeps its start, and, for a form without the leading name, only by a spot holding the part the form starts with, never a town or an area (`PlaceSearch::AREA_KINDS`, one list of every service's words for one, also what the dropdown narrows to). In the dropdown, a far-only answer is not thrown away: its rows follow the next service's, near first, and stand alone if that service fails; credits name every service whose rows are listed. Considered for the dropdown: leaving far-only answers alone (the case #230 reports) and treating every far answer as unsettled (every search for a world city would wait for Photon). For the lookup: pinning a dropped-name form at whatever it finds (a "Venue, Town" would pin the middle of town) and checking the full text's own answer too (feeds write addresses with country names and postcodes a result spells differently, so correct pins would be lost). Costs: a far-only dropdown answer costs a request to every service behind it, Photon's 1 to 2 seconds among them, and someone without a Home location in a zone whose centre is far from them pays it more often; a failed full text in the lookup may cost one or two more cached requests; the street-number form, which until now kept any pin, no longer keeps a town or an area. The narrowing list grew with it: a trailing village, neighbourhood, district, country or postal code named exactly can now narrow a dropdown search that matched nothing. Verified: `run.php` (FallbackPlaces far-only, pin and no-bias cases; the forms and `keeps`), and `place-lookup-forms.test.php` against the real cache table, both seen failing without the change.
- **A location's text is read once, before any service is asked.** Status: choice, built for 1.5.9 (#328); the model is [location-reading.md](location-reading.md). One reading (`LocationText::read`): an optional name, an optional address and an optional rest, over parts split at any separator (comma, semicolon, a spaced dash of any kind or an em dash, a spaced `@`, `/` or `|`), with the start of an address stated once (a house number or range and a street name, or an intersection) and "not a place" decided by what is left once links, numbers, meeting words, later-words, dates and times and filler are taken out, provided one of them was a sign. Every place job acts on it: the background lookup and its sweep, the dropdown, and quick add's place end (`LocationText::placeLength`, the 1.5.8 with-clause rule, now also ending at a later part that asks a question). The lookup's forms are the 1.5.8 forms with the comma generalised, not a second rule. Considered: a branch per shape from #328's table (rejected by AGENTS.md: the shapes came from one install and are the tests, not the rule); sending the text with its separators rewritten as commas (unmeasured on any service, and it would change the first request for texts the services already place); sending not-places through and filtering their answers (spends the requests the change exists to save). Costs: a near-miss can be misread (a venue named only with a time word, a business that opens with "With"); a text with a non-comma separator and a name may cost up to two more requests the first time its full text misses, each cached. What is not a place, or a room alone, keeps the state a link always had (`geocoded_at` set, no coordinates), which is also what a miss stores; the two are told apart by reading the text again (`GET /geocode` answers `noPlace`), not by anything stored, and typed coordinates are stored as a lookup with no provider: no stored form changed. Verified: `run.php` (a table of every shape in #328 with invented text and the near-misses that must stay places, each with the forms the lookup asks; the dropdown asking nothing for a meeting, a room, a link or a note; quick add ending a place at a question), and `place-lookup-forms.test.php` against the real cache (nothing asked or cached for a not-place, coordinates answered, a dash address placed and cached for the text, a with-clause never sent, a failed service leaving the text uncached, and the sweep marking not-places tried without listing them). Every client surface that shows a location decides its pin row and Directions link by one function, `locationKind`, over the server's `noPlace` on each occurrence (computed from the text when serialized, never stored); considered: a JavaScript copy of the reading (two readings to keep in step). Verified by smoke (its results, and that no surface has a check of its own).

## Reminders

- **What a reminder says of the description.** Status: choice, built 2026-11-15 (#26). Chosen: the body's first line stays the time and the place; when the event has a description, a second line carries its start, flattened by `Sanitize::toText` (the one text-only rendering of a description, used for ICS too), its line breaks and runs of spaces made single spaces, cut at the last whole word within 160 characters with an ellipsis (one word longer than that is cut inside it). A phone shows the first line collapsed and the rest expanded; the email shows the same body, the line break kept. It goes in `body`, not a field of its own, so the push and the email read one composition. Else: (a) a field of its own that the service worker joins, which would make the worker and the email each compose the same text; (b) the description's own line breaks kept, which spends the few lines an expanded notification has on blank space. Cost: a description that is only a meeting link repeats the link the Join button already opens. Verified 2026-11-15: `server/tests/run.php` ("rem excerpt:", "rem payload: the description is the second line", "email: the description is a line of its own"), `server/tests/db/reminder-payload.test.php` (the scan's own path: an HTML description, none, and a day of a series edited on its own saying its own). Where: `Reminders::excerpt`, `Reminders::payload`, `EmailSender::buildMessage`.
- **A tapped reminder opens onto its event, never the calendar first.** Status: choice, built 2026-11-15 (#26). Chosen: the link waits in the address, the one place it is read. On a page load, boot opens the linked event (its window loaded, the event open) before it marks the app booted, so the calendar first draws with the event already open; until then the loading frame stays. With an app window already open, the service worker brings it forward and posts it the link (`open-event`) instead of navigating it, and the page puts the link in its address and opens it at once when its session is running, or leaves it for boot or sign-in to open. Else (was, to 1.5.7): the worker navigated an open window to the link, a full reload of the app, and boot drew the calendar and only then loaded the event's window and opened it, which is the calendar shown first; a timed hold of the calendar was not considered (fixes are event-driven). Cost: on a page load the calendar under the event waits one more request (the event's window, then its own); a page loaded before this version has no listener for the message, so the first tap after the upgrade, with that page still open, only brings it forward; the sign-in path still draws the calendar first (a link that arrived signed out opens once signed in). Verified 2026-11-15 by `web/tests/notification-open.mjs` (the real `sw.js` with a stubbed clients API, `main.js`'s `boot()` with its calls stubbed, `push.js` against the real store), each part seen failing on the code before; not on a phone, which the owner checks. Where: `web/sw.js` (`notificationclick`), `web/src/app/main.js` (`boot`, the worker's message listener), `web/src/app/push.js` (`handleEventLink`, `receiveEventLink`).

## Tests and Gates

- **The mutation list runs side by side.** Status: choice, built 2026-11-15. Chosen: every run the list needs (each unmutated baseline once, each mutation once) is a process of its own, the harness or the database suite on one file with its own patched copy and result file in a folder the runner made, and its own database made and dropped by that process; `--jobs N` runs N at once, the database test runs first. Nothing is judged until every run has finished, by the same code in the list's order whatever order they finished in, so the verdicts are the serial run's; a run with no result fails its mutation (and a baseline with none fails every mutation judged against it), and an id with no verdict fails the list. The runner's default is the logical CPUs, counted as the database suite counts them for `BC_TEST_JOBS` (the harness's `--shards` defaults to 1, so the suite's is the derivation followed), and `--jobs 1` runs them one after another; it waited at 1 until a failed drop could no longer cost a run its result (the next entry). The gates run it at 8 at once (the test server's CPUs; `GATES_MUTATION_JOBS`) and CI at 4 (GitHub's hosted runner for a public repository has four CPUs, shared with the MariaDB service): about 4 to 5 minutes there by the local ratio, against 12 min 45 s for the step before, an estimate until a run is read. Why: measured alone on the development machine, the list took 1,055 s one run after another, 555 s of it the three runs of `google-conflict-base.test.php` (about three minutes each, its baseline and the two `choice-` mutations that name it; the eight that name `google-conflict-choice.test.php` take about seven seconds each), and the 23 unmutated baselines 340 s; side by side it took 244 s at 8 at once and 296 s at 4, its floor the longest single run. Else: (a) selecting the mutations whose inputs changed since the last run (not to be built; this is the reason): a sound key is the patched file, the catching test, the runner, the harness, the database suite's helpers, the stand-in Google, the migrations, the libraries and, because the domain loads what it uses transitively, every file under `server/src`; over the last 60 pushes to `main` that key would have run the whole list on 44 and none on 16, of which 6 changed only documentation, so on top of side-by-side runs it saves the mutation step on about one push in six, and a narrower key (patched file, catching test and shared helpers, which ran 69% of the mutation runs over those pushes) would skip a mutation whose check stopped working through a change elsewhere in the domain, the one thing the list exists to catch; not built, the owner's call. (b) Running only the blocks of a database test that hold a mutation's named assertions: it would change what a pass proves (that the mutation fails those assertions and no other in the whole file), so not done. (c) Reusing the database suite's own run of a file as its baseline in CI: saves the 23 baselines, about a third of the serial time and little once side by side. Costs: more databases made at once, which meets a MariaDB 10.11 race (below) more often; since the next entry a drop it breaks is a reported leftover, not a failed run. A known hazard, not fixed here: a migration's `CREATE TRIGGER` on a table that already has a trigger (042 on `calendar_moves`) copies the table's `.TRG` file to `.TRG-` and deletes the copy after the statement only if the statement's DDL-log slot is no longer active; MariaDB frees that slot first, so another connection's DDL that takes it in between leaves the copy (read in 10.11's `sql_trigger.cc` and `ddl_log.cc`). Every one of the 29 databases left on the development server held exactly that file, and `DROP DATABASE` then fails with errno 39 and the process, its result unwritten, fails. Not reproduced on demand: 108 migrations twelve at once and about 5,000 `CREATE TRIGGER` statements beside other DDL left none. Its effect is dealt with in the next entry. Verified: the whole list serially with the old runner and at 4 and 8 at once with the new one, the 114 verdict lines identical, counts included; and three injected faults at 4 at once, each a named failure and a non-zero exit: a patch whose text no longer occurs, a mutation that changes only a comment (trips nothing), and a mutation's database test process killed mid-run (no report, exit 9).
- **A failed cleanup is not a verdict.** Status: choice, built 2026-11-15.
