Every test process that makes a scratch database computes and writes its result first (the suite's report, the harness's findings or a shard's partial ones, the interleaving report, a trace, a comparison's files) and drops its databases after, through one helper, `ScratchDatabase` in `server/tests/db/scratch.php`; a drop that fails prints one line, `scratch database not dropped: NAME: the server's reason`, and changes nothing else: the process exits with the status its results earned. No retry and no wait. Each runner's summary says how many it and its children could not drop (`scratch databases not dropped: N`; a parent counts its children's lines and repeats them when it does not print their output), and `php server/tests/db/sweep.php --older-than 6h [--drop] [--remote] [--only NAME,...]` lists, and with `--drop` drops, the leftovers: only names under `ScratchDatabase::PREFIXES` (`bc_test_`, `bc_edits_`, `bc_interleave_`, `bc_trace_`, `cmp_scen_`, `cmp_issue_`), only older than the age by their oldest table on the server's clock, never one a connection is using, and never on a host other than 127.0.0.1, localhost or ::1 without `--remote`; a database with no tables has no age and is left, with what to do by hand when its folder holds a stray file (remove the file in the server's data directory, in its container if it runs in one, then drop the database). Why: the drop failed with errno 39 when MariaDB left a trigger backup in the database's folder (the entry above), and the drop sat in a `finally` before the result was written, so a passing run became a crash with no report, three times in one week's full gates. Else: a retry (it cannot succeed: the file stays until removed by hand), or deleting the stray file from the runner (it needs the server's filesystem, which CI's service and a remote server don't give). Where: `server/tests/db/run.php`, `tools/edit-harness/harness.php` and `interleave-cli.php` (its rotation every 50 cases too, and `--oracle-probes`, whose `exit` inside the `try` had skipped the drop altogether), `tools/google-probe/record-app.php`, and the mutation runner's count, through the helper (each already loads `server/tests/db/lib.php`, so none takes a new dependency); `tools/compare/boot.php`, `issues/replay.php` (whose report is now written before the drop) and `perf/bench.php` (at exit), with the same few lines in place, since they load another checkout's code and nothing of this tree's tests. A drop before a create of the same name (`replay.php`, `bench.php`) is setup, not cleanup, and still fails the run. Verified: `BC_TEST_DROP_FAILS=1`, a seam in the helper that sends the drop for a name the server refuses, in each runner: the suite on one file (exit 0, 457 passed, its report written, one drop reported) and whole at its default jobs (every child's drop counted); the same file under a mutation (exit 1, 54 failed); the harness alone and on two shards (exit 0, findings written, 1 and 2 reported) and under a mutation (exit 1); the interleaving mode alone (exit 0), under a mutation (exit 1) and its oracle probes (exit 0, 80 caught, 3 reported, one per database). The sweep against databases made for it: an old one dropped, a younger one and one with no tables left, one outside the prefixes never listed, a remote host refused, and one given a stray `calendar_moves.TRG-` in its folder failing exactly as the gates did (1010, errno 39), reported and left. And once without the seam: the next ordinary run of the whole suite met the race itself, one database of 107 left holding `calendar_moves.TRG-` from migration 042; the run reported it by name, passed 22,744 of 22,744 and exited 0, where before the file's process would have died with no report. The whole mutation list at 8 at once after the change: the 114 verdict lines identical to the serial run's, 241 s.
- **A test file that makes an account per scenario, and the longest table, run in parts.** Status: choice, built 2026-11-15. Measured first, with a timer around each part of `google-conflict-base.test.php` (185 s alone, half the mutation list's serial time and the floor of the list run side by side): 597 scenarios and 638 fresh accounts, and 142 s of it in `freshUser`, 0.22 s an account, nearly all of it `password_hash` at the product's default (bcrypt, cost 12 on this PHP); the requests, the event made at the stand-in, the polls, the rules scan around each block and the stand-in itself were 37 s together. Chosen, in two parts. (1) One hash per process: `Setup::createOwner` takes the password's hash from a caller that already has one (`?string $passwordHash`, last and optional), and `freshUser` hashes its one fixed password once per process with `password_hash(..., PASSWORD_DEFAULT)` and passes it, so every account is still made by the one function that makes accounts and still holds a real hash at the real cost; every other caller (`bin/install.php`, the install page, `bin/seed.php`, the compare tools) passes none and hashes as before. The tests about passwords and signing in (`credentials-*`, `credential-barriers`, `resources-live`, `mail-mysql`) make their own accounts with their own hashes and do not go through `freshUser`. (2) The table runs as four files: `server/tests/db/google-conflict-base.php`, not a test file, holds the table and every scenario once, with a map of slices by Google's copy (west of UTC, east, UTC, all-day; the scenarios outside the table in the first), and `google-conflict-base-<slice>.test.php` each name one slice; every slice stops before its first scenario unless the map covers Google's four copies once, the other scenarios once, and every slice has its file. The suite already runs each file in its own process and database side by side, so the files are the unit; the mutation runner's `dbtest` takes a list of files, runs each mutation once per file and judges them as one run (the assertions they add together; any file not running to the end, or not running clean unmutated, fails it). Else: shards declared by one file and fanned out by the runner (a new convention in two runners and the gates); sixteen files, one per cell (most of each would be a fresh database and its migrations); for the hash, a lower hashing cost the test bootstrap sets (a second path production would carry), or a copy of `createOwner` in the test library (two implementations of making an account, and a drift check to keep them equal). Costs: one optional argument on `createOwner`; four small files and one include where there was one file; a row is still added in one place. Verified: the label lists of the file before and of the four slices after, from `BC_TEST_REPORT`, are the same 4,682 labels, each once; the ten `choice-` mutations trip the same named assertions, the same counts (450 and 64 for the two of this table); a runner self-check, a mutation naming a slice that does not exist, fails unjudged; the whole database suite passes (22,744). Times, the same machine, the database suite at its usual jobs (the CPUs, 16) and the mutation list at `--jobs 8`: the table 194 s as one file in the suite, 23 to 28 s for each of the four after; the database suite 207 s wall and 585 s of user CPU before, 141 s and 114 s after (its longest file is now `edits.test.php`, 134 s); the mutation list 240 s before, 102 s after (its longest run 27 s, from 180 s). The suite makes 2,418 accounts with `freshUser` in one run, about nine minutes of hashing before.
- **The interleaving seed runs sharded, its parent judging only its children's results.** Status: choice, built 2026-11-15. Chosen: the gates run the interleaving seed on as many processes as the machine has logical CPUs, at most 8 (`GATES_INTERLEAVE_SHARDS`), CI on 4, as the local and Google modes already ran on 4; the parent loads nothing of the product or the harness and reports from what its children wrote (how each was configured, what it judged by, every case it ran with a digest of its steps and outcomes), and gives no verdict when a shard dies, writes nothing, ran on another configuration or oracle, or leaves a case unrun or run twice. Why: interleaving was the slowest harness step (about 5 minutes here, 3 on CI) in one process, and its sharded path had crashed unnoticed in the parent since the report began reading the conflict-choice setting there, a class only a child loads; the Google mode once did not reach its shards at all, so a sharded result is trusted only where a failure is seen to fail through the shards. Else possible: keep it serial (no risk, no speed); shard with the parent loading the harness to report (the crash's cause: the parent's classes and the children's can disagree, and nothing would show it); shard the oracle probes too (about a minute, a list of pairs of runs compared with each other, so a different merge for little time: left serial, and `--shards` with them refused). Cost: each shard migrates its own database (a few seconds), and the full gate runs up to 8 more processes beside the other seeds and the mutation list, all waiting on one database server; the mutation runner's interleaving runs stay serial (`--cases 0` with a fixed case or two, which no shard count splits), so its `--jobs` is the only parallelism there and the two never multiply. Verified: seeds 7, 211 and 911, 300 cases and the 124 fixed cases each, serial against 2, 4 and 8 shards: the same cases with the same digests, the same fixed cases once, the same run counts by check and role, not-applicable reasons, step pairs, coverage, records and summary lines (so nothing a case draws or checks depends on what ran before it in the process); a mutated product file loaded through `--model` fails the same 114 checks serial and on 4 shards; each option shown to reach the children by a sharded result that changes with it; a shard made to die, to write nothing (`BC_INTERLEAVE_SHARD_FAULT`) and one killed mid-run each give no verdict, the shard named, exit 1; 3 cases on 8 shards run on 3. Wall time here, alone: about 300 seconds serial, 128 to 145 on 4 shards, 112 to 122 on 8.
- **The concurrency tests are ordered by a lock held, not by a snapshot's timing.** Status: correction, 2026-11-15. `concurrency.test.php`'s deadlock scenario failed on CI twice in a row on 1fca2206 (no deadlock counted; the undo went through and the other change was kept) and on no earlier commit. Each scenario's child takes its first lock, touches a marker the parent waits for, and holds its lock until it sees the parent waiting, then takes its second. It asked whether the parent's connection was in `LOCK WAIT` in `INNODB_TRX`, which the server answers from a snapshot it refreshes only when nobody has read it for 100 ms. The previous scenario's child had just read that snapshot showing the parent waiting on it, so a child started within 100 ms of that read saw the old wait, took its second lock and committed before the undo had begun: no deadlock. The 0.22 s each account took to make (a password hash) had kept the next child more than 100 ms away from that read; with one hash per process the gap closed. Measured with the three scenarios looped in one file: before the fix, 39 of 40 misses alone and 30 of 30 with the suite running at 4 jobs alongside, against 0 of 40 alone with the old per-account hash but 29 of 30 with that hash under the same load. So the old hash had hidden the race, not removed it. Chosen: the child goes on only when the parent waits on a lock the child's own open transaction holds (`waitsOnMe`, `server/tests/db/lib.php`: the lock waits that name the parent's transaction as requesting and the child's as blocking; MariaDB's `information_schema.INNODB_LOCK_WAITS`, MySQL 8's `performance_schema.data_lock_waits`). A snapshot taken before the child's transaction began cannot name it as the blocker, so a stale answer is late, never wrong. The parent waiting on the child's lock means it holds the account's row (Undo::write takes it first), so the child's second step, its journal row, needs what the parent holds: the deadlock is certain. The other two scenarios (a move committed while an undo waits, the undo's row order) used the same signal and passed either way, without proving the overlap they claim; they now wait for the same event. Else: a longer gap or a sleep before the child's first look, which is a timed wait. Verified: the loop, 40 alone and 100 with the database suite running beside it, all with a deadlock counted and every check passing; the suite. The MySQL branch of `waitsOnMe` is untested here (the suite runs on MariaDB, as CI does).
- **The database suite runs the edit harness's fixed cases, not its random ones.** Status: choice, built 2026-11-15, by the owner's choice. Measured first: `edits.test.php` took 97 s alone and 134 s under the suite, the suite's longest file. 57 s of that was 200 random cases of seed 7, 24 s the fixed cases and 16 s 40 random cases on a Google calendar (seed 11). There was no waste of the password-hash kind: the time is the harness's own work, about 490 statements a case. Seed 7's cases 1 to 200 are an exact subset of what CI and `tools/gates.sh full` run: the harness's local mode on seed 7, cases 1 to 1,000, with every fixed case (each case's random source is the seed and its number, so a case is the same in any run). Chosen: the database suite drops those 200 cases; `edits.test.php` runs the fixed cases and keeps its three judgements over them (no failure outside the known code paths, no plan-data check failing on a known path, every required category of the plan-data checks ran). Only the last needed a case: one category, `c-placement | to (generated)`, was reached only by drawn cases 71 and 74. Case 71 is now a fixed case (`a-whole-series-moved-to-other-weekdays-keeps-a-generated-skip-on-its-day`), saved with `--save-spec`, so the check stays whole. The Google block is a file of its own, `edits-google.test.php`, with the same assertions; seed 11 is a draw CI and the gates do not run. What changes for a worker: `quick` and plain `merge` run no random series-editing cases; the fixed cases still run, and `merge --since REF` runs one seed in each harness mode when the engine, the Google write path or the harness changed. Else: shard the drawn cases into files, which needs the category judgement merged across files; or weaken the category check to the fixed cases' own categories. Costs: no random local-mode cases before CI or `full`; one more fixed case in every full harness run. Verified: the labels of the two files against the old one's, 33 each, the only differences the count label ("every fixed case ran" for "200 drawn cases and every fixed case ran") and the six rule labels renamed with the scenario ("the fixed cases" for "a seeded slice and the fixed cases"); the harness's full local run of seed 7 with the new fixed case is clean; the whole mutation list trips every check (no mutation names `edits`, and the harness mutations run their own named cases); the suite passes. Times: `edits.test.php` 50.7 s and `edits-google.test.php` 29.9 s under the suite, which took 99 s wall, against 134 s and 141 s before (both suite runs on a machine busy with another session's gate). Alone, the Google block takes 15 s whether it runs first in a fresh file or after the fixed cases; a 44 s reading earlier was a gate's busiest phase.
- **A plan's hash never moves by itself.** Status: correction, 2026-11-15. A change for all events, or this and following, that set new coordinates (a place picked from the search) was refused with `series_plan_changed` whenever its commit came in a later second than its preview: the change the plan hashes is `columnPatch`'s output, and `Events::locationProvenance` stamps `location_placed_at` there from the clock. A person's confirmation always comes a second or more later, and the app's answer to the refusal, a new plan, carried a new second too. Since 1.5.0, when the plan's hash came in; the 1.5.6-era tree refuses the same probe. Found by a database test that failed once under load. The rule: a plan's hash covers what the plan read and what the request asks for, never a value the write itself stamps from the clock; the stamp is still written at commit, with the commit's time. Chosen form: the clock's stamps are named where they are made (`Events::CLOCK_STAMPED`, from which `locationProvenance` builds its stamp, so a stamp added there is in the list by construction) and `Model::hash` leaves them out of the change it hashes. Else: hash the request's own fields instead of `columnPatch`'s output. Not taken because `Model::plan` is also called with a derived change and no request (the unit tests of the model, the harness's Google plan), so the hash would have needed a second form for them. Inventory of the hash (`Model::hash`, compared by `SeriesPlans::checkedPlan`): the master and each edited row, every column as read (read by value); their tags, people and links (read); the change: the day acted on, where it landed, typed clocks, a rule, tags, people, a calendar (asked, or derived from what was asked and the rows read, with no clock), and the content columns (derived by `columnPatch`; its one clock input, `location_placed_at`, now out); the chosen options (asked); whether the calendar is Google's (read). `today` was already out. Neighbours, not changed here: the conflict choice compares six fields by value (`requestBase`, `overColumns`, `servedFields`: title, notes, place text, link, status, and the time as start, end, all-day and zone), none of them a stamp, so it obeys; undo's staleness leaves bookkeeping columns out (`Undo::BOOKKEEPING`, `updated_at` among them) but compares `location_placed_at` and `geocoded_at` with the place they belong to (`Undo::TOGETHER`), which is right: they change only with a write of the place, a person's or the lookup's; the events window's tag is the change cursor, which moves on a write, never by time; a half-done change's plan (Google pending) is checked again by the same hash. What still moves a plan's rows without the person changing the series: a write that touches a row's `updated_at` (a poll storing a change at Google, the place lookup filling a row's coordinates), each a real write of the series' rows, so a stale answer then is the rule working. `location_placed_at` is written by `locationProvenance` (an edit, a create, a copy), the place lookup (`GeocodeSweep`, `Events` at its own lookup) and cleared by a feed or Google sync changing a place (`Feeds`); `geocoded_at` by the lookup and cleared by a change of place text (`columnPatch`, `Feeds`). Read by: the served record, undo's TOGETHER, and the plan's hash through the rows as read. Verified: `server/tests/db/series-plan-clock.test.php` moves the clock a second between preview and commit through a seam made for it (`Time::$testOffsetSeconds`, 0 everywhere else, set only by tests): coordinates alone for all events and for this and following, a new place with an hour later, each on a calendar here and on Google's stand-in, each going through with the stamp of the commit's second; and a rename between preview and commit still refused as stale, the new plan then going through a second later. `planned()`, the tests' preview-then-commit helper, now commits a second after its preview, so every test that confirms a plan (`google-series-recovery-held`, `google-series-undo` and `undo` among them) does it as a person would. A mutation putting the stamp back into the hash fails all eight assertions of the new file; the retargeted `plan-hash-without-edited-rows` is still caught. The unit and database suites, the harness's recorded seed in local and Google modes and the oracle probes pass.
- **The stand-in takes events.import as far as Google was seen to.** Status: choice, 2026-11-16 (the move to Google had no test against the stand-in because it had no import route). Chosen: `FakeGoogle::import` makes an event under the iCalUID sent, with an id of the stand-in's own, and a second import of an iCalUID a live event holds updates that event and answers its id (the access probe's listread stage, L3 and L4, 2026-11-14); an import whose iCalUID only a deleted event holds, or one with no iCalUID, is refused with a status Google was never seen to give (501, 400), so a test that reaches it says so instead of passing on a guess. Else: no route, and the move stays untested against the stand-in; or a full model of import, which nothing seen supports. What it costs: none in the product. Verified: `server/tests/db/google-move-other-form.test.php` (a calendar moved to Google whose timed series holds an all-day day and whose all-day series holds a timed day: two imports, one PATCH per day with the held form's fields null, both days kept in their own form at Google and here after two polls; 23 assertions, passing on the current code), the record-app scenario `move-other-form` replays clean on the stand-in, and the probe, replay and access tests pass. Probe 12.11 asks Google what the refused cases do.

## Revisiting One

Changing a choice is a changed decision: change the rule where it lives, say so in the changelog, and update the entry with the new reading and the date, keeping the old one as "was". Repairing code that broke a recorded choice is a bug fix, and the entry's verification line is what changes. A defect's entry is closed when its repair lands, with the release named.
